- **Rhea Calder** — Y1 Class A. Drills, spear practice, field teams and friendly competition.

- **Marta** — Clothing, laundry, linen and discreet household maintenance.

- **Mirelle Thorne** — Damaged wards, curses and contractual scripts.

- **Lord Arvayne** — Celestine’s family history, debts and guardianship.

- **Pell** — Facilities submissions, stamped dockets and office procedure.

- **Master Gellert** — Ward-failure benches and diagnostic exercises.

- **Ilse Vael-Castellan** — Y1 Class A. Ward geometry, notebooks and field-team technical work.

- **Riven Morcant** — Y1. Arena topics, heavy sword practice and Vharos connections.

- **Dorothy Pellisar** — Y1. Rune geometry, constructs and research workshops.

- **Garen Mornstahl** — Y1. Smithy, armor, shield drills and practical sparring.

- **Vayne Kettwald** — Y1. Tracking, monsters, crossbows and Blackgorge topics.

- **Kujou Sara** — Y1. Archery, aerial scouting and field-command exercises.

- **Zima Varnek** — Y4. Siege math, winter engineering and senior military work.

- **Eisen Brasswake** — Y1. Smithy, runes, metalwork and axe practice.

- **Miyamoto Musashi** — Y3. Sword society, tournaments, demonstrations and food/travel encounters.

- **Senku Ishigami** — Y1. Workshops, experiments, siege mechanics and technical societies.

- **Dehya Emberfell** — Y1. Melee drills, Cindervale circles and protective group scenes.

- **Amiya Senval** — Y1 Saryuun diplomat-scholar. Foreign-student circles, mediation, charter law, negotiation and cooperative ward work.

- **Senshi Ironmantle** — Y2. Survival training, field cooking, shelters and expedition logistics.

- **Perrin Ashgate** — Y1 reserve. Asterfen reserve-roster scenes.

- **Halden Rusk** — Y4 cadet. Rescue drills and Asterfen casualty records.

- **Halle Rennick** — Y1 reserve. Asterfen reserve-roster scenes.

- **Corin Ashby** — Academy field-office clerk. Field-exercise paperwork, equipment issue, rosters, east-gate logistics and exercise follow-up.

- **Dario Vesk** — Y1 reserve. Asterfen reserve-roster scenes.

- **Nell Quarry** — Y1 reserve. Asterfen reserve-roster scenes.

- **Master Verane** — East-hall pattern theory, release-line lessons and instructional faults.

Other named locals: **Corvan Hess** (dead Vharos factor), **Marta Sedge** (Vharos nurse, distinct from household Marta), **Perla** (Vharos washing/lifting helper). Load only for relevant evidence or care scenes.

**Master Vell** — Minor; Sarthe & Vell, financial instruments, attested copies and the controlled prosthetics commission. **Dara Joe** consolidates the source’s Dara/Dara (Dara Joe) cards; currently Arc 0/P0 after captivity, formerly Arc 2/P2.

### Elaranth ward timber trade
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq861swnjcn","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605480,"nanoseconds":894000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684296,"nanoseconds":309000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
Updates:
- [T247 · October 738] Sylwem's answer: Veyrath wagons take Elaranth north-province ward timber, half at the low treaty price and half from resettlement-seized stands moved as "salvage allotment" under the imperial seal never signed over by her king — legal in the Empire, theft in Elaranth, covered by the treaty's silence.
<!-- nera-line: {"evidence":[{"order":557,"id":"msg_mv0yc6jmapx2ca","revision":0},{"order":558,"revision":0,"id":"msg_mv0ycpal0eyqdx"}],"at":1791552877396,"text":"Sylwem's answer: Veyrath wagons take Elaranth north-province ward timber, half at the low treaty price and half from resettlement-seized stands moved as \"salvage allotment\" under the imperial seal never signed over by her king — legal in the Empire, theft in Elaranth, covered by the treaty's silence.","src":558,"when":"October 738","by":"auto","id":"ln_mvjpvwq8nyd9lizf","turn":247,"sourceRevision":157} -->

### Crownspire Year-One Excursion Itinerary
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8a814gnat","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":857000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684297,"nanoseconds":80000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Narrative status:** **Vharos has already occurred; Miravel and Tideglass remain future opportunities.** They occur if the narrator advances time and the Academy actually organizes travel; they are invitations and scenario frameworks, not automatic disasters or forced choices. Crownspire charter and protected-student rules remain applicable during supervised educational travel.
**1. Vharos — City of Chains and Arenas:** **Departed 28/10/738; scheduled road return 01/11; actual Crownspire roll closed 03/11.** Students travel to the enormous Grand Black Amphitheater and surrounding weapons schools, monster pens, noble galleries, gladiator demonstrations, courts and slave markets. Themes: martial technique versus spectacle, laws of punishment, slave economy, elite reputations, competing moral viewpoints and potentially dangerous exhibitions. Do **not** assume an individual student is forced to fight or that any named gladiator is already dead.
**2. Miravel — City of the Nine Mage Towers:** **Late winter–early spring, 4–6 days.** Nine rival magical towers, skybridges, libraries, enchanted markets, aether-device trials, research contests and restricted laboratories create academic rivals, alliances and possible secrets. Visiting instructors and tower licenses matter; experiments can be investigated without automatically causing a citywide catastrophe.
**3. Crownward Leviathan — Tideglass Island Voyage:** **Spring, 1–2 weeks including sea travel.** A huge rune-reinforced passenger vessel with cabins, lounges, medical decks, banquets, student groups and shipboard practice hosts navigation lessons. At the Tideglass Isles: island festivals, lighthouse ruins, archaeology, coastal ecology, commerce, local fishermen, island courts and optional piracy/smuggling investigation. Storms, misnavigation, criminals and sea monsters are potential complications, **not predetermined events**. Crew, students and shore communities retain agency. All dates are year-one windows rather than retroactive edits to the October 738 present.

Earlier source context — Crownspire Year-One Excursion Itinerary (retain as historical context wherever later dated records supersede it):

**Narrative status:** As of **October 738, the first-year Academy excursions listed here are future curriculum/event anchors, not already played scenes**. They occur if the narrator advances time and the Academy actually organizes travel; they are invitations and scenario frameworks, not automatic disasters or forced choices. Crownspire charter and protected-student rules remain applicable during supervised educational travel.
**1. Vharos — City of Chains and Arenas:** **Late autumn, 3–4 days.** Students travel to the enormous Grand Black Amphitheater and surrounding weapons schools, monster pens, noble galleries, gladiator demonstrations, courts and slave markets. Themes: martial technique versus spectacle, laws of punishment, slave economy, elite reputations, competing moral viewpoints and potentially dangerous exhibitions. Do **not** assume an individual student is forced to fight or that any named gladiator is already dead.
**2. Miravel — City of the Nine Mage Towers:** **Late winter–early spring, 4–6 days.** Nine rival magical towers, skybridges, libraries, enchanted markets, aether-device trials, research contests and restricted laboratories create academic rivals, alliances and possible secrets. Visiting instructors and tower licenses matter; experiments can be investigated without automatically causing a citywide catastrophe.
**3. Crownward Leviathan — Tideglass Island Voyage:** **Spring, 1–2 weeks including sea travel.** A huge rune-reinforced passenger vessel with cabins, lounges, medical decks, banquets, student groups and shipboard practice hosts navigation lessons. At the Tideglass Isles: island festivals, lighthouse ruins, archaeology, coastal ecology, commerce, local fishermen, island courts and optional piracy/smuggling investigation. Storms, misnavigation, criminals and sea monsters are potential complications, **not predetermined events**. Crew, students and shore communities retain agency. All dates are year-one windows rather than retroactive edits to the October 738 present.

### Character importance & power scale
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8cmolk720","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792131601,"nanoseconds":343000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684296,"nanoseconds":705000000}} -->
always load: yes

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Narrative importance controls memory/detail, not combat rank.**

- **Core:** central long-term characters. Preserve identity, motives, relationships, signature powers, weaknesses and growth potential. Core characters should be unusually strong or possess exceptional long-term potential.
- **Major:** recurring plot drivers with meaningful goals and choices. Keep the richest practical card detail because their individual cards may not always be loaded.
- **Minor:** named supporting characters. Keep a compact identity, role, distinctive trait and combat niche.
- **Background:** world-populating characters. Keep only the minimum needed for consistent recognition and portrayal.

Read **Aether and Arcs**, **Physical Capability Scale**, and **Combat Experience & Matchups** together. Strong magic usually trades against raw physical capability; strong physical specialists normally have narrower or lower-Arc magic unless explicit lore says otherwise. **Nera is the clearest exception: Arc 4 / P6 in Y1.** Potential is future ceiling, never current output. Never power-scale from narrative importance alone.

### Aurelian Empire
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8jzecicn0","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605476,"nanoseconds":537000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684297,"nanoseconds":453000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
The hereditary sovereign governs with ministers, the Crown Council, provincial governors, and major houses.
The Crown commands the continent's largest combined-arms military establishment: mass infantry, armored knights, horse and monster cavalry, archers, battle-mages, defensive warders, and destructive long-range aether artillery. Its main advantages are numbers, diversity of capabilities, strategic reserves, and the capacity to fight several fronts at once.
The Empire also draws enormous forced levies from enslavement, war captivity, penal servitude, and debt bondage. Commanders commonly drive disposable captives ahead of regular troops as screens, assault waves, and expendable labor; this is a brutal feature of the regime, not a sign those captives are loyal soldiers.
The Crown depends on nobles, suppliers, lenders, transport, and provincial troops; particularly House Veyrath's iron, armor, roads, and military supply network. Political power comes from institutions and material dependencies as well as magic. Great nobles remain difficult but not absolutely impossible to prosecute.
See **Imperial Military — Legions and Combined Arms** for battlefield structure and strengths.
The Crown further operates royal griffon riders, provincial wyvern outriders, skywatch sentries, beast-drawn chariots and conventional black-powder bombards; all use pre-modern mechanisms. It fields numerous aerial units, but cannot mass-produce true dragons and does not possess modern guns, aircraft, or engines. See **Imperial Air Corps and War Machines**.

**Corrupt sovereign order:** The Emperor and Prince Cedric knowingly sustain and benefit from caste privilege, bought courts, coerced labor and the slave economy. Nobles, royalty and great wealth hold effective power; legal remedies depend heavily on patronage and rank. Capable or honest individuals can exist, but an isolated fair official does not make the regime secretly just. The precise perpetrator of a particular crime still requires evidence.

### Black Lantern
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8rf7iwr2w","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605478,"nanoseconds":700000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684297,"nanoseconds":80000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
Black Lantern is an independent trafficking and assassin-training network serving multiple buyers.
Destroying or reforming House Veyrath would not automatically destroy Black Lantern.
Its survivors differ in training and condition; Isolde is not representative of every victim.

### Aerial warfare and defenses
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8rh3reqq5","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":88000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684297,"nanoseconds":836000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Classification:** Great birds (hawks, eagles and sea-rocs) are fast fragile mounts for archery, scouting and messages; **wyverns** are lesser two-legged flying reptiles with wings for forelimbs, much smaller, less intelligent and less magically potent than true four-legged **winged dragons**. Veyrath's **Blackscale War Drakes** are heavy **wingless ground mounts**, not either flyer. Griffons and hippogriff-like beasts are separate aerial shock predators with limited rider capacity. Dragons remain exceedingly rare strategic assets; House Veyrath's total is exactly **30** Skythorn dragon pairs in 738. Bird riders cannot hover in place forever and their missile payload is limited; wyverns tire and can be dragged down by trained archers; true dragons need huge meat reserves and specialized aeries.
**Air-unit tasks:** Raptors shoot while wheeling and hunt skirmishers; wyverns strike supply parks, seize rooftops and intercept enemy scouts; armored gryphons protect important commanders; a handful of true dragons can smash exposed siege towers or deliver superior Fourth–Fifth Arc mages into decisive positions. Air supremacy is temporary, local and weather-dependent.
**Anti-air defense:** Multi-shot torsion ballistae and rotating sky-scorpions launch giant bolts; net/chain mortars or great bows foul wings; rune-grounding towers and counterspell mages disrupt aerial wards; cliffside archers form upward-facing volleys; mobile gryphon/wyvern patrols intercept attackers. Wind, fog, ceilings, lightning, air currents, physical ammunition, exhausted wards, range and friendly-fire restrictions matter. Anti-magic wards can reduce spell damage **but cannot make the physical impact of a giant bolt disappear**.
**Global balance:** Elaranth leads mobile flying archery and forest ambush; the Empire dominates massed mixed aerial scouts and layered defenses; Varkesh favors rugged wyverns and cold-weather fliers; Solenne excels in sanctified aerial escort; Orsenn specializes in sea-roc coastal watch; Kharad has little cavalry aviation but exceptionally strong counter-air engineering; Cindervale uses heat-adapted volcanic wyverns; Veyrath fields small numbers of heavily armored flyers and the renowned Thirty Skythorn true-dragon riders.

### Calendar
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8svrojdn4","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605476,"nanoseconds":537000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684297,"nanoseconds":453000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
The opening is 18 September, Imperial Year 731.
Nera is eleven at the opening.
Nera's Crownspire intake begins in autumn 738 when he is eighteen.
The standard four Academy years conclude in summer 742.
**Age and cohort indexing (October IY738):** Nera is eighteen and a first-year, so any character described as the same age is eighteen in IY738. "N years older" means age 18+N in October 738; "N years younger" means 18−N. "Turns eighteen in IYXXX" is the explicit annual age anchor; birthdays within a calendar year are not otherwise pinned. Ordinary first-years in 738 are eighteen, second-years are generally nineteen, third-years generally twenty and fourth-years generally twenty-one, but individual enrollment histories can differ (e.g. Senshi is a twenty-year-old second-year). New explicit age anchors for previously undated adults are continuity assignments, not retroactive encounters. A deceased character has no present living age. **A student already on Crownspire's roll has not necessarily met Nera.** The distinct first-year 739 cohort (Taigar, Fiammetta and Sanhua) is not attending Crownspire in October 738; 736 graduates such as Tanya are no longer students.
**Specific upper-year anchors:** Lucien and Prince Cedric are fourth-years, Magerrett is second-year, Katarina is an enrolled third-year, and the new Irelia Senka is an enrolled third-year who turned eighteen in IY736. All established Class A 738 entrance ranks remain unchanged.

**Narration:** use DD/MM/YYYY and the modern 24-hour clock. Repeated T401/T443 directives supersede bell-based narration; preserve bells only inside historical records and physical landmarks. Autumn 738 ages/cohorts remain the baseline and advance with time.

### Counting-house warrants and signatures
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8xvvoznuq","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792500521,"nanoseconds":386000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684297,"nanoseconds":836000000}} -->
aliases: ["Counting-house practice"]

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Counting houses audit signatures rather than purpose (T653, 04/11/738). A warrant under a lodged countersignature is lawful use, not theft; magistrates can act on a false warrant or an office exceeding its grant (T655). An unknown purpose does not establish embezzlement. Military Blackwing money and household/private accounts remain distinct.

### Elaranth Greenhost
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8y1wtgemj","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":286000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684297,"nanoseconds":836000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Military character:** Forest-domain elites with fewer soldiers than the Empire and far less interest in static siege. **Silverbranch Wardens** (longbows, moving camouflaged skirmish), **Moonblade Guard** (spear/glaive foot), **Stagbound Lancers** (deer/elk forest cavalry), **Galequill Arrowriders** (giant hawk and eagle-mounted archers), **Eagle-Crown Knights** (heavier great-eagle interceptors), **Living Bough Sentinels** (ancient tree guardians and entangling ward roots), **Ithilwood Chariots** (light war chariots on open forest roads), **Wardwood Bolt Arrays** (mobile repeating enchanted bolts), and Grove-Seer healers/illusionists. Elite archers can fire while banking through gaps in foliage; few others match them in aerial skirmishing. They lack major blackpowder manufacture, heavy steel cannon, or large conventional armies.
**Strategic geography:** Elaranth guards Aelthalas, Ravensong Canopy, Moonwake, Silverleaf Borderwoods and the Greenveil River. Its roots, forest paths and disguised towers frustrate invaders; open plains and sustained artillery punish its lightly armored troops. The ward-timber treaty remains unequal; Sylwem's status is a political leverage point, not permission to erase her agency.

### Crownspire Citadel defenses
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8yoyijjpt","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605477,"nanoseconds":800000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684296,"nanoseconds":309000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Academy as a fortress:** Crownspire Imperial Academy is a **self-contained gothic military-academic citadel on a raised plateau**, guarded first by Vaelcross's upper-city rings and then by its own defenses: sheer stone faces, three staggered bastioned wall lines around the most exposed approaches, massive iron-bound gates, elevated sentry towers, rune-etched reliefs, great halls with vaulted dark stone ceilings, giant banners and lantern-lit cloisters. The parade and duel grounds are enclosed by regulated containment wards so normal Academy exercises do not damage the surrounding city. Rooftop ballista platforms and spell-countermeasure pylons guard against hostile flying beasts; underground cisterns, medicinal stores, ward batteries and sealed lower corridors can sustain a siege.
**Academy autonomy:** The rector, marshals, trained wardens and specialist ward-mages operate the *inner campus defenses*. Imperial guards patrol outer capital approaches, but cannot casually seize, imprison or remove enrolled students on Academy grounds; charter rules, posted hearings and protected-student clauses remain in force. Routine student movement between dormitories, workshops, lecture halls, tournaments, clubs, visiting festivals and supervised excursions remains possible using permitted passes and guarded gates. The gates are secure, **not** permanently locked like a prison; academy examinations still happen in functioning, accessible arenas.
**Magic and tactical limits:** The campus's shields stop stray projectiles, ordinary intrusion spells and routine aerial raids, while watchmen and faculty can isolate individual hall or workshop incidents. Aether stores, anchors, cliff foundations and staffing limit defensive uptime; coordinated Fifth Arc spells, major siege artillery, sabotage, infiltration, dragons or Nera's extraordinary unsuppressed power can overwhelm or bypass parts of its protection. These safeguards are not plot devices that automatically negate Nera's known abilities or override the Academy charter. The visual atmosphere should be awe-inspiring, threatening, scholarly and lived-in rather than a permanently mobilized battlefield.
The elevated campus maintains bolt-caster terraces, warded great-bird roost observation towers and defensive storm-net pylons; it does not keep a permanent army of battle dragons. Visiting student excursions leave through documented gates and may use a guarded imperial port or chartered ship without abolishing protected-student rights.

### Black Concordance
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8ys9ximiv","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605478,"nanoseconds":260000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684297,"nanoseconds":453000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
Cassian's hidden Black Concordance links military ward relays with coerced service oaths to centralize command.
It requires equipment, registered subjects, authorization, and an emergency that can justify its use.
Removing components can permanently reduce its capability.
Cassian is willing to engineer political conditions favorable to it, but he does not secretly control every foreign actor.

### Combat Experience & Matchups
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq8zfvmoy01","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792131601,"nanoseconds":94000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684297,"nanoseconds":80000000}} -->
always load: yes

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Arc and Physical are capability scales, not automatic win scores. Fighting experience is a separate determinant of combat results.** Resolve fights from the whole matchup rather than adding two numbers.

Experience includes live-combat exposure, years of drilling, quality of opponents, weapon familiarity, ability to read feints, pain and fear tolerance, tactical judgment, battlefield awareness, teamwork and experience fighting the opponent's specific style. Academy sparring is useful experience but is not equivalent to years of lethal field combat.

A smaller Arc or Physical disadvantage can be overcome by superior experience, preparation, terrain, counters, surprise, reach, equipment, teamwork or a favorable specialization. A veteran may defeat a more powerful but inexperienced prodigy if the veteran can survive the raw power long enough to exploit mistakes. Conversely, experience does not magically erase overwhelming gaps: a fighter who cannot perceive, endure, reach or meaningfully damage the opponent still loses without a specific counter or circumstance.

**Fight-resolution guidance:**
- A **one-grade gap** is meaningful but often contestable through skill, experience or matchup.
- A **two-grade gap** is a major advantage and normally requires a strong counter, preparation, teamwork or situational edge to overturn.
- A **three-or-more-grade gap** should dominate direct contests unless established lore provides an exceptional counter, severe restraint, surprise, injury or mission constraint.
- Specialized powers can bypass raw grades: wards may stop stronger bodies, anti-magic may trouble stronger casters, ranged control may punish melee specialists, and assassins may win before a fair duel begins.
- Real combat objectives are not always "defeat the opponent." Rescue, delay, escape, protection, secrecy and collateral limits can make a weaker combatant succeed.

Do not invent veteran experience merely because a character is important. Use established history. When experience is unspecified, assume only what the character's age, schooling, profession and recorded scenes support.

### Ancient Glyph Magic
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq91g7511j0","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605476,"nanoseconds":537000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684299,"nanoseconds":3000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
Nera can perceive meaning in an ancient family of written magical strokes associated with Ilyra's lineage.
Aldh is a discharge glyph that releases raw aether without converting it into an element.
The script can convey concepts in ways Nera experiences almost like sound or hierarchical resonance.
Vane believes Ilyra may have been the last known practitioner of this line before Nera.
Nera's complete aptitude is not public knowledge.

### Imperial Air Corps and War Machines
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq964ejke6q","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":286000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684301,"nanoseconds":322000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Royal Skyward Cohorts:** Imperial gryphon lancers are armored flying shock scouts and interceptors; numerous lesser-wyvern wings escort siege trains and raid exposed rear units; hawk/eagle messengers and observing mages coordinate artillery. Gryphons remain rare enough to require trained breeding houses. Vaelcross's three-tier ring defenses combine signal beacons, anti-dragon scorpions, field ward pylons and quickly dispatched interceptors.
**Wheelguard and Grand Battery:** Bronze-wheel horse chariots protect flank artillery; monster-hauled siege wagons carry rune scorpions; matchlock handgonners and heavy crossbows suppress approach lanes. Grand Battery uses long-range stone-throwers, bronze field cannon, great bombards, siege mortars, counterweight trebuchets and expensive aether lances. Other provinces retain distinctive beasts; imported Kharad engineering and Veyrath ore make the Empire's scale possible. Crew totals are inside existing regular/provincial armies.
**Doctrinal weakness:** Imperial air can cover multiple fronts but coordination, rival nobles, captive labor, powder supply and slow engines degrade reliability. Its strongest flyers are outmatched individually by a Veyrath true dragon; numbers and air-defense networks are the equalizer.

### Solenne Sacred Hosts
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq97ihfoe8u","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":474000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684300,"nanoseconds":919000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Military character:** The southern kingdom's Church and crown divide command among **Flameward Templars** (heavily plated sword/spear religious orders), **Pilgrim Shield Companies**, **Reliquary Bow and Arbalest Guard**, **Lionheart War Chariots** (heavily decorated lion-drawn shock vehicles), **Gryphon Luminous Guard** (flying knight escorts), **Dawnwing White-Roc Riders** (aerial support and courier archers), **Consecrated Flame Mages / healer-priests**, and **Sun-Bell Counterspell Towers / Blessed Trebuchets**. Enchanted shields can break curses and protect wounded formations but consume relic charges and trained clergy.
**Strategy and limits:** Strong morale, hospital networks, purification wards and defended pilgrimage passes; rival Church orders and royal officers can make the chain of command slow or legalistic. Solenne is not uniformly benevolent: charity exists beside contracts and adjudications that can sustain bonded service. Soleris and Whitewatch dominate its routes.

### Cindervale Frontier Hosts
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq97vhaudmf","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":668000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684298,"nanoseconds":620000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Status:** Cindervale is a politically semi-autonomous southern **march of the Aurelian Empire**, not a new independent kingdom. It neighbors the independent **Umbrath Covenant** beyond the Glassfire Fault, but its demonkin communities retain separate citizenship, civic identities and loyalties. **Ashshield Garrisoners** (heat-treated infantry), **Cinderkin Polearm Cohorts**, **Salamander Chargers** (mounts adapted to ash and heat), **Ash-Wyvern Scree Riders** (fast but fragile volcanic fliers), **Cinder Hook Skirmishers** (throwing spears and shortbows), **Obsidian-wheel Chariots**, **Fireglass Boltcasters**, and **Steamless Ember Mortars** (alchemical short-range siege pots, not modern artillery). Demonkin communities provide some specialist ward lore and monster-handlers but do not all swear to one marshal.
**Strategy and limits:** Lava gullies, choking ash and old magical seals protect Emberwatch and Ashbridge; intense heat limits other nations' remounts. The region is politically divided between imperial governors, local councils, and demonkin enclaves. It should not be flattened into a villain faction.

### Continental Atlas — Extended Nations and Strategic Borders
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9c1tshw6a","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605482,"nanoseconds":43000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684298,"nanoseconds":229000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Relative compass; map is approximate, not fixed surveyed coordinates:** Vaelcross and the Aurelian heartland occupy the middle three-river basin. Elaranth's forest kingdom lies west, and the Western Glass Sea beyond imperial Gullhaven leads toward Tideglass and Orsenn shipping. Orsenn's seven independent ports are north along cold estuaries. The dwarven Kharad Holds guard the northeastern Crownfang passes. Veyrath's eastern marches stand between the Empire and Varkesh, with Blackgorge leading into the Redsteppe. **Saryuun Concord**, an independent beastkin state, extends farther east and southeast beyond Varkesh over the Namar grasslands to the Dawnwater Gulf; merchants can approach via Varkesh trade roads, not through Veyrath territory by magic. Solenne's holy kingdom lies south across the Sablewater; imperial Cindervale sits in the volcanic southern frontier belt. **Umbrath Covenant**, an independent demonkin country, lies farther beyond Cindervale across the Glassfire Fault, with the Nhal-Soryn caldera inland. The Varkesh–Saryuun and Cindervale–Umbrath relations are negotiable borders rather than settled permanent wars.
**Signature places by external nation:** Elaranth — Aelthalas and Ravensong Canopy; Varkesh — Kharzun and Urzak Gate; Solenne — Soleris and Whitewatch; Orsenn — Nacre Quay and Saltspire; Kharad — Kar-Durim and Anvildeep; Cindervale imperial march — Ashbridge and Emberwatch; Saryuun — Luneshar and Skyrake Eyries; Umbrath — Nhal-Soryn and Gravefire Gate. Smaller pre-existing borderwoods, regional harbors, mountain ranges and trade corridors remain real but are **supporting geography**, not additional world capitals.
**Narrative boundaries:** Multiple peoples reside across national borders, so no beastkin character is automatically a citizen of Saryuun and no demonkin character automatically obeys Umbrath. **Vesper Nox's secret eastern royal identity (Lan Hsin) and magically hidden nine tails remain unchanged and must not be inferred from the invention of Saryuun; only Nera knows about the nine tails.** Her ancestry and private history are not automatically assigned to the new country. Foreign rulers, wars, coups and Academy trips are possible future plots, not retroactively played history. The planned first-year Vharos, Miravel and Tideglass excursions remain future anchors.

### Registry of Instruments

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->

### Crownspire enrollment and charter
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9cn5ud0vz","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605478,"nanoseconds":260000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684298,"nanoseconds":229000000}} -->
aliases: ["First-year billets","Class A top ten","Crownspire drill","Crownspire first-year yard","Crownspire 738 Full-Term Supporting Cast — Enrollment Boundaries"]

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Each annual intake has 240 students divided into Classes A through F.
Placement considers combat, magical control, theory, judgment, and professional specialty.
Class A receives stronger equipment access and prestigious opportunities.
Class F is remedial but students can advance.
The Academy charter provides limited protection from noble authority and permits some bonded students under special clauses.
Performance rankings reward efficient, controlled application rather than raw power alone.







**New continuing Academy cast, October 738:** Irelia Senka, Riven Morcant, Dorothy Pellisar, Malfoid Valecourt, Garen Mornstahl, Vayne Kettwald, Kujou Sara, Artoria Pendragon, Skadi Morozh, Zima Varnek, Eisen Brasswake, Jeanne d’Arc, Miyamoto Musashi, Ningguang, Senku Ishigami, Dehya Emberfell, Amiya Senval, Yinlin Veysh, Changli Solvair, Mordred Ashborne, Lappland Velsyn, Senshi Ironmantle, Fran Kurofen and Okita Souji are either first-years or upper-years as individually specified. Their foreign enrollment is **for the complete four-year Academy program**, not brief visiting exchanges. New first-years are about 1–2 weeks into their first term and are **already formally enrolled**; being on the roll does not mean Nera has already met them. Musashi and Irelia are third-years; Artoria is a first-year; Zima is a fourth-year; Changli and Senshi are second-years. **Fiammetta Kaorun and Sanhua Orren belong to the incoming 739 first-year cohort** and are not yet present at Crownspire in October 738. **Tanya Rauth is a 736 graduate serving Nera’s banner**, not a current student. No new entry changes the established 738 numbered examination rankings, top-ten placements, or current group membership. The characters have lives and ambitions independent of Nera; do not auto-create friendships, romances, victories, rivalries or dialogue as if they occurred off-screen. Fran and Teacher are prominent recurring figures but have not yet met Nera on screen.
**Casting convention:** Prefer approximately five female characters per male among future new recruits; dwarf characters are male. The above cast is mostly female. Preserve existing characters’ established genders. Liora remains the only Surtr-inspired character, while only Vesper uses the secret identity Lan Hsin and only Nera knows she has nine tails. All franchise cues are appearance/temperament references, **not** imported powers, histories, or literal crossover identities.

### Orsenn War Confederacies
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9fxlxpoed","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":474000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684300,"nanoseconds":536000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Military character:** Seven independent commercial ports rent and maintain **Blackwake Marines** (armored deck infantry), **Crossbow Privateers**, **Gullwing Arrowriders** (sea-bird aerial scouts), **Harbor Roc Lancers** (heavy but rare coastal fliers), **Saltwind Horse Archers** (beachland cavalry), **Ironhook Boarding Chariots** (compact harbor-road war carts), **Chain-Gate Ballista Galleys**, **Bronze Swivel Gun Crews** and **Shore Bombards**. Individual cities may field mage pilots, aether fog-makers and private corsairs; their fleets are not a single obedient royal navy.
**Strategy and limits:** Excellent port warfare, smuggling surveillance, amphibious raiding and rapid paid reinforcement, but weak at prolonged interior conquest. Nacre Quay, Saltspire and the Seven Harbors can arm either imperial clients or their opponents. Orsenn's business with trafficking is mixed and contested, never universally endorsed.

### Geography, Borders, and Trade Routes
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9i1t9p7fn","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":668000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684299,"nanoseconds":768000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Known continental compass (relative, not a mathematically precise map):** The Aurelian heartland and **Vaelcross** occupy the central river basin. **Elaranth** lies **west** beyond Silverleaf Borderwoods and the Greenveil. The **Orsenn Free Cities** lie **north** on cold coastal estuaries. **Kharad Holds** occupy **northeast** mountain passageways. **Varkesh Dominion** stands **east**, beyond Veyrath's militarized eastern marches and the Redsteppe. **Solenne** is **south**, across Sablewater and the pilgrimage highways. **Cindervale** is an internally diverse southern imperial march toward volcanic ground. The **Western Glass Sea**, **Gullhaven** and **Tideglass Isles** link sea travel with Elaranth and Orsenn trade.
**Imperial route network:** Vaelcross -> eastern forge and grain highways -> Thornhold / Forgevault / Blackgorge -> Varkesh; Vaelcross -> west timber crossings -> Elaranth; Vaelcross -> Vharos -> Solenne crossing; Vaelcross -> Miravel mage crossroads; Vaelcross -> northern caravan/river routes -> Orsenn / Kharad passes; Vaelcross -> Gullhaven -> Crownward Leviathan's offshore route -> Tideglass Isles.
**Further east/south:** Independent beastkin **Saryuun Concord** lies beyond Varkesh toward the Dawnwater Gulf; independent demonkin **Umbrath Covenant** lies beyond the imperial Cindervale Marches and Glassfire Fault. Neither replaces the older nations or makes the imperial Cindervale march sovereign. See **Continental Atlas — Extended Nations and Strategic Borders**.
**War-planning constraints:** Mountain passes are seasonal; rivers need guarded bridges and ferries; fog and sea shoals hinder shipping; wyverns, great birds and dragon riders can scout ahead but cannot carry a national army's winter fodder, black powder or granary supplies. Every country has plausible reasons to compete, trade, negotiate and sometimes cooperate; a war is a potential arc, never an automatically completed event.

### Ilyra Veyrath trust
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9krqdfa3a","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792500523,"nanoseconds":304000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684299,"nanoseconds":384000000}} -->
aliases: ["Ilyra Veyrath Trust trustees"]

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Ilyra personally lodged the deed on 09/03/IY733 before a commissioner. Public beneficiaries: Nera, child of her body, and any later children of her body. Operative clauses remain enrolled under seal (T664, 04/11/738). Trustees: Dame Verrine Sable of Vaelcross; Master Otho Talgarth of Talgarth and Rye; and the office of keeper of the trust, not a third named person. Verrine lives near the sixth-ring Ashgate stair; a named trustee must hear that the heir requested an audience but may refuse to open the door (T665). The deposit book’s first entry is in Ilyra’s handwriting; Isolde copied it line for line into the day-book on 04/11/738 (T678). Existence and copied deposits do not reveal every sealed term or prove unrestricted access.

### Physical Capability Scale
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9kznetn97","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792131601,"nanoseconds":94000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684300,"nanoseconds":919000000}} -->
always load: yes

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Physical grade (P0–P6)** measures the character's body without temporary magical amplification: strength, speed, durability, stamina, coordination and natural combat reactions. It does **not** measure spell power, tactical judgment or accumulated fighting experience.

- **P0 — Noncombatant:** ordinary body with negligible combat conditioning.
- **P1 — Basic:** healthy or lightly trained; can function in danger but is not a serious fighter.
- **P2 — Trained:** regular martial conditioning; competent soldier, guard or ordinary Academy combat student.
- **P3 — Elite:** exceptional trained body, speed and reactions for a student or professional fighter.
- **P4 — Superhuman:** clearly beyond normal human physical limits; can dominate ordinary trained fighters physically.
- **P5 — Master / monster-class:** extreme body and conditioning associated with veteran champions, elite assassins or exceptional martial masters.
- **P6 — Catastrophic / legendary:** extraordinarily rare physical force, speed or durability capable of overwhelming conventional fighters and breaking normal battlefield assumptions. **Nera is P6.**

Temporary aether reinforcement can raise effective performance for a scene but costs aether and does not permanently change the character's listed Physical grade. Weapon quality, armor, reach and injuries also matter. A physically weaker mage should not trade blows with a stronger martial specialist merely because her Arc is higher.

### Saryuun Skyclans and Field Hosts
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9lirefxlg","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605482,"nanoseconds":43000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684300,"nanoseconds":145000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Military doctrine — Beastkin Concord:** Saryuun fields fewer massed troops and less siege iron than the Empire, but excels at scouts, ambush, terrain mastery, mobile archery and combined beast cavalry. **Moonshield River Guard** hold cities with layered spear-and-shield walls and ward-silk shields; **Seven-Court Foxbow Scouts** are mixed-ancestry reconnaissance archers skilled in diplomacy and deception; **Wolfroad Rangers** are organized patrol companies trained to cross wooded ravines; **Thunderhoof Elk Lancers** and **Tuskback Boar Cataphracts** are shock riders for broken grassland; **Cloudquill Bird Archers** on great hawks or cranes excel in aerial skirmishing; **Galecrest Roc Guard** are scarce heavier interceptors; and **Stormsilk Wardweavers** provide illusion-breaks, healing and counter-air weather readings.
**Engines and limits:** Portable bamboo-and-steel bolt throwers, siege bows, folding shield wagons, wooden chariots and rare imported matchlocks supplement these troops; the Concord has no Veyrath-style endless heavy foundry system and no standing flight of true dragons. Skycliff cities favor light armor for flying archers; even legendary rocs must avoid massed ballista fire. Regional militias answer to charter courts and can refuse another court's expansionist campaign. Beastkin ancestry is not a single political loyalty, magical power level or military role.

### Dreadlance Cataphracts
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9n5w96nd7","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605477,"nanoseconds":384000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684299,"nanoseconds":3000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Approximately **150–300 active riders** in the standing warhost, drawn from the most powerful cavalry veterans; for a representative full-strength muster of **240**, the best-known arrangement is **120 Dreadmane Destriers, 80 Ironhorn Behemoths, and 40 Blackscale War Drakes** (the actual mix varies with remounts and campaigning). These are superheavy **ground cavalry**, distinct from the 30 flying true-dragon knights of the Skythorn Dracoguard. The Dreadlances are the cavalry counterpart to the Thorn Paladins: exceptionally strong knights wearing sealed rune-reinforced, fully articulated plate and carrying enormous lances, great sabres, shields, or warhammers. Each rider and mount looks like one moving iron citadel.
**Dreadmane Destrier:** A huge, muscular black warhorse bred through generations for exceptional endurance and aether-reinforced strength; approximately 2.2–2.5 meters at the shoulder. Full armor protects head, neck, shoulders, chest, flanks, and legs; the mount remains fast enough for a devastating pursuit or flanking charge. **Ironhorn Behemoth:** An exceptionally massive, usually six-legged horned armored charger, about 2.8–3.2 meters at the shoulder and several tonnes in weight, bred for aether-enhanced impact and demolition of shield formations, gates, and barricades. Slower to turn and vulnerable to unstable terrain. **Blackscale War Drake:** A powerful **wingless** draconic land beast with four massive legs, natural black scales, tremendous bite and claws, and fitted runic plate; it excels in rocky approaches, prolonged close assaults, fortification fighting, and attacks on other monsters. It does not fly and is not a true dragon.
Dreadlances attack in compact, disciplined wedges over routes prepared by engineers and warders. Their momentum can burst conventional infantry and cavalry lines and rip light fieldworks apart, but their mounts remain living creatures with vulnerable stamina, joints, footing, and temperament. Their combined ward fields withstand arrows and lesser spells without making them immune to artillery, precision magic, anti-charge obstacles, marshland, hunger, or determined combined arms. The ordinary Blacksteel Lancers and March Beast Riders also wear heavy armor, but lack this elite corps' rare strength, bespoke equipment, and extremely expensive breeding stock. Cataphract cadres are permanently divided among all five heirs and the duke's host; **Nera has 24 Dreadlance riders and mounts in his own banner**, within the overall representative 240.

### Noble conduct and Katarina’s society
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9puf4phtm","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792500524,"nanoseconds":151000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684301,"nanoseconds":708000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Ordinary aristocratic life:** In this Empire, elite indulgence, coerced sexual access to adult dependants, slave exchange, household domination and contempt for common suffering are familiar privileges. Powerful families commonly treat scandal as a breach of discretion or property rights rather than an injury to an equal. People reason through bloodline, rank, ownership, oath, faith, patronage and family obligation; avoid inserting a uniform modern moral vocabulary. Individual dissent has specific personal or cultural grounds.

**Katarina’s inner circle:** An exclusive society of adult heirs and daughters converts shared atrocities into fellowship, mutual exposure and political debt. Ordinary exploitative nobles need not be initiated members. The circle organizes raids on settlements to obtain captives, sacrificial rites, coerced adult sexual violence, deliberate destruction of family bonds, abusive medical experimentation and induced illness, blood bathing, cannibalistic rites and the keeping of living victims as collections. Rare forbidden bodily preservation or transformations require established means and costs; not every horror has a genuine supernatural benefit.

**Treatment:** Establish these acts through restrained aftermath, testimony, euphemistic household routines, medical findings, inventories and perpetrators’ ordinary celebrations. Never eroticize assault or render torture as an instructional spectacle. The horror is an order that can account for everything and regard the result as proper. Starlia’s history is already fixed; no new injury, ritual, death or failed rescue is required by the plan.

**Protection and limits:** Patronage, bought clerks, compliant physicians, guards, estate supply and court influence sustain the society. Membership, the Emperor’s knowledge of individual rites, and Cedric’s operational involvement must be earned facts. Removing one member can materially save people without abolishing the whole order; a proved rescue stays rescued.

### Premodern weapons and siege engines
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9sojx54ja","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":88000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684299,"nanoseconds":768000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Technology ceiling, IY 738:** Black-powder weapons exist as **single-shot hand cannons/handgonnes, slow matchlock arquebuses, early wheellock pistols for a few wealthy specialists, bulky bronze or wrought-iron muzzle-loading cannon, short mortars and swivel bombards**. They use loose powder, shot, lit matches or crude locks, manual ramrods, wheel carriages, powder wagons and limited field repair. No cartridges, repeating rifles, machine guns, self-propelled modern tanks, aircraft, modern explosives or automatic weapons. Wet weather, smoke, misfires, slow loading and unreliable powder are serious costs. Aether fire is a distinct magical technology, not modern electricity.
**Chariots:** Light archer chariots for harassment; heavy iron-wheel impact chariots for breaking lightly armored infantry; bear- or monster-drawn sled/chariot equivalents for snow, steppe or broken ground; protected command/ward chariots with signaling mages; siege cars carrying small bolt throwers. Chariots excel on prepared open ground and fail on stairs, tight forest tracks, deep marsh or steep rock.
**Siege engines:** Torsion scorpions, giant ballistae, stone-throwing mangonels, counterweight trebuchets, heavy siege rams, armored mobile towers, primitive bombards, powder mortars, incendiary pots, rune-dampening pylons, enchanted bolt casters, and immobile or wagon-mounted aether bombard arrays. Kharad's precise multi-barrel volley guns remain mechanically hand-cranked blackpowder weapons, **not** modern automatic cannons. Ammunition, ward batteries and specialist crews limit extended bombardment. No kingdom has a perfect answer to every fortification or magical threat.

### Prosthetics and Forgevault Commissions
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9vawljwb2","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792500525,"nanoseconds":429000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684300,"nanoseconds":145000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
A good set of silver-and-linen sockets, four fittings, a surgeon, a bone man and a craftsman’s year costs roughly 60–150 gold (T658, 04/11/738). Clinical fitting, infection risk and rehabilitation matter. Forgevault’s best smiths/artificers are already under Thornhold’s war commission; Vaelcross post-road correspondence there and back takes forty-some days, longer with bad passes (T659). A proposed 2,000-gold ceiling was not a purchase or proof of liquid funds.

### House Veyrath
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9vqbhxhx0","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605478,"nanoseconds":700000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684300,"nanoseconds":919000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
House Veyrath controls the eastern marches, major iron and aether mines, upland fortresses, foundries, armored mount-breeding grounds, and the Empire's strongest privately maintained professional warhost. Its crest is a black thorn branch around a silver tower.
Unlike the Imperial armies built for breadth and numbers, the Veyrath warhost prizes lifetime training, immense physical strength, extreme discipline, full heavy armor for even ordinary front-line soldiers, devastating shock cavalry, and unmatched stockpiles and logistical endurance. It has far fewer fighters than the united Empire but an extraordinary concentration of quality; its elite Thorn Paladins, Dreadlance Cataphracts, and thirty Skythorn anti-magic dragon knights are treated as living siege engines.
The house uses illegal levies, bonded labor, concealed prisons, manipulated ledgers, and political coercion alongside genuine frontier defense. These abuses help sustain its mines and logistical apparatus; they do not mean the professional Veyrath heavy troops are untrained slave formations.
A persistent legend says even a duke's private army could crush any neighboring kingdom and overthrow the Empire itself if Veyrath committed fully. This is a feared, politically explosive **in-world claim**, not an already fought or guaranteed outcome: logistics, food imports, rival armies, and coordinated opponents remain meaningful complications.
Cassian retains supreme strategic control of the warhost. His five children each hold combined-arms hereditary banners; **Nera commands the 5,000-warrior Blackwing Banner including his own Thorn Paladins, Dreadlance Cataphracts and six heavy flying dragon knights**, in addition to his inherited household wing. The other banners, family treasury, ducal strategic reserve, and Black Concordance do not belong to him. Banner detachments defend assigned fortress sectors but do not thereby own entire cities. See **Veyrath Family — Warhost, Heirs, and Authority**, **Veyrath Warhost — Structure, Equipment, and Logistics**, and the elite-unit entries.

### Slavery and Bondage
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9vrg21eo8","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605475,"nanoseconds":993000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684301,"nanoseconds":322000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
Slavery includes war captivity, hereditary bondage, penal labor, and intentionally permanent debt arrangements.
Traffickers also use forged identities, judgments, tags, and transport documents.
Ordinary guards, buyers, officials, debt, and records are more common coercive tools than magical soul contracts.
Legal papers can be fraudulent while still carrying real institutional force.

**Ordinary markets:** Elf and beastkin war captives are common sale commodities in the Empire, alongside human captives and debt-bound workers. Merchants advertise provenance, health, skills and title as routine trade. A legal sale may still be an atrocity; an illegal transfer is a separate legal question. Free elves/beastkin, sovereign nations, treaty protections and existing individual personhood clauses still exist. Do not assume every member of a species is enslaved.

### Aurelian imperial regional geography
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9w1aro74x","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":857000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684298,"nanoseconds":620000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Six primary destinations (heartland):** (1) **Vaelcross** is the seven-ring river and palace metropolis; (2) **Crownspire** is its independent-charter fortress academy on the upper plateau; (3) **Vharos** is the dry southern amphitheater and slave-market city; (4) **Miravel** is the hot-spring limestone region of nine competitive magical colleges; (5) **Gullhaven** is the western tidal harbor and naval gate; (6) **Amberreach** is the central canal country of harvest guilds and imperial granaries. **Tideglass Isles** are an offshore voyage region, not a seventh imperial capital. Smaller markets and bridges continue to exist, but these six are the main overland/urban showcase settings.
**Landscape gradient:** The central three-river basin supports walled cities, manor agriculture and irrigated grain. Western roads turn damp and timbered near the Elaranth border and then salt-swept toward Gullhaven. Southern roads become ochre stone, hotter, dryer, and religiously contested before Solenne and Cindervale. Northeastern roads become steeper and more militarized toward Kharad and the Veyrath marches. Season, river height, snow and harvest schedules determine ordinary travel and military supply more reliably than convenient teleportation.
**Dark-fantasy contrast:** Noble terrace districts, universities, arenas, chapels, municipal festivals and night markets coexist with forced levies, debt tribunals, slave compounds, disease wards and guarded grain monopolies. The Empire does not have identical laws or architecture in every province; a city governor, temple synod and local guild may each wield real power.

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Mid-year admission is restricted to outstanding power or talent. Tuition is still owed even with a scholarship (Nera, T589, 01/11/738). Starlia has no enrollment, qualification or promised automatic admission.

<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9wg70nl2q","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605480,"nanoseconds":894000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684299,"nanoseconds":384000000}} -->
