### Aether and Arcs
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9wnxz5spk","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605478,"nanoseconds":700000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684299,"nanoseconds":3000000}} -->
always load: yes

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Magic channels aether through stable internal circuits called **Arcs**. Arc measures magical development, capacity and complexity only; it is not total combat strength.

- **Arc 0:** no stable Arc or no combat-usable aether circuit.
- **Arc 1:** basic spells, simple reinforcement and one straightforward magical discipline.
- **Arc 2:** reliable trained combat magic, stronger reinforcement and sustained specialist techniques.
- **Arc 3:** combined techniques under pressure, advanced control and strong battlefield influence.
- **Arc 4:** difficult battlefield-level magic capable of deciding major engagements when used well.
- **Arc 5:** strategic-level mastery; rare even among senior students and professional mages.
- **Arc 6:** rare, internationally significant magical power. Never assign casually.

**Crownspire IY738 expectations:** Y1 average Arc 0–1, outstanding Arc 2; Y2 average Arc 1–2, outstanding Arc 3; Y3 average Arc 2–3, outstanding Arc 4; Y4 average Arc 3–4, outstanding Arc 5. Arc 3+ in Y1 requires explicit exceptional lore. Graduates and veteran adults are not restricted by student-year averages.

**Nera is an extreme exception:** he is currently **Arc 4** in Y1 while deliberately concealing his real capability. His perfected Heaven Splitter and established physical feats are not capped by ordinary Academy comparisons. Equipment, aether reserves, spell compatibility, preparation, restraint, collateral risk, terrain, physical capability and combat experience all remain separate factors.

### Peoples
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9x7gh1y3c","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605478,"nanoseconds":260000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684299,"nanoseconds":768000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
Humans, elves, beastfolk, demonkin, dwarves, and mixed families exist across political borders.
Ancestry does not determine allegiance, morality, occupation, citizenship, or magical ceiling.
Beastfolk have largely human bodies with animal ears, tails where appropriate, and limited other animal traits.
Demonkin are mortal descendants of peoples altered by the Cinder Rifts and are not inherently corrupted.
Rift creatures are separate from demonkin.
Saryuun is a beastkin-majority sovereign country and Umbrath a demonkin-majority sovereign country, but all peoples also live elsewhere; no ancestry automatically confers their citizenship. See the two national entries.

### Imperial legions and combined arms
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9xy44x69x","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605477,"nanoseconds":12000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684300,"nanoseconds":536000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Scale (Imperial Year 738):** Approximately 400,000–500,000 professional Crown troops in standing formations; another 250,000–450,000 provincial and noble soldiers can be summoned with varying readiness; under a general mobilization, hundreds of thousands more enslaved captives, convicts, and coerced debtors can be driven into expendable field formations. These are empire-wide strategic estimates, **not** an army gathered at one battlefield. Headcounts fluctuate with campaign and political obedience; forced levies are not equivalent in combat quality to trained regulars.
**Doctrine:** The Imperials seek a solution to every type of threat. An ordinary offensive combines a massed infantry center, armored reserves, swift cavalry on each flank, missile ranks and field archers, mobile wards and combat mages, and long-range magical siege batteries. They prefer layered formations, simultaneous campaigns, and replacing losses by mobilizing men and conquered resources. The Empire is powerful through breadth and depth, not because every Imperial soldier is an elite warrior.
**Principal formations:**
- **Crown Legions:** Disciplined spear, sword, and shield infantry, the army's dependable core; hold ground, build camps, and defend the artillery line.
- **Ironwall Orders:** Heavy infantry and armored knights in full or near-full plate, with shield-breakers and reserve cavalry escorts; high-quality shock fighters, but costly and much less uniformly equipped than Veyrath line troops.
- **Horse Lancers and Border Riders:** Light reconnaissance, pursuit, and conventional heavy horse cavalry. Mounted nobles often command these wings.
- **Imperial Beast Cohorts:** Mounted gryphons, horned war-beasts, drakes, and other domesticated or bound monsters; used for vertical attacks, rough ground, flanking, and anti-cavalry work. Mount type varies by province, and none is assumed invulnerable.
- **Bow and Bolt Corps:** Longbowmen, heavy crossbowmen, monster-hunters, and mage-supported marksmen who saturate approaches and protect gun-free aether artillery positions.
- **Arc Colleges:** Integrated battle-mages, healers, counter-mages, and ward specialists. Lesser Arcs support companies; Third and Fourth Arc specialists can decide engagements locally; rare Fifth Arc figures are strategic assets, not numerous line soldiers.
- **Aether Bombard Batteries:** Enormous crystal-fed siege casters, rune ballistae, storm projectors, and long-range magical destruction arrays. They can batter fortresses, formations, and wards from far behind the line, but require trained crews, time to charge, stabilizers, and large supply convoys.
- **Chain Levies:** Enslaved captives and penal/debt-bound conscripts pressed into front ranks with crude shields and tools. Officers sacrifice them to exhaust arrows, trigger defenses, dig dangerous approaches, absorb first strikes, or cover professional troops. Coercion, starvation, desertion, and revolt make them unreliable and expose the Empire's cruelty.
**Strengths:** Overwhelming total manpower, balanced specialist coverage, destructive magical artillery, many replacement armies, access to multiple provinces and their economies, and political resources to wage drawn-out wars. **Weaknesses:** Uneven provincial quality; rival commanders and nobles; expensive mage and artillery chains; moral and military instability from enslaved screens; dependence on long, vulnerable transport routes and outside contractors—especially Veyrath arms and armor.
**Additional integrated branches (part of the standing/provincial totals):** Skywatch Gryphon Riders (interception and dispatch), Lesser-Wyvern Cohorts (fast assault and reconnaissance), Eagle-Signal Scouts, **Bronze-wheel War Chariots** (mounted spear- and crossbow-crews), **Field Handgonners** (single-shot matchlock hand cannon/arquebus companies), and **Grand Siege Trains** (stone-throwers, giant scorpions, slow muzzle-loading black-powder bombards, rune-piercing ballistae and expensive aether engines). Fortress sky-towers use bolt curtains, grounded anti-flight wards, burning nets and interceptors. Deployment figures are included in existing military strength estimates; no new empire-wide soldiers are added. See **Imperial Air Corps and War Machines**.

### Kharad Holdfast Armies
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9ygyhfeu1","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":668000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684301,"nanoseconds":322000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Military character:** Allied but independent dwarven holds excel in defensive engineering. **Granitewall Longshields**, **Deepaxe Hammerguards**, **Tunnel Sappers**, **Ironhorn War-Goat Chariots**, **Rune-Runic Thunderers** (primitive hand cannon/arquebus teams), **Anvildeep Organ Volley Guns** (multiple manually loaded barrels), **Grudge Stone-Throwers**, **Great Foundry Cannons**, **Rune-Flame Projectors**, **Counter-Wyvern Harpoon Batteries**, and cumbersome **Suspended Survey Gliders** (small tethered or wind-borne observation devices, not a flying war fleet). Slow armored mule wagons transport powder and supplies. Dwarves do not casually field true dragons or mass bird cavalry.
**Strategy and limits:** Fortified passes, accurate siege fire, countersapping, elite armor and anti-air denial dominate Crownfang terrain. Dwarven columns are slow and vulnerable to being surrounded on open farmland. Councils can refuse passage or trade with any power; Kar-Durim and Anvildeep retain separate guild rivalries.

### Money and Everyday Prices
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwq9zqnf5nx9","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605480,"nanoseconds":636000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684300,"nanoseconds":536000000}} -->
always load: yes

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Gold, silver and copper:** 1g = 100s; 1s = 100c. Pricing intuition only: 1g ≈ US$1,000, 1s ≈ $10, 1c ≈ $0.10, not an actual exchange market. “Crown” is the older gold name, not a separate currency. The earlier twelve-silver ratio is superseded; do not recompute already recorded nominal sums without explicit accounting.

Foreign coins trade by purity and weight; clipped coins lose value. Imperial banknotes circulate chiefly in Vaelcross and deteriorate with damp/travel; outside it, metal is strongly preferred.

Typical prices: bread/simple meal 10–15c; hot stew/decent meal 40c; modest private inn room 80–150c/night; fine dining 1–5s/person; ale 5–12c; bath 15–30c; stabling/fodder 20–50c/day; wool cloak 1–3s; dagger 3–6s; serviceable sword 10–25s; riding horse 25–60s.

Daily employed pay: farm laborer 1s; town middle-class worker 3s; professor/skilled adventurer/specialist 6–9s; wartime soldier 2s plus rations; junior commander 3–4s; senior commander 6–9s. Employment can be seasonal, delayed or paid partly in kind. Famous fighters/mages, major merchants and exceptional professionals have no income ceiling. Prices vary with harvest, danger and scarcity.

Earlier source context — Money and Everyday Prices (retain as historical context wherever later dated records supersede it):

**Currency:** Gold (g), silver (s), and copper (c) coins circulate across kingdoms and are widely trusted. **1g = 100s; 1s = 100c.** For pricing intuition only: **1g ≈ US$1,000; 1s ≈ $10; 1c ≈ $0.10.** These are not actual exchange rates. Foreign coins are accepted by metal weight and purity; clipped or debased coins lose value.

**Imperial banknotes:** Issued by the Empire, but used mainly within its capital. The fragile notes deteriorate rapidly from dampness, travel, and handling. Outside the capital, merchants strongly prefer solid metal coins.

**Ordinary prices (guidelines, not fixed laws):**
- Bread or simple meal: **10–15c**; hot stew and a decent meal: **40c**.
- Private room at a modest inn, one night: **80–150c** (0.8–1.5s). A stable bed or shared floor costs less.
- Fine dining: typically **1–5s per person**; noble banquets have no upper limit.
- Ale: **5–12c**; bath: **15–30c**; horse stabling and fodder: **20–50c/day**.
- Wool cloak: **1–3s**; ordinary dagger: **3–6s**; serviceable sword: **10–25s**; riding horse: **25–60s**.

**Typical daily pay (when employed):** Farm laborer **1s**; town middle-class worker **3s**; respected professor, skilled adventurer, or specialist **6–9s**. Wartime soldier **2s plus food/rations**; junior commander **3–4s**; senior commander **6–9s**. Pay can be seasonal, delayed, or partly in kind. Elite fighters, famous mages, major merchants, and exceptional professionals have **no earnings ceiling**; contracts, spoils, and patronage can dwarf wages.

**Use:** Prices vary by city, harvest, war, danger, and scarcity. Copper handles daily purchases; silver covers wages and travel; gold serves savings, estates, large trade, and expensive commissions.

### Vharos bonded registry law
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqa49qayp93","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792500527,"nanoseconds":784000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684303,"nanoseconds":290000000}} -->
aliases: ["Vharos bonded pens"]

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Bonded persons are recorded as chattel, indentured or clause-bound. Indentured wages are held in trust by the registrar and released to the holder on request (T555, 31/10/738). The city watch needs a magistrate’s writ to investigate a bonded pen; friends on the bench make writs hard to obtain (Isolde, T601, 01/11/738). A sealed pen is not proof its records are secure.
Updates:
- [T555 · Monday 31/10/738] Bonded persons are recorded as chattel, indentured or clause-bound; indentured wages are held in trust by the registrar and released to the holder on request.
<!-- nera-line: {"when":"Monday 31/10/738","evidence":[{"revision":0,"order":1296,"id":"msg_mvcldo6hvpitay"},{"order":1297,"id":"msg_mvdard24jqoefi","revision":0}],"by":"auto","text":"Bonded persons are recorded as chattel, indentured or clause-bound; indentured wages are held in trust by the registrar and released to the holder on request.","id":"ln_mvjpvwqaxcfrz8qq","turn":555,"sourceRevision":1171,"at":1792302340283,"src":1297} -->
- [T601 · Tuesday 1/11/738] Isolde says the city watch cannot investigate a bonded pen without a magistrate's writ, and such writs are hard to get when the pen's owner has friends on the bench.
<!-- nera-line: {"when":"Tuesday 1/11/738","by":"auto","text":"Isolde says the city watch cannot investigate a bonded pen without a magistrate's writ, and such writs are hard to get when the pen's owner has friends on the bench.","evidence":[{"id":"msg_mvdwahc8hzx2p5","order":1403,"revision":0},{"id":"msg_mvdwayecvzl3wc","order":1404,"revision":0}],"turn":601,"id":"ln_mvjpvwqajtgswgzl","sourceRevision":1331,"at":1792336626877,"src":1404} -->

### Veyrath warhost logistics
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqa685lme2n","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605477,"nanoseconds":12000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684302,"nanoseconds":880000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Scale (Imperial Year 738):** A professional, permanent force of roughly **75,000–90,000 fighters** across the eastern marches and outlying garrisons, **including the 20,000 fighters of five hereditary child banners and the thirty dragon knights**. It is dwarfed by the Empire's combined muster but may be the most lethal privately controlled army in the known region. The number excludes separately counted miners, smiths, civilian wagon crews, coerced industrial workers, and household retainers who are not warhost combat troops.
**Central doctrine:** Quality over quantity. The Veyrath house does not regard ordinary foot soldiers as disposable flesh. Every accepted professional receives years of physical conditioning, shield and weapon drills, formation training, and logistical education; veterans form the bulk of the ranks. Even regular front-line infantry wear unusually complete **knight-grade full heavy plate** over mail and padding, with reinforced joints, visored helms, and heavy boots. Their shields, polearms, greatswords, and massive war-hammers are built for strength-assisted combat. Rune work and aether-tempered steel make equipment durable, but not indestructible; the most extravagant runic enhancements are reserved for elites. They fight slowly when conservation matters and terrifyingly fast on a committed charge.
**Standing combat formations (approximate; specialist elite companies and the five children's hereditary banners are allocations within these totals, not added on top):**
- **Thornwall Shield Legions, 35,000–40,000:** The line backbone; full-plate spear-and-tower-shield infantry trained to absorb cavalry, hold narrow passes, advance beneath magical fire, and remain disciplined after days of attrition.
- **Ironthorn Breakers, 11,000–14,000:** Greatsword, maul, and poleaxe assault foot, used to shatter shield walls, storm gates, and exploit a breach opened by artillery or elite knights.
- **Blacksteel Lancers, 7,000–9,000:** Heavy armored conventional cavalry; riders in complete plate on exceptionally strong warhorses wearing plated barding, intended to break formations in a disciplined charge rather than scout lightly.
- **March Beast Riders, 2,000–3,500:** Fully armored heavy cavalry on bred war-beasts—ironhorn chargers, drake-like ground mounts, and other massive marching monsters. Every battle mount receives custom fitted face, chest, flank, neck, and limb protection.
- **Steelwatch and Longbow Guard, 4,000–6,000:** Veteran armored archers, heavy crossbow crews, scouts, border watch, and anti-mage skirmish specialists. They remain better protected than most rival missile troops.
- **Fortress Artificers, Ward Guard, Sappers, and Reserves, 10,000–14,000:** Armored combat engineers, professional siege crews, field warders, and fortress fighting units; their work keeps the warhost supplied and hard to dislodge.
**Signature elites and shared distribution:** **Thorn Paladins** are a selected superheavy knight class within assault/guard forces; **Dreadlance Cataphracts** are the armored land-mount shock champions; and **Skythorn dragon knights** are precisely **30 flying true-dragon pairs**, each rider a master swordsman and Fourth- or Fifth-Arc battle-mage in layered anti-magic runic armor. All count within the existing warhost headcount and **are permanently distributed across Cassian's central host and all five child banners**, including **six personally under Nera's Blackwing Banner**; there is no separately locked ducal dragon order and no 30 added on top. The dragon knights are distinct in function from Dreadlance ground cavalry and excluded from its rider tally. Elite detachments cooperate across banners during a grand muster while retaining their sworn command affiliations.
**Logistics — the real Veyrath weapon:** The house owns mines, ore refineries, massive forges, road-and-bridge patrols, stone granaries, armor repair yards, remount stables, beast-breeding fortresses, **dragon aeries and dedicated meat stores**, reserve aether cells, medical stations, convoy escorts, and prepositioned depots. Armor and even mount barding have replacement fittings. Each march route has surveyed bridges, water sources, and cached feed. Units maintain reserve kits and trained farriers or monster-handlers. Professional soldiers can be resupplied, re-armored, and rotated faster than opponents expect. Its logistical machine is exceptionally effective but depends on coercive labor, long-held economic control, and vulnerable grain imports from outside its own mountainous lands.
**Why the Empire fears Veyrath:** An open-field Veyrath breakthrough can destroy larger conventional formations; normal arrows and common weapons struggle against its steel, its disciplined heavy cavalry can roll a flank, and its elites can puncture defensive lines almost like siege engines. Yet the house is not literally omnipresent: long sieges, scorched farmland, concentrated strategic magic, severed grain roads, political fracture, and simultaneous fronts threaten it. Courtiers repeat the **legend** that a Veyrath all-out march could overthrow any neighboring kingdom or even the imperial throne with ease. That legend is treated as credible enough to change royal policy, but it remains an untested claim, not an already settled historical fact.
**Additional war assets already inside these formation counts:** Thornwall anti-air pike/bolt teams; Ironthorn gate assault and mobile ram crews; **Blacksteel Ironwheel Chariots** (armored four-horse war chariots with heavy blade or crossbow crews); **Behemoth Siege Cars** (slow horn-beast drawn mobile shield walls and giant scorpions); small, heavily armored **March Wyvern Flights** (lesser two-legged winged drakes for scouting, interception and short assault runs); trained **Ravencrest Great-Bird Bowriders** (fast arrow skirmishers, especially effective screening flights but fewer and less agile than Elaranth's); **Forgevault War Machines** (torsion bolt-throwers, counter-dragon chain launchers, rolling siege towers, giant shielded rams, black-powder bombards, rune-stabilized aether artillery and field ward pylons); and small numbers of heavy **matchlock handgonners**. True winged dragons remain the uniquely counted **30 Skythorn pairs**; war drakes in the Dreadlance are wingless, wyverns are smaller two-legged flyers, and giant birds are neither dragons nor wyverns. All added machines/crews and aerial riders are *within*, not on top of, the 75,000–90,000 standing headcount. Forgevault's arms industry produces spare axles, torsion cords, powder kegs, and ward cells; cannon remain cumbersome, slow-firing muzzle-loaders. See **Veyrath Air Wings, Chariots and Engines**.

### Veyrath Eastern Marches
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqa6ra88wqw","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":857000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684302,"nanoseconds":96000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Six primary destinations:** (1) **Thornhold**, the fortified three-ring ducal military capital; (2) **Forgevault**, the canyon of ore, aether-tempered steel and siege manufactories; (3) **Blackgorge**, the vertical frontier gate facing Varkesh; (4) **Greyford**, a debt-and-caravan river market on the main war road; (5) **Harrowmere**, the protected wagon, grain and war-beast fodder supply basin; and (6) **Blackwing Keep**, Nera's own combined-arms banner cantonment. **Nine Hollows** remains the already established poor mining settlement and a crucial personal-history location, but is not promoted to a grand city. The **Veyrath Estate** remains the family's inner Thornhold compound and the **Veyrath Townhouse** is its Vaelcross residence.
**Terrain and architecture:** Black basalt ridges and cold streams in the west; steep iron-bearing ravines and slag forests in the central forge district; wind-hard alpine cliffs toward Blackgorge; warmer lowland agriculture, peat waterways and stone granaries around Harrowmere. The farther east a traveler moves, the more frequent military milestones, reinforced bridge piers, beast-proof road widths, tunnel depots, roofed powder magazines and high anti-air signal towers become. Civilian homes use the same dark stone, iron fittings and steep slate roofs at humbler scale.
**How the warhost lives:** A Veyrath armored soldier is supported by armor-polish crews, feed contracts, farriers, medics, smiths, wardwrights, stable labor, bridge engineers, winter grain stores and standardized replacement fittings. This logistical reliability is why smaller Veyrath numbers can field such dense heavy plate; it is not endless magical resupply. Cassian commands the collective warhost; his children command their **already counted hereditary banners** and assigned garrisons. Nera's Blackwing Banner remains exactly **5,000 counted troops**, including six of the fixed **thirty Skythorn dragon knights**, without new battalions added by the new city descriptions.

### Vaelcross defensive rings
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqa7kezjm80","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605477,"nanoseconds":800000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684302,"nanoseconds":880000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Scale and silhouette:** Vaelcross, capital of the continent-spanning Aurelian Empire, is a fortified megacity where **seven nested defensive districts or rings** rise from river ports and outer common wards to an elevated imperial palace precinct. These are not seven perfectly continuous geometric circles: three rivers, cliff terraces, fortified bridges and canals tie the defensive sectors together. Outer walls and parapet towers are roughly 20–35 meters high in principal stretches; the upper palace bastions are even more monumental. Layered dark stone, gilded steel imperial eagles, gothic basilicas, enormous iron bells, pointed ward pylons, red crystal lamps, chained river harbors, imperial banners and winged-beast statues give the city a crushing dark-fantasy scale.
**From outside inward:** (1) fortified farms, watch roads and levy camps; (2) outer people's quarters behind a great rampart; (3) river gates, stone bridge keeps and chained fleet moorings; (4) merchant, arsenal and artillery wards; (5) noble residence and ministry walls; (6) cathedral, tribunal, elite barracks and upper plateau approaches; (7) the sovereign palace citadel and the highest strategic ward towers. **Crownspire sits on its own high plateau reached through guarded approaches in the protected upper capital region**; it is a separate chartered campus fortress, **not a wing of the palace or a site where palace guards can casually ignore Academy law**.
**Defensive force and magic:** Crown Legions and Ironwall Orders rotate through bastions, with beast-cavalry ready in vaulted stables, ward-mage colleges in tower stations, and aether bombard batteries set to cover rivers and long approaches. Skyward ballistae and aerial barriers deter drakes and hostile dragon riders. Gate-runes scan dangerous enchantments, while inner sanctums are protected by staggered, individually fueled ward lattices; alarms echo as both bells and violet beacon fires. Slower-moving siege engines must fight across multiple elevations and bridge choke points, while assault magic must peel successive rune layers rather than breach one universal shield. The city's scale allows enormous strategic reserves, but civilian congestion, coerced labor, corrupt tolls, food supply and rival commanders can also obstruct a defense. No barrier offers absolute immunity to Fifth- or Sixth-Arc-scale attacks.
**Dark-fantasy social contrast:** Emperors and high nobles move under warm ward-lit colonnades while lower boroughs crowd against soot-covered walls, guarded checkpoints and labor musters. The capital's terrifying fortification also exists to **contain unrest and preserve imperial hierarchy**, not merely protect citizens against foreign armies.
**Sky interdiction:** Each key river bridge and upper wall hosts a rotating anti-air ballista nest, counter-flight mage team, and gryphon signal post. Three-tier aerial response: outer scout patrols, mid-city gryphon/wyvern interceptors, and upper-city ward pylons and artillery. Campus patrols at Crownspire's gate remain under charter rules; military altitude restrictions do not authorize arbitrary arrests.

### Thorn Paladins
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqa9c3dekro","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605477,"nanoseconds":384000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684302,"nanoseconds":494000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Also called the **Black Thorn Guard**. Approximately **300–600 active champions** selected from veteran Veyrath warriors: this is the warhost's most feared **superheavy infantry**, a dark-fantasy equivalent in battlefield presence to legendary towering super-soldiers, not a modern firearm-bearing force. Years of punishing conditioning, elite Arc reinforcement, exceptional constitution, and masterwork runic armor produce fighters far larger in impact than ordinary human knights. They wear **sealed, layered, full-body blackened plate** with silver thorn runes, enormous reinforced pauldrons, completely encased limbs and hands, heavy closed helms, and integral ward screens; even weak points are plated and warded. These are living warriors, **not** autonomous suits or invulnerable machines.
Their weapons are oversized two-handed blades, executioner axes, great hammers, tower shields, and fortress-breaking lances wielded on foot. A Paladin may smash a gate, overturn a siege carriage, carve a path through ranks of regular troops, or stand through volleys that would kill other men. They operate in small disciplined assault cadres, serve as fortress-breaking champions and local reserves, and are distributed among the five hereditary banners and Cassian's central host, with **30 permanently sworn to Nera**. Destroying one usually demands focused high-tier magic, a specialized monster, anti-ward weapons, a battlefield trap, or sustained combined assault. Even so, enough concentrated force, exhaustion, damaged wards, or poor tactics can kill them. **They must never be scaled automatically to Nera's fully mastered mountain-cleaving and sky-splitting Heaven Splitter.**

### Umbrath Ash-Covenant Armies
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqaa935qt3k","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605482,"nanoseconds":43000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684302,"nanoseconds":494000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Military doctrine — Demonkin Covenant:** Umbrath fights best on shattered basalt, obsidian slopes and warded Rift approaches. **Basalt Oathguard** are disciplined full-plate polearm-and-shield veterans; **Emberveil Duelists** use quick blades and anti-illusion stances; **Glassfire Sappers** employ heat-treated tools, tunnel supports and rune-sealing skills; **Ashbound Salamander Lancers** ride furnace-resistant beasts; **Cinderwing Wyvern Flights** conduct reconnaissance and violent dives but remain lesser than true dragons; **Nightglass Batwing Scouts** are light cliffside flyers rather than dragon equivalents; and **Bell Choir Countermages** suppress hostile rituals around fixed ward-bells.
**Siege and air defense:** Obsidian mirror pylons, chain-harpoon scorpions, alchemical ember pots, slow stone bombards and rare glass-shell siege beasts give the border keeps strong area denial. Rune magic is powerful only while charges, inscription integrity and trained singers endure; wards cannot nullify every Fifth-Arc caster, dragon or physical siege engine. Officers must cooperate with ritual custodians over ancient Rift seals, making large campaigns politically slow. The Covenant is neither united with Cindervale's imperial demonkin nor automatically hostile to all humans and elves.

### Veyrath Air Wings, Chariots and Engines
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqacvjuiycj","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":286000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684301,"nanoseconds":708000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Sky hierarchy:** (1) **Ravencrest Bowriders**: trained great hawk/eagle mounts flown by armored scouts with short composite bows, javelins and signal drums; agile and invaluable for reconnaissance, but inferior in woodland stealth and mounted arrowcraft to veteran Elaranth elven units. (2) **March Wyvern Flights**: small wings of armored lesser wyvern riders, often Second–Third Arc professionals, for anti-scout strikes, anti-raider sweeps and counter-air defense; each mount is far weaker than an adult dragon and lacks a dragon's massed anti-magic harness. (3) **The exact Thirty Skythorn true dragons**: the only strategic heavy dragon rider force, with twenty distributed among the heirs and ten retained by Cassian. No extra dragon numbers are implied by adding other flying beasts.
**Chariots and war machines:** **Blacksteel Ironwheel Chariots** use armored heavy destriers, iron wheels and enclosed crew baskets with lances or mass crossbows; **Behemoth Siege Cars** mount heavy scorpions or shielded rams on beast-drawn platforms. **Forgevault Siege Trains** field great rune-bolts, chain-net anti-air launchers, twin-armed torsion scorpions, counterweight stone-throwers, tracked-by-muscle portable ward screens, slow breach bombards, aether-powered siege casters, and countermining devices. Heavy **matchlock handgonners** are uncommon but useful against unwarded knights; every fired shot costs powder and a long reload. Exceptional Veyrath forge steel improves barrels and armor but does not erase maintenance needs.
**Banner access/no double counting:** All five children's banners contain archer flyers, wyvern riders, chariot crews, early guns, anti-air operators, and war-machine specialists within their already recorded military headcounts. Nera's Blackwing **5,000** includes, within existing subtotals: **560 Blacksteel mounted lancers + 60 Ironwheel chariot fighters (20 three-person chariots) = 620**; **184 March Beast Riders + 36 Behemoth siege-car fighters (12 three-person crews) = 220**; **240 Steelwatch ground missile/scout troops + 40 Ravencrest bowriders + 20 March Wyvern riders = 300**; **250 existing battle-mage/engineer/fortress soldiers + 60 specialist air-ward and skywatch defenders = 310**; **210 artillery/armed supply specialists including bombards, giant scorpions, matchlock teams, ammunition guards and forge repair crews**. The **2,350 Thornwall, 930 Ironthorn, 30 Paladins, 24 Dreadlances and 6 true-dragon knights** stay unchanged. The allocation remains **exactly 5,000** without conjuring additional troops, animals, or uncounted rider regiments. Civilians remain separately accounted for. Other banners maintain comparable categories scaled to their own strengths; Cassian's central forge reserve remains larger.

### Imperial noble conduct
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqacypo1cmm","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792501479,"nanoseconds":73000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684303,"nanoseconds":690000000}} -->
always load: yes

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null,"unavailable":false,"sourceRevision":0} -->
**Social norms:** The Empire is a deeply corrupt feudal society. Noble blood, wealth, power and connections determine rights. Inequality and aristocratic entitlement are considered natural, not immoral.

**Ordinary behaviour:** Lust, greed, domination, cruelty and extravagant indulgence are commonplace among nobles. Ownership of slaves, concubines and dependent servants is normal. Sexual exploitation, coercion, humiliation and harsh punishment of powerless people are widely tolerated, not exceptional scandals.

**Slavery:** Humans, elves, beastkin, war captives and debtors are legally traded, inherited, gifted and exploited. Their consent and suffering carry little weight against an owner's authority.

**Law:** Courts, temples, officials and merchants uphold the hierarchy. Noble wrongdoing against commoners rarely brings punishment unless another powerful interest is threatened. The Emperor and Prince Cedric participate in and benefit from this system.

**Social attitudes:** Nobles may be affectionate parents, loyal friends, generous patrons or devout believers while casually exploiting dependents. Most see no contradiction. Individual nobles differ in kindness and cruelty without necessarily rejecting noble privilege.

**Katarina's society:** Her secret circle goes beyond ordinary noble behaviour through organized atrocities, ritual sacrifice and cruel experimentation. Its extremity is exceptional; the hierarchy enabling it is normal.

**Narrator rules:** Never portray routine noble exploitation as shocking or automatically illegal. Avoid imposing modern moral standards or universal outrage. Show corruption through ordinary etiquette, markets, households, festivities and institutions. Individual kindness or resistance remains possible but does not automatically reform society.

**Tone:** Berserk, Dark Souls and Elden Ring-inspired dark fantasy. Cruelty is embedded in civilization, not limited to villains.

### Soul Scripts
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqag0ury286","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605475,"nanoseconds":993000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684302,"nanoseconds":880000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
Soul Scripts are rare specialized talents rather than ordinary Arc techniques.
Isolde's existing soul bond is inscribed into her living identity and cannot be erased or transferred by Nera.
The bond compels service, protection, and inability to deliberately harm Nera.
The bond does not create affection, sexual consent, omniscience, infinite stamina, or guaranteed victory.
Updates:
- [T275 · October 738] Nera has known since the bond-wright read the seal that cancelling Isolde's bond is impossible; Isolde had that clause struck as impossible from the record of what Nera can offer.

### Skythorn Dragon Knights
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqahprf380x","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605477,"nanoseconds":384000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684301,"nanoseconds":708000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Thirty Black Wings:** House Veyrath fields **exactly 30 active true-dragon-riding heavy knights** in Imperial Year 738: 30 veteran or genius riders bonded to 30 genuine winged war dragons, supported by armorers, handlers, aether ward engineers and fortified aeries. A winged war dragon differs from a Blackscale War Drake, the wingless Dreadlance ground mount. They are **an integrated specialist arm of the general warhost**, not a separate ducal division whose knights cannot belong to Nera or his siblings. Their 30 riders count inside the existing 75,000–90,000 warhost fighters and **outside** the 150–300 Dreadlance land-cavalry total.
**Hereditary assignments:** **Nera's Blackwing Banner has 6 permanently sworn dragon knights; Lucien 6; Magerrett 4; Taigar 3; Elin 1 (under her appointed marshal and regent); Cassian's direct reserve 10. Total 30, without duplication.** The riders receive ordinary tactical commands from their own banner leader and marshal, not from a separate order-captain overruling the heirs. Flights may combine temporarily for a large battle, but return to their actual banners afterward. Nera's six are his own troops to inspect, assign, and command, including for authorized local defensive action; they are **not borrowed only with the duke's prior approval**. Permanent dragon aeries and armor workshops service both central and banner contingents on recognized supply agreements.
**Swordsmanship, magic and experience:** Every dragon knight is either a proven veteran from severe campaigns or an exceptionally talented warrior who passed the same rigorous standard. **All 30 have mastered sword combat and combat aether magic: 26 are Fourth Arc, 4 are Fifth Arc**. The Fifth Arc riders are allocated **2 to Cassian's reserve, 1 to Lucien, and 1 to Nera**. Every rider can fight competently in full armor with a blade while casting ward-breaking, counter-magic, aerial suppression, and destructive battlefield spells. Third Arc riders never qualify for the active Thirty.
**Heavily armored anti-magic knights and dragons:** Riders wear enormous fully enclosed blackened **aether-tempered heavy steel plate**, segmented at joints, over reinforced underlayers and dense silver-thorn rune tracery. The helmet, visor, hands, legs and throat are enclosed; inner and outer counterspell lattices route stray aether through charged ward cells. Their partnered dragons wear **massive tailor-fitted runic barding** over the skull, throat, chest, belly, spine, wing roots and limb joints, with protected but functional flight membranes. Rider and dragon share one calibrated ward harness. It can **neutralize or disperse ordinary/lower-Arc offensive magic**, sharply reduce much high-tier spell damage while supplied, and withstand prolonged routine magical bombardment. It is **not absolute immunity**: peer-tier Fourth–Fifth Arc magic, unfamiliar spells, saturation, exhausted ward batteries, broken anchors, giant bolts, wing injuries or anti-dragon siege weapons can still kill them.
**Fighting style and support:** The winged eastern dragons have crushing talons, jaws, armored tails and immense lifting power; their aetheric breath can destroy field positions but requires reserves and rest. The riders use monumental enchanted swords, armored aerial lances, high-Arc magic, airborne counterspells and disciplined diving assaults. They intercept enemy monsters, break magic batteries, smash tower tops and shield retreating troops, but are sensitive to storms, supply, aerie security, bodily fatigue and sustained anti-air artillery. Their armor's weight is physically supported by large trained dragons and aether reinforcement, not ignored. **None of these riders is automatically equal to Nera's already-established, mountain-cleaving, sky-splitting Heaven Splitter.**

<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqal3u0rbfx","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792540968,"nanoseconds":123000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684303,"nanoseconds":690000000}} -->

### Still Interval
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqamehfgkku","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605475,"nanoseconds":993000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684302,"nanoseconds":96000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
Still Interval is Nera's rare talent for interrupting magical transitions through timing and contact.
It does not erase an already completed soul bond.
It is not a universal answer to every spell, oath, ward, or magical structure.

### Vaelcross merchant guilds
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqascfqjgbe","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792504654,"nanoseconds":666000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684303,"nanoseconds":690000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Updates:

### Vaelcross–Thornhold Relay
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqat29ap8cf","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792500526,"nanoseconds":597000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684303,"nanoseconds":290000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Blackwing Keep lies west of Thornhold on the Eastern Road: Vaelcross → Amberreach → Greyford → Harrowmere → Thornhold. A hard-pace relay rider needs five days one way (T653, 04/11/738). Normal post and industrial replies take longer; do not treat this as a five-day heavy-army march.
Updates:
- [T653 · Friday 4/11/738] Blackwing Keep lies west of Thornhold on the Eastern Road (Vaelcross–Amberreach–Greyford–Harrowmere–Thornhold); a hard-pace relay rider needs five days one way

### Veyrath fortifications
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqatdufi6f0","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605477,"nanoseconds":800000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684302,"nanoseconds":96000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Doctrine:** Veyrath cities are fortresses first and settlements second. The march has **three defensive depths**: (1) road towers, watch-forts and iron-gated bridges that delay raiders and warn the main host; (2) walled industrial and market cities with overlapping artillery ranges; (3) inner mountain castles and storehouse citadels that can fight even if outlying roads fall. Its appearance is relentlessly grim: soot-black basalt, scarred steel shutters, silver thorn runes, chains across chasms, armored gate guardians, ward-lit execution squares and enormous banners under crimson watch lamps. Beneath the spectacle lie grain accounting, water collection, ammunition vaults, sappers' tunnels and fast repair access.
**Typical defenses:** Outer walls about 15–30 meters high at the major fortified cities and far higher citadels on natural cliffs; walls are reinforced with steel ribs and laid upon ward-anchored bedrock. Dry moats or flooded ditches, barbed stake fields and anti-cavalry pits channel enemies into crossfires from heavy bolt-casters and crystal-fed aether artillery. Gate systems include angled approaches, double/triple portcullises, murder galleries, countermining chambers and independently sealable districts. Anti-air towers and rune-cast nets threaten flying monsters; layered ward screens diffuse ordinary siege spells while specialist battle-mages maintain the anchors. Armored war-beast patrols can sally from wide internal ramps; strong bridges are engineered for cataphract behemoths and dragon logistics.
**Garrisons and endurance:** Heavy-infantry shield legions hold walls; elite Paladins act as breach seals; Dreadlances countercharge through sally gates; permanently distributed Skythorn dragons patrol above assigned banners; engineers, warders, counter-mages, artillery crews and armored archers protect every fort. Blackwing, Silverward, First Thorn, Ironroot and Greenwatch have recognized defensive sectors, but **a banner's garrison duty does not make an heir owner of an entire ducal city**. Veyrath's military roads connect magazines, armor forges, underground cisterns, hardened hospitals, emergency grain vaults, monster fodder stores and ward-cell depots. The house can withstand many sieges far longer than outsiders expect, but no wall is immune to concentrated Fifth Arc magic, compromised supply, famine, sabotage or a high-level monster. Nera's unique full-force Heaven Splitter is not normally tested against these structures, and the fortifications should not be portrayed as guaranteed to contain him.
**Overlapping aerial denial:** Clifftop repeating scorpions, huge torsion sky-bolts, dragon-sized harpoon cables, anti-flight rune meshes, ward-grounding towers, and signal-trained Ravencrest riders work with wyvern patrols and the limited Skythorn dragons. A dragon's magic-resistant armor defeats routine spells but not a crossfire of large physical bolts, exhausted ward cells or specialist counterspell towers.

### Veyrath family authority
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqawtpl3dm8","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605477,"nanoseconds":12000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684302,"nanoseconds":494000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Imperial Year 738 — hereditary command charter:** Every recognized Veyrath son and daughter commands a real, permanently assigned personal war banner. All five child banners together field **20,000 professional fighters within the pre-existing 75,000–90,000 warhost**, not 20,000 extra soldiers. They have their own sworn officers, garrison sectors, supply ledgers, and field authority. Each banner is a miniature **combined-arms Veyrath army** containing heavy infantry, assault foot, fully armored conventional cavalry, armored monster riders, archers/scouts, combat aether-mages and warders, artillery/siege crews, engineers, **Thorn Paladin superheavy knights, Dreadlance superheavy cavalry, and Skythorn true-dragon heavy knights**. The latter elite types are distributed across the banners as permanently sworn units, **not** kept in a separate corps beyond Nera's reach. Each heir can give ordinary operational orders to every soldier and dragon knight assigned to their banner; a sworn marshal carries out field orders while the heir studies. Children may not seize a sibling's banner or Cassian's central reserve; the duke remains supreme strategic commander for wars, treaties, reallocating house-wide fortresses, and emergency grand musters, subject to the hereditary charter and its recognized banner rights.
**Five child banners (troops already counted within the warhost):**
- **Lucien Veyrath — First Thorn Banner, 6,000:** Assault-oriented combined arms, veteran heavy line infantry, fortress-breach companies, full-plate horse and beast cavalry, aether warders, siege batteries and supply train. Permanently includes **42 Thorn Paladins, 32 Dreadlance Cataphracts, and 6 Skythorn dragon knights (one Fifth Arc)**. His marshal runs the daily campaign schedule while he is a Crownspire fourth-year.
- **Magerrett Veyrath — Silverward Banner, 4,200:** Heavy shield legions, ward-mages, fortress engineers and heavy anti-siege bolt/cannon batteries, reinforced horse/beast cavalry, scouting and supply troops. Permanently includes **28 Thorn Paladins, 22 Dreadlance Cataphracts, and 4 Skythorn dragon knights**. She emphasizes protection and counterspells; her Fourth Arc wardwork informs her command while she studies at Crownspire.
- **Nera Veyrath — Blackwing Banner, 5,000:** **His own professional personal army, with a share of every class of Veyrath soldier and elite—not a household honor guard, no borrowed dragon contingent, and not subordinated to Lucien.** Its counted order of battle is **2,350 Thornwall heavy infantry; 930 Ironthorn assault infantry; 620 Blacksteel heavy horse cavalry; 220 March Beast Riders; 300 Steelwatch scouts, armored archers and mage-hunters; 310 warders, combat aether-mages, sappers and fortress troops; 210 siege/artillery crews and armed supply defenders; 30 Thorn Paladins; 24 Dreadlance Cataphracts; and 6 Skythorn heavy dragon knights (including one Fifth Arc rider)**. **Total: exactly 5,000 fighters**; attached civilian stablehands, metalworkers, dragon keepers and wagon drivers are supported separately. This banner has its own armored aether artillery, ward screens, full heavy barding for horses and monsters, field granaries and assigned fortress sectors. Nera chooses officers, combat doctrine, patrols, logistics and local protective deployments. His sworn Blackwing marshal executes orders in the marches while Nera attends first-year Crownspire; the six dragon knights and all other elites answer to Nera through the banner chain of command, not to Lucien or a remote separate elite commander. The duke can order a general muster but **cannot simply strip away Nera's allotted dragon knights or elites as casual temporary loans**.
- **Taigar Veyrath — Ironroot Banner, 3,000:** Heavy sword-and-shield infantry, pass-holding engineers, assault soldiers, mage support, armored cavalry, monsters and siege trains. Permanently includes **22 Thorn Paladins, 18 Dreadlance Cataphracts, and 3 Skythorn dragon knights**. Senior officers supervise dangerous external deployments while she completes her education.
- **Elin Veyrath — Greenwatch Banner, 1,800:** Fortification garrisons, heavy infantry and reserve assault troops, archers, ward-mages, armored horse/monster riders, supply defense and field siege engineers. Permanently includes **12 Thorn Paladins, 8 Dreadlance Cataphracts, and 1 Skythorn dragon knight**. Elin has a legitimate command seal; owing to her youth, a designated adult regent and senior marshal countersign battle deployments until her majority. Her dragon knight is a sworn banner asset, not a personal mount for an untrained child.
**Elite allocation / no double-counting:** At representative full muster, the warhost maintains **450 Thorn Paladins, 240 Dreadlance Cataphracts, and exactly 30 Skythorn dragon knights**. The five banners together permanently hold **134 Paladins, 104 Cataphracts and 20 dragon knights**. **Cassian's central host** retains the remaining **316 Paladins, 136 Cataphracts and 10 dragon knights**. There are no additional unseen thirty dragon riders or second copies of any elite. Of all 30 dragon riders, **26 are Fourth Arc and 4 are Fifth Arc**: two Fifth Arc among Cassian's ten, one among Lucien's six, and one among Nera's six; the others are Fourth Arc. Each rider remains a sword-master and fully capable battle-mage regardless of banner.
**Authority and succession:** The banners' officers have banner-level loyalty and an overriding house oath; ducal emergency command does not erase their normal owner. The duke controls strategic taxation, cross-border war, and the hidden Black Concordance, which is **not automatically disclosed to his children**. Nera's personal physical strength, instantaneous casting, and perfected Heaven Splitter remain his unique abilities, not typical traits of any of these troops. The arrangement makes alliances and rivalries between Cassian's children politically meaningful without inventing a decided succession war. Ilyra is deceased; Octavia and Sabine are wives, not child-banner commanders.
**New equipment entitlements within old allocations:** Every heir banner, including Nera, fields a small sworn slice of the warhost's **wyvern reconnaissance/interception wing, armored bird-rider scouts, heavy chariot formations, primitive handgonners, siege war machines and anti-air warders**, in addition to the already distributed true dragons and superheavy ground cavalry. These are subtypes of their existing riders/scouts/engineers and do not increase any banner's headcount. Cassian retains the larger central reserves, but cannot reclassify all flying assets as his private unit. Elin's regent still countersigns hazardous missions.

### Varkesh Ironsteppe Armies
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqaxp8psusn","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605481,"nanoseconds":474000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684303,"nanoseconds":290000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Military character:** Eastern iron-and-winter kingdom of tough frontier soldiers and hard-won grain. **Redsteppe Spearguard** (layered mail and long pikes), **Ashfur Bear Riders** (enormous armored steppe-bears and mauls), **Frostmane Heavy Lancers** (fully barded horsemen), **Rimebolt Handgonners** (primitive single-shot guns, often from fortified wagons), **Iron-Sledge Chariots** (bear- or horse-drawn armed sleds, suitable for ice and firm plains), **Storm-Wyvern Riders** (aggressive lesser wyverns rather than true dragons), **Winter Owl Scouts** (night reconnaissance and warning), **Kharzun Great Bombards** and **Ironjaw Siege Engines** (cannon, stone-throwers, heavy bolt batteries). Cold-weather mage specialists strengthen roads and suppress hostile flying wards.
**Strategy and limits:** Excellent endurance in upland winter and strong shock cavalry, though the massive animals consume feed and powder corrodes in snow. Varkesh needs contested grain roads; Urzak Gate, the Redsteppe and Blackgorge are strategic choke points. Hardliners favor border escalation while merchants and reformers are not automatically committed to war.

### Thread: Nera and Lysandra’s sparring
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqbcb44hoy2","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792112472,"nanoseconds":778000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684305,"nanoseconds":719000000}} -->
aliases: ["Nera Lysandra spar","Nera Lysandra practice fight"]
status: closed

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**03/11 practice:** In an aether-only west-court pass, Lysandra saw all eight bolts, stopped/avoided seven and took a clean calf hit: Nera won the pass; she did not personally yield. No beams above the second ring because the east ward was cracked; chalk breach cancels the pass. She learned only of two rescued women and one dead perpetrator. Her other personal answer remains open; a dinner would require Nera to cook.
Updates:
- [T478 · Friday, 21/10/738] Nera asked for a practice spar at the west practice court. Lysandra set blunt-steel terms: first to yield, disarm or step off the square, ending 18:45. The result was not yet shown at turn end.

### Thread: Lysia Solenne’s pre-Academy history
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqc1rl1rdov","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605482,"nanoseconds":240000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684307,"nanoseconds":328000000}} -->
aliases: ["Lysia Solenne burned at pyre","Lysia heresy trial"]
status: closed

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**IY736–737 — Soleris Church pyre and aftermath:** Lysia Solenne, once an idealistic sixteen-year-old consecrated healer, exposed forged persecution and trafficking records. Soleris clergy betrayed her and burned her publicly as a heretic in IY736. She survived through awakened cinderfire, saved civilians afterward, and by IY737 the people of the same region hailed her as their Cinder Saintess despite the hierarchy's complicity. She entered Crownspire in the autumn IY738 medical-track cohort as an eighteen-year-old first-year envoy. Her dark-saintess look and embittered faith already exist when Nera meets her in the infirmary; do not narrate her pyre as a coming first-year crisis.
**Continuity boundary:** All encounters already recorded in the 731–738 childhood and early October 738 Academy timeline remain true. Neither new backstory grants Nera secret knowledge or creates preexisting relationships. Potential investigations into surviving witnesses and clerical conspirators are **future choices**, not guaranteed plot outcomes.

### Thread: Katarina’s private society inquiry
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqcb1cstwel","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792500531,"nanoseconds":494000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684308,"nanoseconds":154000000}} -->

status: open

**Private plan and knowledge:** At Pell’s Cross, Nera identified the grey-coach witness as Katarina to Liora, Vesper and Isolde without explaining how. He proposed gaining society access through a debauched noble persona; Starlia’s document/public reading formed part of that reputation. He forbade the three to tell Starlia or Dara, investigate independently or write Katarina’s name in the day-book. Obedience did not mean agreement. No invitation, successful infiltration or awareness by Katarina is established. The imperial bloodline remains author knowledge, not automatically Nera’s discovery. Recovery comes first.

**Hess and the missing book:** Isolde killed third-pen factor Corvan Hess on Nera’s 31/10 order and returned at 21:52 with his red-stone merchant ring. She staged robbery; the docket book was still in its drawer and she did not take it. Nera showed the ring as proof Hess was dead but withheld the killer’s identity from Dara and Starlia. The watch sealed the pen; by 02/11 the docket book was missing. Nera suspects Katarina without proof. Neither a noble ring nor a recovered employer’s name is established.

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Current state: Group 3 is now building Sylwem’s six-person winter post-road warded shelter within a forty-crown project envelope. The group decided on a joint filing that protects her anchor geometry, with Nera as co-attestor and the hearing early next month. The freeze on spending ended, nineteen crowns of milled work were moved to an Academy-approved bench run, and Nera has successfully cut both anchor sigils, avoiding a separate rune-wright fee. Evelyne holds the budget. This project is underway, not merely proposed.
Updates:

### Thread: Starlia Marin’s rescue and recovery
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqcdhcdhocy","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792500531,"nanoseconds":494000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684307,"nanoseconds":721000000}} -->
aliases: ["Starlia rescue","Nera saves Starlia","Starlia Vharos aftermath"]
status: open

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Rescue and care:** On 30–31/10, Nera and Isolde secretly left the permitted district and found Dara and Starlia at the refuse slope. Isolde’s warning did not negate bond compulsion. Nera funded surgery and ongoing room, physician, food and nursing above the oil press; transport was deferred for medical clearance. Care was extended by a week, renewable on the cutter’s word. Dara, nurse Marta Sedge and washing/lifting helper Perla provide distinct support.

**Correspondence and return:** Nera sent Krail a request for balance/deductions since summer, sought trust release terms rather than payment, and confirmed Vharos care. Posting is not receipt. The rear party left 01/11 at 09:00 and Crownspire closed its roll 03/11 around 16:10; no field-office authority to import persons was yet granted. Isolde’s day-book was to be secured privately.

**03/11 medical conversation:** Lysia received an anonymized account of two injured women and requested the cutter’s complete raw notes, crossed-out concerns, operations, warnings and fever trend. Nera accepted direct updates, prompt deterioration reporting and her clinical authority over travel if she treats the patient. Affection did not waive those terms. She knows neither the women’s names nor the society plan from this account.
Updates:
- [T552 · Monday 31/10/738] alive after surgery at 01:40 on 31/10/738 but critically ill with fever; physician gives a poor chance and wants a dawn check

### Thread: Starlia Marin’s contract and hearing
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqcgtflhiv2","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792500531,"nanoseconds":494000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684307,"nanoseconds":721000000}} -->
aliases: ["Starlia Dara contract","Vesper Starlia contract"]
status: open

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**Agreement:** Nera signed an amended document with Starlia’s name first, lifelong possession/service and intimate-service language, residence/duty control and a no-suicide/self-injury clause in return for treatment, protection and revenge. Starlia had verbally accepted while critically dependent, insisted on exact words, purchase price and public reading before year-end, and rejected Dara’s substitution. **Starlia has not signed; Dara has not witnessed or completed the independent copy; no court date is confirmed.** Copies remain with Dara. A proposed 07/11 appearance was not a booked hearing. Vesper intends to witness Starlia’s own words; Lysia would attend for the patient.

**Objections and money:** Vesper condemned the bargain while acknowledging real care; Liora demanded the contingency if Katarina never takes the bait. Friday 04/11, 19:00 was set for private review of Starlia’s paper first, then wing accounts. Nominal income of 3,000g/month from age fourteen, estimated seventy-odd gold expenses and possible steward offsets do not establish the spendable balance. Blackwing funds are restricted military money. Nera’s personal purse was empty during return travel; Liora lent three silver and Vesper expected handwritten accounting. Proposed prosthetics funding was conditional on actual figures and clinical assessment.
Updates:
- [T550 · Sunday 30/10/738] accepted becoming Nera's personal possession in exchange for treatment and his vengeance; she speaks only in halting fragments

### Thread: Irelia Senka’s pre-Academy history
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqcpn6t1m32","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605482,"nanoseconds":240000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684307,"nanoseconds":328000000}} -->
aliases: ["Irelia Senka temple defense","Irelia rescue eastern terraces"]
status: closed

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
**IY735 — Saryuun eastern terraces:** Before she enrolled at Crownspire, Irelia Senka defended a river-temple evacuation against trafficker-backed raiders, then entered the IY736 Crownspire class at eighteen. As of October 738 she is a twenty-year-old third-year senior. This is a past Eastern incident, not a future Academy excursion, battle or already played meeting with Nera.
**Continuity boundary:** All encounters already recorded in the 731–738 childhood and early October 738 Academy timeline remain true. Neither new backstory grants Nera secret knowledge or creates preexisting relationships. Potential investigations into surviving witnesses and clerical conspirators are **future choices**, not guaranteed plot outcomes.

**Latest financial state, 04/11/738:** Personal deposits of 145,854g are accessible; 10,000g committed for Starlia’s limbs, 500g initially authorized with controlled milestone releases. The exact records are in Nera’s history and the money-related World facts. Lysandra’s tower hour is 15:00. Master Vell and all eight location cards from the source export are retained.

**Current continuation: Friday 04/11/738, through T678.** Asterfen and Vharos are completed. Starlia remains under care in Vharos; her signature and any public hearing are unconfirmed. Trust deed/trustee discoveries and the copied deposit book are established in World facts. Later checkpoints override the older October snapshot below; do not turn that snapshot into the current scene. Nera’s pre-Academy two-year Eastern campaign is established history.



**IY731, childhood:** Nera’s mother Ilyra is already dead when Liora’s accidental lamp fire burns her last portrait. Nera refuses violent punishment and brings Elise and Liora into his wing. He obtains Vesper from Greyford, later affirms her freedom, and begins ancient-glyph and Heaven Splitter training with Aldissa Vane. Cassian’s debt scheme brings Lord Arvayne, the sealed box Nera did not know the contents of, Lord Arvayne’s subsequent death and Celestine’s creditor-court wardship into Nera’s life. Nera meets Katarina at an imperial reception.

**Winter IY735:** At Nine Hollows Nera discovers wage abuse, falsified deaths and exploitation. Their party is ambushed; Liora’s protective soul-fire awakens. Some immediate abuses are corrected, but broader Veyrath responsibility persists.

**Summer IY738:** Nera, Isolde, Vesper and Liora reach eighteen. Isolde receives legal personhood and wages despite her unremovable bond. Vesper confides the **nine-tail secret only to Nera**; all others continue to see one magically concealed tail. Liora’s compulsory childhood term expires; she remains voluntarily. Nera has perfected Heaven Splitter and has immense concealed physical power.

**October IY738 — admission:** Academy Class A ranks Alaric #1, Nera #2, Sylwem #3, Rhea #4, Lysandra #5, Evelyne #6 and Liora #7 (Ilse #9); Vesper and Isolde enter Class B. Nera defeats Rhea and Aurelia in entrance combat. Alaric reveals that Veyrath men injured his father; he cooperates but expects Nera to prove he is different. Group 3 forms with Nera, Alaric, Sylwem, Rhea, Liora and Evelyne; the six-person shelter project advances through design, budget, sigils and a coming review.

**October IY738 — played relationships and bouts:** Adult Liora and Vesper each independently agree to be Nera’s girlfriend. Neither is property or a veto-holder; no monogamy demand is canon. Sylwem’s paid practice bout with Nera ends when his deliberately restrained Heaven Splitter breaks the platform; the board fee is accounted for and her personal question cancelled. Nera beats Garen 2–1 in a fair west-ring spar and receives his handshake. Vayne Kettwald challenges Nera to a sabre bout, but fifth bell ends the session before their fight; she still wants that future contest.

**Most recent live matters, October IY738:** Sylwem’s shelter ward has Nera’s name on the Registry sheet; assessors may question his ability to cut its non-imperial anchor. The Group 3 filing and near-term Academy excursion remain pending. Vesper’s registry identifies her as a household acquisition; after an evasive initial answer, Nera tells her directly he will not sign her away for convenience, will personally hear any clerk and decide, and will repeat this at first bell. All unplayed later school/war/excursion arcs remain prospective, not already resolved.
**Restored historical turn index — IY 731 through early IY 738 (from 10 October archive):** The newer sections above and the later dated outcomes below control when snapshots differ.

### Thread: Vharos Academy excursion
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqfewv5gid8","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792112472,"nanoseconds":778000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684313,"nanoseconds":534000000}} -->
aliases: ["Vharos excursion","Vharos excursion dates","Vharos excursion costs","Vharos school trip","Vharos travel papers","Nera Vharos itinerary","Vharos return journey"]
status: closed

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"user","kind":"canon","cutoff":null} -->
Planned November first-year Vharos excursion. As of Friday 21/10/738, the public notice has not yet been posted and Nera has not yet paid for applications. Planned details retained from the earlier notice draft: departure the week after Founders' Festival closes; four days on the road/in Vharos and return on the fifth day; expected fee of two crowns six silver; thirty-six first-year seats; bursary by need closing four days before the general window; class-tutor countersignature required; the office assigns seats; lodging margin notes are not honored; Asterfen rosters do not carry over.
Updates:
- [T472 · October 738] the plan says November, but the excursion notice was not yet posted as of Friday 21/10/738.

- [04/11/738] **Later confirmed:** Departed Friday 28/10, arrived Saturday 29/10 at 18:20. Sunday 30/10 after the mandatory tour was Liora’s fulfilled market/private-time choice. Monday 31/10 10:00–14:00 was Isolde’s independent registry window (left 13:35); Vesper chose the remaining afternoon/evening. Tuesday 01/11 was the road return, not a free city day. The authorized 09:00 rear baggage party rejoined the main column; its 2g 6s voucher counterfoil is at the Academy office (T647, 04/11). Crownspire roll actually closed on 03/11 about 16:10. Admission of southern persons/purchases still needs field-office authorization. Old November-only notices and promises of four free city days are superseded.

### Thread: Vesper’s Lan Hsin identity
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqfqsbw0cfs","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1791605482,"nanoseconds":844000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684313,"nanoseconds":534000000}} -->
aliases: ["Lan Hsin","Vesper nine tails","Vesper true name","Nera knows nine tail secret","foxkin Vesper identity"]
status: open

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
Vesper is publicly known as an ordinary one-tailed foxkin called Vesper Nox and has a concealed eastern identity as Lan Hsin. **Her nine tails are innate, magically hidden, and known only to Nera.** No one else, even close companions, saw them at Nine Hollows or knows their true number. This secret remains open and protected.
Updates:
- [T161 · summer 738] Vesper tells Nera that discovery could get her killed and asks that nobody outside the wing ever count past one.
<!-- nera-line: {"sourceRevision":0,"text":"Vesper tells Nera that discovery could get her killed and asks that nobody outside the wing ever count past one.","src":null,"when":"summer 738","by":"import","turn":161,"id":"ln_mvjpvwqfst9odmgb","evidence":[]} -->
- [T213 · October 738] Crownspire still knows her through the Vesper Nox identity and ordinary foxkin presentation.
<!-- nera-line: {"by":"import","evidence":[],"src":null,"id":"ln_mvjpvwqfex928tl6","turn":213,"sourceRevision":0,"when":"October 738","at":1791384108161,"text":"Crownspire still knows her through the Vesper Nox identity and ordinary foxkin presentation."} -->

### Thread: Founders’ Reception 731
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqft5g9s5tn","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792679453,"nanoseconds":0},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684315,"nanoseconds":132000000}} -->
aliases: ["IY 731 Founders Reception","Founders Reception 731","Nera Lysandra 731","Nera and Lysandra at royal party","Nera Katarina royal reception","Nera at imperial reception","Founders' Reception"]
status: closed

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Completed historical scene — winter IY 731; Nera was eleven.** This is an event account, not a fresh quest or invitation. Source cross-check: the October 10 turn index, *Asterfall_Summary_Through_T440*, and the October 22 Celestine/Katarina threads. Details below are summaries, **not verbatim original dialogue**.

**Founders’ Reception (T107–T120):** Nera attended with Liora, Vesper and Isolde. Princess Lysandra **observed** him at T107; the available old records do not establish a significant direct exchange with her at this reception. Nera spoke publicly about the eastern marches and what House Veyrath received for imperial service. The Emperor tested him, marked him **“Attended,”** and drew attention to his maternal Ilyra lineage; this was political notice, **not a proven grant or favor**.

**Knowledge boundary:** Nera’s private foreknowledge and later explanation to Vesper are **not automatically known to Celestine, Katarina, Lysandra or the general public**. Do not invent unrecorded conversations between Nera and Lysandra or treat the assassination mechanism as proven.

### Thread: Arvayne sealed box and wardship 731
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqg1atmqzex","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","createdAt":{"seconds":1792679453,"nanoseconds":0},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684316,"nanoseconds":467000000}} -->
aliases: ["Lord Arvayne sealed cordial box"]
status: closed

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Before the reception:** Harl & Merrow’s penalty structure trapped indebted House Arvayne; the fourth note passed through Cassian Veyrath. At T90–T94 Celestine privately questioned Nera about her future. He expressed intent but admitted he could not dictate the outcome at eleven. Cassian’s creditor leverage was his own scheme, not a financial deal designed by Nera.

**Lord Arvayne (T111):** Nera delivered a sealed apothecary cordial box connected to Cassian’s planned assassination. He knew his father intended to kill Arvayne, but **did not know the contents or confirmed murder method**. He warned Arvayne that death approached so the father could spend his remaining time with Celestine. The paper trail linked Nera to the delivery. At T368 in October 738, Nera told Vesper the box was a **symbol**; its physical role in the death remains unproven.

**Afterward (T149–T155):** Four days after the box delivery, Lord Arvayne died, officially of a winter cough; the exact means are **not conclusively established**. The creditor court assigned Celestine to the holder of the largest note, Cassian’s House Veyrath. She arrived with one chest. Nera did not personally engineer the legal transfer. He owed her an honest answer about her father when she turned eighteen, and she required future promises in his own handwriting. This obligation cannot be erased by treating her wardship as charity.

**Knowledge boundary:** Nera’s private foreknowledge and later explanation to Vesper are **not automatically known to Celestine, Katarina, Lysandra or the general public**. Do not invent unrecorded conversations between Nera and Lysandra or treat the assassination mechanism as proven.

**NOT PROMOTED TO CANON.** This entry preserves useful information from *Nera_story_summary_15k_tokens.md*, a narrative summary that extends through 06/11/738, whereas the user-supplied 22 October Events export explicitly ends **04/11/738 at T678**. There are no corresponding turn-index entries in the supplied Events file. Do not advance the live scene, count promises as completed, or change current duties from this draft until the chronology is confirmed.

**05/11, summary account:** A Vharos physician’s packet said Starlia’s fever had fallen but not broken and the left-arm stump infection was still concerning; no survival promise or prosthetic authorization. Nera requested the full unedited report for Lysia and proper sealed custody for Starlia’s reserved funds. Liora and Nera visited the old wall, bakery and boatyard. Vesper led Nera to the river winch gate, lunch and Tallow’s glass workshop; her diagnosis of a faulty lead seam earned an invitation to observe a reset on Monday 07/11, not an apprenticeship. At the Academy gate, she accepted a freely chosen kiss. A proposed Tuesday 08/11 22:00 room visit remained limited to an hour, conditional on her energy and consent.

**06/11, summary account:** Elise received Nera’s signed authority to seek itemized statements from seven unpaid suppliers with Marta witnessing, not to seize private merchant ledgers. Fleurdelys corrected Nera’s claim that they had a missed “date”: she had proposed a harder drill, not a date. Her next supervised return-blade fault exercise was proposed for Tuesday 08/11 07:00 with no audience. Liora and Nera visited the north gallery with the green pattern book. In Lysandra’s tower pass, she used ice pillars and staggered spears; Nera landed the decisive blunt hit after a real tactical setback, while his earlier claim of using full power was admitted false. A Friday 11/11 19:30 dinner was accepted in that summary **as an invitation, not a wager, prize, or promised romance**.

**Review required:** Confirm the actual post-T678 RP turns, all timings, Starlia’s clinical course and money disbursements before switching this card out of draft. Earlier user-supplied Events entries keep their current statuses.

## Plan

### Fixed Past and Current Continuity — Preserve, Don't Retell
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqh26m97dr6","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"30/09/741","phases":[],"status":"pending","start":"01/09/738"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027219,"nanoseconds":300000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684323,"nanoseconds":482000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
- **IY731–738:** Ilyra has already died before the original lamp fire. Elise and Liora enter Nera's wing; Vesper's childhood acquisition and eventual freedom, Celestine's Arvayne wardship and sealed-box tragedy, Nine Hollows, the development of Nera's strength, Blackwing command, Isolde's certification, and all seven years of relationships and training remain exactly as lived.
- **October IY738:** Nera is a first-year **Class A #2** behind Alaric #1. Sylwem #3, Rhea #4, Lysandra #5, Evelyne #6, Liora #7 and Ilse #9 retain their positions; Vesper and Isolde are Class B. Group 3's charter workshop consists of **Nera, Alaric, Sylwem, Rhea, Liora and Evelyne**. The existing ward, shelter, credit, review-board and filing matters persist. Nobody is secretly added to the group.
- **Current continuation:** The **Asterfen field exercise and its specific team scenes** have already occurred in the live story. Continue from the actual post-excursion situation, including Nera's recent conversations, classmates and any pending requests to join *future* excursions. Do not restage the ridge watch, fabricate another Asterfen outcome, or turn a completed conversation into an unresolved written note. The existing Founders' Festival notice and future Vharos/Miravel/Tideglass school trips remain relevant unless the live story has already modified their schedule.

### Starlia Arc — The Princess's Collection
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqh6kms3c6e","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"status":"pending","start":"04/11/738","end":"24/01/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792500534,"nanoseconds":116000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684326,"nanoseconds":474000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Function:** The first major personal horror arc after the played Vharos rescue. Nera's investigation or infiltration exposes a royal society whose pleasures are sustained by ordinary Imperial privilege. Starlia remains a powerless survivor with a name, preferences, anger, dependence and relationships. She is never assigned an awakening, forensic talent, required romance, new captivity or compulsory act of revenge.

**Current continuity:** Her rescue, surgery and the first payment for care have happened. Continue from the latest actual medical, financial and contractual state.
