Hess was identified in play as a buying factor and is dead; his role is not interchangeable with the separate sadistic holder. The third-pen docket is missing and its taker unproved. Nera identified the grey-coach woman as Katarina and told Isolde, Vesper and Liora. Starlia and Dara do not acquire that identification without disclosure. Nera's disguise is an intention; invitation, attendance, deception and subsequent actions remain the user's choices.

**Hidden author truth:** Katarina leads the adult heirs' society and arranged her unrecognized paternal half-sister's destruction through its contacts. The buyers need not know the dynastic purpose. This truth does not grant anyone in-world proof or knowledge. The Emperor's prior awareness of Starlia remains unsettled until established.

**The society's particular horror:** Its members cooperate in deliberate psychological destruction, sexual violence against adult captives, abusive medicine, living collections, blood ceremonies, human sacrifice and grand acts such as the destruction of settlements for captives. They also dine, fall in love, fund scholarships and protect their peers. Many outsiders accept the hierarchy that makes this possible. Keep intimate violence non-graphic and show victims as people through their lives and aftermath.

**Dramatic spine:** Follow an acquisition that the world considers settled, meet the admired people behind it, recognize a collective atrocity as a celebrated gift, discover people maintained for private rites, and face a royal family concerned with power and pedigree. Evidence guides access, choices and leverage. The act's major scenes take place with people and in consequential places, rather than requiring repeated visits to clerks.

#### Phase: Care, the missing docket and a commissioning household
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"starlia-plan-aftercare","start":"04/11/738","end":"08/11/738","status":"pending"} -->
Use Y1-04. Give Starlia and Dara ordinary private scenes and actual medical choices. Two credible clues can identify a commissioning household and an unusual winter procurement. Nera may pursue them, delegate a limited task, or leave them alone. Existing concerns about his agreement and covert plan are followed from their actual state, not replayed as the same argument.

#### Phase: A household's wedding gift
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"starlia-plan-kestrel","start":"10/11/738","end":"16/11/738","status":"pending"} -->
Use Y1-05. Local warning, travel and protective decisions determine whether the commissioned raid proceeds. The victims' fates are not fixed to guarantee a later spectacle. A prevented atrocity reduces the circle's later capacity.

#### Phase: The admired patrons
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"starlia-plan-festival","start":"20/11/738","end":"23/11/738","status":"pending"} -->
Use Y1-06. A genuine social introduction and an ordinary exercise of household ownership offer the first close encounter with the circle. Malfoid, Lysandra, Cedric, Sylwem or Fleurdelys can become involved through their existing roles and actual attendance, not a compulsory ensemble.

#### Phase: Private company and unequal protection
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"starlia-plan-innercompany","start":"24/11/738","end":"12/12/738","status":"pending"} -->
If Nera accepts or seeks access, show a small dinner, a medical patron's visit or a household transfer before any grand ceremony. Establish at least one recurring adult dependent through an ordinary conversation and wish. Public charity and deliberate harm share the same sponsors. The society can regard Nera's expensive treatment of Starlia as possessive indulgence if the relevant information reaches it. Nothing requires him to harm someone to maintain a disguise. Keep the Winter Banner League and ordinary classes playable.

#### Phase: The Gallery of Living Saints
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"starlia-plan-wintergallery","start":"15/12/738","end":"29/12/738","status":"pending"} -->
Use Y1-09. The household celebration, research collection and proposed 22 December rite create a real opportunity for intervention with multiple concrete objectives. Earlier protection, reconnaissance, cancelled supplies, absence or direct force change what happens. Katarina can lose here; the outline provides no plot armor.

#### Phase: Royal blood and the people left behind
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"starlia-plan-royalaftermath","start":"10/01/739","end":"24/01/739","status":"pending"} -->
Use Y1-10. Address the actual aftermath, Starlia's requested reading, any earned parentage discovery and the corruption of royal settlement. An important faction can defend imperial blood while accepting the remaining slave trade. Starlia chooses her response to recognition; Nera chooses his commitments and next acts.

**Checkpoint:** Record Starlia's health, residence, carer arrangements, money, signature status, wishes and relationship with Dara; living captives protected or still held; the roles and fate of individual perpetrators; the docket's real status; each person's knowledge of the grey coach, society and bloodline; Katarina's actual resources; the royal family's concrete actions; property and armed consequences; and Nera's explicit orders. Do not replace these results with an assumed spring reset.

### Plan-Wide Purpose and Priority
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqh6q2r4yq6","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"30/09/741","status":"pending","phases":[],"start":"01/09/738"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027219,"nanoseconds":300000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684326,"nanoseconds":861000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
This is a **narrator-facing event plan**, not a summary of events already played and not a script for Nera. It replaces the previous long-term anchor outline **for the first three Academy years, adapted to the latest played checkpoint**. Everything already played, including childhood (IY731–738), admission, classes and rankings, Group 3's actual work, relationships, and the Asterfen excursion, outranks this plan. An outline may propose an opportunity; it may never retroactively declare that Nera attended, accepted, spoke, fought, loved, forgave, or won.

**New pacing:** The main trafficking, institutional corruption, foreign diplomacy, Black Concordance, succession and eastern war story progresses across **Year One (738–739), Year Two (739–740), and Year Three (740–741)**. Year One receives the most playable detail and ordinary Academy life. **Year Four (741–742) is deliberately EMPTY of prescribed plot anchors**, reserved for a later separate plan. The existing **four-year Crownspire enrollment remains intact**: solving the principal story in Year Three is not automatic graduation, an accelerated diploma, or a skipped fourth year.

**Key change:** By late Year Two or the Year Two–Three summer recess, offer Nera a credible **personal return to his own Blackwing Banner and an actual organized battlefield operation**. This is not an observer-only mock exercise. Armed frontier clashes involving Blackwing forces are a real developing situation; Nera receives a genuine chance to lead soldiers under the hereditary charter, with battlefield scenes if he chooses to go. His participation, deployment plan, orders, exposure of secret abilities, and the battle's result remain his decisions and live consequences. Do not have another character automatically accept the assignment on his behalf.

**Starlia arc integration:** The winter Starlia/Katarina act below is a major Year One thread. Its actual result changes later patrons, surviving suppliers and royal relationships. Subsequent investigation anchors use those surviving causes and do not require Katarina or a dismantled society to reappear. The first act can end in a local victory while the Empire's slave economy and corruption persist.

### Narrative Pacing, Continuity and Adaptation Checklist
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqh8yyiyhsk","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","start":"01/09/738","phases":[],"end":"30/09/741"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027265,"nanoseconds":502000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684326,"nanoseconds":474000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
- **At least two clues before a major consequence:** A transport invoice precedes a missing-person inquiry; a troop roster and grain demand precede a Blackgorge engagement; a legal draft precedes the Concordance emergency-power vote. Show institutional reactions and elapsed travel time.
- **After every major arc, play the aftermath.** A day's training, a strained conversation, music after dinner, medical follow-up, cleaning gear, a letter from home or a student festival can be more revealing than another crisis. Give Academy friends time to change at ordinary human speed.
- **Never stack all relationships into a compulsory choice scene.** Nera and the other adults may pursue consensual nonexclusive relationships, with genuine individual agency, without repeated artificial tests demanding exclusivity or a 'first wife' authority.
- **Track evidence and knowledge individually.** Different officials see different ledgers; an enemy officer does not know a secret because the narrator knows it. Technical inspections, public disclosures and private conversations establish knowledge only when played.
- **Track military supply and legal orders.** Record exact banner casualties and reinforcements (no automatic extra 5,000), siege ammunition, dragon injuries, roads open or closed, authorized command boundaries and actual terms of ceasefires. Heavy units cannot fly above every road or ignore winter feed.
- **Do not reset achieved outcomes.** Earlier prevention reduces harm. Discovered documents are not lost and rediscovered without cause. An acquitted person is not automatically guilty again, and a dismantled Concordance component stays gone.
- **No predestined deaths, forced promotions or obligatory war victory.** The planned late-Year-Two Blackwing battlefield is an authentic operational **opportunity** in a genuine localized war; Nera's choice to attend and the result are never narrated for him. Wider war and final peace follow causes and decisions.
- **Play travel and breaks.** Vharos late autumn, Miravel late winter/early spring, Crownward Leviathan via Gullhaven to Tideglass in spring; Year One, Year Two and Year Three each end with freely chosen time, not a silent skip.
- **Keep the final state portable.** At summer 741, preserve a clear checkpoint for Year Four. The new document ends there.

**Compressed main-arc route:**

**Completed childhood and October 738 Academy history** → **Asterfen debrief and Group 3 review** → **Vharos excursion** → **Starlia's aftermath and the commissioning household** → **Kestrel Hollow's threatened wedding-gift raid** → **Founders' Festival and Katarina's admired patrons** → **private salons alongside the separate carriage case and Winter Banner League** → **Gallery of Living Saints and the actual winter confrontation** → **royal corruption, earned bloodline discovery and survivors' aftermath** → **Miravel tower visit** → **Spring Exchange Fortnight** → **Crownward Leviathan / Tideglass voyage** → **Blackgorge border clash** → **Crown Trials and free first summer** → **Taigar's intake / council election / scholarships and Cinder Archive** → **Winterglass Ball** → **Vaelcross civic placement and Market of Oaths** → **Interacademy Concord Games / South Causeway danger** → **Campaign Examination and eastern muster** → **PLAYABLE BLACKWING WAR CAMPAIGN AT THE SECOND SUMMER BREAK** → **Year Three return and provincial practicum** → **petitions, Soleris convocation and Elaranth treaty** → **Vaelcross grain pressure and Concordance evidence** → **Thornhold succession assembly** → **Three Bridges diplomacy** → **Blackgorge campaign or earned ceasefire enforcement** → **Concordance decision, survivors and settlement** → **Year Three Grand Command Melee** → **freely chosen summer 741** → **YEAR FOUR: UNPLANNED**.

**Remember:** The world brings the situations. The player determines Nera's actions. The other characters determine their own responses. The earned outcome remains true.

### Starlia Arc — Connections to Core and Major Characters
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhaoe7by0g","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"start":"04/11/738","status":"pending","end":"24/01/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792500534,"nanoseconds":116000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684327,"nanoseconds":256000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Core focus — Nera and Starlia:** Nera drives possible exposure and possesses the rank and force that could secure access. His complicating questions concern ownership, protection, personal desire and methods, without preselecting his answer. Starlia's identity cannot be reduced to evidence against a princess. She may want a quiet day, a particular person protected, an explanation or revenge, and she need not earn care through usefulness.

**Isolde, Core:** Her current personhood and unremovable bond give the society's vocabulary personal force. Her narrow magic, stealth and physical expertise can support a concrete task if commanded or chosen within her actual circumstances. A household can recognize her certificate yet address Nera about her, exposing unequal recognition. Preserve her chosen loyalty, opinions and distinction between compulsion and consent. The society does not gain a control key to her bond.

**Vesper, Core:** Her old acquisition label and foxkin appearance can shape how a patron addresses Nera in her presence. Her public presentation remains one tail; Lan Hsin and the nine-tail secret stay separately concealed. She can read a specific ward seam or choose to assist an exit, but is neither an infallible lie detector nor an automatically volunteered lure. Preserve her independent relationship rather than using the case to manufacture jealousy.

**Liora, Core:** Her devotion, possessiveness and willingness to confront threats can produce active protective plans, sharp judgments or support for force. She also has history as a sentenced commoner and a mother whose labor funded her education. Her views need not match Vesper's or Lysia's. Keep her private soul-fire concealed unless actual use or disclosure reveals it. She has an ordinary life and relationship alongside the case.

**Lysia, Core:** Medical care puts her in contact with injuries and deliberately induced illness, giving her a professional line into the circle's charitable face. She can identify what a treatment does within her competence and defend a particular patient's care. Her IY736 pyre remains completed history; the society is not retroactively made its architect. Her faith, anger, medical commitments and connection with Nera can pull in different directions. No mandatory romance or oath of obedience follows cooperation.

**Cedric, Core:** His polished pragmatism now operates within the user's established royal corruption. He benefits from patronage and the slave economy and may actively protect useful families, narrow an inquiry or offer settlement when events reach him. His affection for his sisters and regard for Nera can coexist with those choices. He is neither automatically an inner-circle participant nor the secret organizer of every crime. His Arc 5 command wards do not make him physically equal to Nera.

**Lysandra, Core:** Her belief that institutions ought to uphold justice brings a specific personal conflict when her siblings or father protect a known perpetrator. Let her learn through credible evidence and choose an actual limited action. Protecting one person under her title can reveal both the usefulness and inequality of royal privilege. She does not know Starlia is her half-sister in advance and is not an obligatory victim or traitor.

**Sylwem, Core:** A treaty-protected royal hostage and ordinary elven captives can share a room while being assigned very different value. Her commitments to Elaranth constrain public promises; she can independently seek news, protect someone within her reach, negotiate a narrow return or refuse a politically impossible demand. Her Group 3 authorship, closed personal thread and limited knowledge of Nera continue.

**Fleurdelys, Core:** Her lake-court compact and dislike of being displayed as a symbol give her an individual response to living collections and the purchase of exceptional people. She can pursue a limited protection through her own contacts or offer chosen assistance after actual acquaintance. Her current Arc 2 and trained body retain their limits. She neither becomes Starlia's miraculous cure nor shares Sylwem's royal treaty position.

**Malfoid, Core:** Her aristocratic fluency offers genuine social access and a sharp view of whose insult matters. She can normalize ownership, distinguish a peer's dignity from a servant's, or revise a particular judgment after seeing its consequences. A royal-lineage discovery can expose rather than instantly cure her bloodline assumptions. Her choices can strengthen, strain or redirect her relationship with Nera; neither secret membership nor instant humanitarian conversion is assigned.

**Katarina, Major antagonist:** Her power here comes from leadership, indulgence, calculation, royal protection and willing cooperation. She is responsible for her commission against Starlia. Attraction, affection or a personal conversation with Nera cannot erase that responsibility. She learns and counters through actual agents and evidence. Her eventual fate follows live action.

**Dara, essential supporting relationship:** Her importance to Starlia is practical and personal, regardless of roster rank. She can refuse access, correct assumptions and make independent requests within her circumstances. Her own injury and care workload matter. She is not a disposable hostage introduced to prove how dangerous Katarina is.

**Celestine, Major:** A specific creditor instrument, family account or attempted claim can echo the machinery that placed her under Veyrath guardianship. Her expertise can produce a useful narrow distinction without making her an omniscient analyst. Her father's history and Nera's unanswered promise remain separate unless someone actually addresses them.

**Magerrett and Alaric, Major:** Magerrett can offer family counsel, an introduction or limited protection consistent with her interests and charter; Silverward does not become Nera's private capital police. Alaric can challenge noble impunity through what he actually knows and decide whether any cooperation is warranted; the case does not erase Veyrath's harm to his family.

Use these connections across separate scenes and ordinary school days. No compulsory investigation squad, shared revelation, relationship tribunal or automatic companion roster is created.

### Y1-02 — Vharos Excursion: The Grand Black Amphitheater
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhc7jftppw","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"31/10/738","start":"28/10/738","phases":[],"status":"completed"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027224,"nanoseconds":478000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684320,"nanoseconds":637000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Continuity update:** This excursion has already occurred in live play, including Starlia's rescue and the group's actual return. Its original planned dates and opportunities below are reference material. Continue from the recorded aftermath rather than staging another arrival, rescue or excursion.

**Length:** 4 days

**Type:** First major off-campus city arc; participation optional.

Crownspire offers a faculty-escorted trip **south along the Imperial road toward Solenne** to **Vharos, the City of Chains and Arenas**. This city's established landmark is the **Grand Black Amphitheater** amid the ochre-red basin—not a renamed tower or an unexplained overseas port. Applications specify the dates, price, capacity, escorts, conduct rules, medical cover and alternative coursework for students who stay behind. A school trip does **not** force first-years into lethal gladiatorial bouts.

Play Vharos in scenes rather than one summary. On arrival: red dust, sun disks, baths and crowded weapon stalls; later: the lawful galleries where cheering nobles watch skilled professionals, condemned criminals, debt-bound fighters and dangerous beasts. On another day: a demonstration by a living local champion, armor testing, a physician's work, or ethical debate over whether a signed arena contract can be voluntary under threat of hunger. Nera's incredible power does not automatically make him an official arena contestant or cancel public law.

An **optional** lead may surface a contracted barracks laborer, creditor court, surgical ledger or arena supply wagon. Because this excursion now occurs before the later missing-supply-roll inquiry, do **not** declare the Vharos record to be connected to a missing Crownspire worker yet. If Y1-04 later produces a matching name, date, route, seal or other credible record, the earlier Vharos material may become corroborating evidence. A local reformer may have other priorities than the Academy; a celebrated voluntary champion need not secretly be enslaved. Some inhabitants adore the arena, others survive by it, and others organize genuine reform. Make room for enjoyable markets, a bathhouse, a comical souvenir search, food, music, and classmates arguing about the best fighter.

**Open play:** observe, meet a professional, challenge someone in a separate voluntary **warded exhibition** if approved, enjoy the city, attend a hearing, pursue a lead, criticize the arena or decline the trip. Return travel consumes time; schoolwork and news continued in Vaelcross meanwhile.

### Y1-09 — The Winter Guests: The Gallery of Living Saints
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhd6wws94g","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"start":"15/12/738","status":"pending","end":"29/12/738"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027229,"nanoseconds":446000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684326,"nanoseconds":861000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** The central horror and confrontation opportunity of Starlia's first act.

A winter celebration at a private estate in the central Imperial basin gathers adult heirs from Katarina's circle. Invitations and an announced household celebration precede the gathering. Its proposed ceremonial evening is 22 December, subject to real preparation, disruptions and earlier intervention. Actual road travel and any Academy leave are arranged in play.

**Public face:** The hosts boast of patronage for medical research and the preservation of rare gifts. A room of adult dependents is presented as a collection of living saints, prized talents and beneficiaries. A physician treats deliberately induced illness as a professional research result. One captive healer is publicly credited with the owner's generosity while privately exhausted by compelled service. A remembered festival performer or independently introduced adult can appear here through a traceable transfer.

**Private purpose:** Members seek terror and the destruction of identity as ends in themselves. Accounts of sexual violence and forced witnessing can identify a particular perpetrator and surviving family without requiring reenactment. Some guests observe blood rites or sacrificial and cannibalistic ceremonies. Rare attempts to preserve youth or bind awareness require the specialists and materials actually present; an aristocrat's claim of a miracle is not proof of success. These practices neither require a demon's command nor excuse the humans who commission them.

**The wedding gift:** If the Kestrel commission succeeded, some of its surviving captives have reached the estate after a recorded journey. The recipients regard them as a commemorative gift. If earlier action intercepted or protected them, those people remain safe and the celebration is materially reduced, disrupted or cancelled. Established captives are tracked individually; there is no replacement village atrocity.

**Open entry points:** A voluntary invitation to Nera, an actual medical referral, an independently justified household visit, a worker seeking help, surveillance from lawful access, or direct intervention after credible reconnaissance. No Core character is forced to attend, disguise herself as available property, or suffer a new assault to establish villainy. Their actual abilities can help if they independently agree.

**Live objectives:** Stop a specific rite, protect a living person, find the surviving commissioning agent or holder, secure a route out, learn Katarina's role, or choose a different goal. Nera can use real force; ordinary nobles and guards cannot become physical equals to him. The difficulty can lie in separated captives, unfamiliar terrain, allies' limits and what happens afterward. Avoid turning bodies and hostages into a perpetual excuse that nullifies successful preparation.

Katarina responds to the information and access she actually has. The rite can be stopped, the estate seized, an enemy killed, captives protected, a bargain accepted or the opportunity missed. Neither her survival nor any named person's death is guaranteed. A destroyed operation stays destroyed.

### Y1-08 — Winter Banner League: Five Ways to Win
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhg5vosoup","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","phases":[],"end":"12/12/738","start":"05/12/738"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027229,"nanoseconds":446000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684326,"nanoseconds":40000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** High-energy student competition and formation of rivals.

Teams contest warded escort routes, capture-and-defend objectives, tactical puzzles, relay rescues and controlled battles. Classes **A–F** can contribute different specialties; the fastest swordswoman need not know how to evacuate a simulated village, and Nera must not be scripted into command of every team. A good opponent may try to win by timing, cunning or legal point rules, not by magically becoming stronger than Nera's true body.

Introduce opportunities for **Riven, Artoria, Mordred, Garen, Vayne, Skadi, Fran, Okita, Alaric, Rhea, Malfoid** or other classmates to show their own distinctive strengths without necessarily fighting Nera. In side contests, **Jeanne**, **Ilse**, **Dorothy**, **Senku**, **Ningguang**, **Amiya** or **Yinlin** can distinguish themselves. Older students judge techniques but do not award prestige automatically to their siblings or political allies.

**Open play:** training partnerships, creative tactics, disagreements over fair play, a voluntary exhibition, a surprising low-ranked team, an injury handled responsibly, celebration and school gossip. Placement and prizes follow actual performance. The league creates new contacts who can matter in Year Two diplomacy and real campaigning.

### Y1-13 — Spring Exchange Fortnight: People Who May Later Stand Opposed
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhgkd2qq33","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"18/03/739","phases":[],"end":"31/03/739","status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027231,"nanoseconds":985000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684326,"nanoseconds":40000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Foreign visitors, friendships and arguments that have future consequences.

Formal **Elaranth, Solenne and Varkesh** delegations visit the already fortified Crownspire plateau. They arrive as teachers, performers, students, religious envoys, military observers and trade officials with diplomatic rules and their own limits. **Sylwem** and **Lysia** are existing students, not new visiting strangers; **Lysandra** is an Imperial princess with obligations, not an automatic host for everyone. Additional speakers or observers from **Orsenn, Kharad, Saryuun or Umbrath** may attend only through appropriate charters, not by making all nations one convenient allied delegation.

The fortnight can include archery, ward craft, medicines, language exchanges, a debate over frontier maps, a musical recital, interschool duels and dances. **Kujou Sara, Amiya, Irelia, Musashi, Skadi, Zima, Jeanne, Artoria** or other enrolled international students have independent views shaped by their actual origins. Foreign friends need not renounce their homelands; Varkesh students must not suddenly become enemy spies. Some guests may view Vharos with horror while others argue that their own institutions are hardly innocent.

A concrete policy seed concerns **Elaranth ward-timber export terms**, access through western forest crossings, **Varkesh grain transit east of Blackgorge**, and **Solenne's contradictory systems of sanctuary and service oaths**. Nothing is settled simply because Nera is popular. A polite exchange can end in a contact, a trade memorandum, an unresolved question or just a photograph in the student newspaper.

### Y1-07 — The Carriage That Never Passed the Checkpoint
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhgmpbiv6b","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"30/11/738","phases":[],"start":"24/11/738"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027226,"nanoseconds":998000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684323,"nanoseconds":62000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** A separate Academy-connected danger that may intersect a known supply route.

A properly registered provincial academy carriage fails to reach a relay station on the southern Imperial causeway serving Vharos and the Solenne corridor. Its actual passengers, losses and fate follow preparation, adversary action and any protective decisions. A sealed transit order and incompatible passenger lists can expose a particular intermediary.

Do not predetermine a massacre, make an existing Core character a hostage, or assume Katarina ordered this incident. If the intermediary is independently shown to supply her circle, the two cases can share one route or provider while retaining distinct orders and victims.

Families with titles receive immediate audiences. Relatives of poorer attendants wait or are asked which household can speak for them. The Disciplinary Committee can protect its students and records, but it does not command the Empire's forces. Nera may pursue the case, compare a real lead with Starlia's, support a search or remain in school.

If earlier protection prevented the attack, preserve the success and investigate the attempted diversion. Continuing Starlia's arc does not require a second interchangeable disappearance.

### Long-Running Character and Institution Directions
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhhwqzxegv","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"status":"pending","start":"01/09/738","end":"30/09/741"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027263,"nanoseconds":133000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684326,"nanoseconds":861000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Nera:** Already extraordinarily powerful and already the legitimate commander of Blackwing, but not the duke, emperor, sole general or source of everyone's choices. His growth is leadership, judgment, personal commitments and what he builds in place of Cassian's methods. Concealment or disclosure of his ability belongs to him. Avoid convenient power nerfs.

**Alaric Dawnmere:** The grievance against Veyrath retainers remains concrete. He may accept fair actions, criticize actual wrongs, oppose Nera and still be principled. He has his own friends, studies and career. Do not villainize him merely to justify the protagonist.

**Liora Fen:** Adult, no longer sentenced to serve, genuinely Nera's girlfriend by choice. Her soul-fire and burning sword are unusual and potentially dangerous; she practices restraint. She is not assigned a compulsory jealousy, ownership or monogamy arc. Elise remains an important living adviser and mother.

**Vesper Nox:** Chooses her relationship and continued place in Nera's life. Her registry and separate **Lan Hsin** past may become important through credible encounters. **Only Nera knows the nine magically concealed tails**; everyone else sees one. Do not turn her eastern origin into automatic proof of affiliation with a newly named state.

**Isolde Veyless:** A certified person with wages, residence and rights despite an unremovable soul bond; she seeks to understand what she freely chooses. She can hold firm opinions, disagree in the space her bond permits, and have private pursuits. Compelled service is not evidence of romance, and her identity is not a device for an easy magical emancipation scene.

**Starlia and Dara:** Carry the actual result of the winter arc: recovery, care, living arrangements, contract status, known perpetrators, protected people and disclosed secrets. Starlia remains powerless at this stage. Their story continues through ordinary life and chosen responses, not compulsory renewed suffering.

**Cedric and the Emperor:** Both knowingly preserve and benefit from the corrupt Imperial order. Personal affection, competent rule and occasional concessions coexist with protection of privilege. Their knowledge of individual atrocities or Starlia's bloodline requires a concrete source. Do not present royal judgment as an automatic fair solution.

**Celestine Arvayne:** Her father's death, the sealed box, debt and creditor wardship are already history, not new mysteries invented for Year Three. She has a precise memory of sums and refuses cheap charity. Financial, legal and personal independence remain viable arcs if she acts on them.

**Sylwem Aelthir, Lysia Solenne and Lysandra Aurent:** Elaranth diplomacy, Solenne's compromised religious institutions and Imperial duty are real obligations, not obstacles invented only for Nera to overcome in romance. Sylwem's authorship and custody remain her issues; Lysia has already survived the pyre; Lysandra's trust is not guaranteed by a school exercise.

**Katarina Aurent:** Major personal antagonist in the Starlia arc and head of the adult heirs' inner society. Preserve her actual complicity, independently held resources and knowledge limits. Her prior invitation and any affection do not redeem or erase the crimes. Her age, royal duty and **Year Four status in IY739** matter; after summer 740 she is no longer a sitting student. If the first act removes her, later events use only surviving causes.

**Magerrett, Lucien, Taigar and Elin:** Each has distinct family standing, independent banner officers and priorities. Magerrett protects without automatically obeying Nera; Lucien manages genuine strategic pressure; Taigar grows into her own judgment; young Elin's adult regent retains dangerous-deployment countersign authority. No sibling's troops become Nera's by casual request.

**Tanya Rauth and the Blackwing marshal:** Both professionals with legitimate operational command in Nera's absence. Tanya leads the **930 existing Ironthorn assault infantry**; her Fourth Arc earth/metal-alchemy work uses accessible matter and has limits. A military operation should give experienced officers useful recommendations rather than making them fools so a first-year or second-year student can appear brilliant.

**New Academy cast:** Artoria, Jeanne, Musashi, Okita, Fran and Teacher, Riven, Malfoid, Skadi, Zima, Ningguang, Senku, Amiya, Dehya, Yinlin, Changli and others are **people enrolled in this setting**, not literal imported franchise crossover lore. Respect their specified current year and real school contacts; nobody has met Nera off-screen just because their name appears in the roster. Maintain the user's general preference for a predominantly female new cast and male dwarves, without rewriting existing genders.

**World texture:** Kharad's stronghold engineering, Saryuun's mobile beastkin hosts and charter courts, Umbrath's volcanic oath culture, Solenne's sanctuary orders, Orsenn's shipping houses, Elaranth's Greenhost and Varkesh's ironsteppe formations are available for grounded visitors and diplomacy. These places do not need new compulsory invasion arcs to feel alive. Pre-modern handgonnes and bombards exist but no modern cartridges, automatic weapons, mechanized tanks or effortless airlift.

### Y1-12 — The Counterspell Hearing and a Choice of Credit
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhjol8ykv0","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"start":"06/03/739","status":"pending","end":"12/03/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027231,"nanoseconds":985000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684327,"nanoseconds":256000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Follow-through on Academy scholarship.

Crownspire hosts a ward reliability symposium: projects are tested under changing conditions, declared contributors must defend maintenance plans, and rival classes can present useful failures. The results of Group 3's earlier filing determine its actual standing. A teacher may ask who should be allowed to inspect foreign-made wardwork; **Sylwem's provenance**, **Nera's publicly demonstrated capabilities** and **Evelyne's resource accounting** could make this personally awkward without forcing secret revelations. Liora's controlled soul-fire and Vesper's permitted perception skills need not be exhibited merely because they are unusual.

In parallel, a small problem—a student attempting to claim another's design, a broken practice barrier, a fee-waiver dispute—illustrates how institutional reputation is made by documentation. This can lead to a new mentorship, scholarship or mixed-class friendship. **Keep it a school story first**, not another human-trafficking raid.

### Y1-10 — The Princess's Household Matter
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhjt7k2vbj","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"24/01/739","start":"10/01/739","status":"pending","phases":[]},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027229,"nanoseconds":446000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684326,"nanoseconds":474000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Royal reaction, survivor decisions and an earned end to the first act.

Continue from the actual winter result. Living survivors need treatment, accommodation, wages or support, safe passage and decisions about their future. Some want compensation or relatives found; others reject publicity or prioritize revenge. Captivity's aftermath does not end with an estate's destruction. Starlia's own care and relationship with Dara retain scenes beyond the conspiracy.

**The public reading:** If Starlia's requested hearing occurred before the end of IY738, preserve its actual wording and outcome. If it did not, establish why and let her respond. Do not backdate a hearing or invent her signature. A court may enforce Nera's position while ignoring her suffering. Any achieved protection remains effective.

**Royal corruption:** Cedric can offer a polished private settlement protecting the dynasty, valuable supporters and captive-derived wealth. The Emperor can use appointments, silence, compensation, confiscation or selective punishment to preserve rule. They can sacrifice a troublesome intermediary while retaining the system that employed him. Their participation in corruption is real; knowledge of the royal secret or private rites still requires evidence. Neither is a dependable final court of moral appeal.

**The concealed bloodline:** Nera's inquiries may uncover a witness to Starlia's mother's history and corroborating household evidence. No resemblance, dormant ability or convenient universal blood test proves parentage. Starlia does not become the investigator; she decides how she responds to a claim about her own life and who may use it. If proof reaches a royal faction, its outrage can attach to imperial blood having been treated as common property while identical suffering among ordinary captives remains accepted.

**Family choices:** Lysandra must make her own judgment when reliable evidence implicates Katarina or Cedric. Her principles may lead to a limited protection, a confrontation, silence, refusal or a breach with family according to play. Malfoid may find royal pedigree more immediately compelling than a commoner's suffering, revealing the reach of her class assumptions without scripting her eventual response. Sylwem and Fleurdelys have their own people and political duties; a particular safe-conduct or repatriation requires authority and negotiation.

**Outcome space:** Katarina can lose people, money, safety, standing or life; gain a temporary settlement; survive through actual leverage; or face an unresolved conflict. Starlia is not required to forgive, accept recognition, pursue romance or become powerful. A real local victory can coexist with a corrupt Empire. If Katarina fell earlier, this phase addresses surviving assets and claims rather than reviving her.

Return to study, the infirmary, ordinary company and preparations for Miravel. Carry the true result onward; any continuing Katarina plot has to grow from surviving causes.

### Y1-11 — Miravel Excursion: Nine Towers, Nine Different Truths
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhlork2c4h","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"26/02/739","end":"03/03/739","status":"pending","phases":[]},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027231,"nanoseconds":985000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684323,"nanoseconds":62000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Length:** 6 days

**Type:** Wonder, technical challenges and independent scholars.

Crownspire's next optional academic expedition follows the inland route to **Miravel**, the pale-limestone hot-spring city with nine rival chartered towers. Identify each honestly: **Wardglass** (barriers), **Argent Measure** (spell mathematics), **Verdancy** (healing/cultivation), **Gloam Script** (ancient runes), **Stormneedle** (weather), **Cinder Crucible** (artifice), **Moonwell** (divination), **Mirror Lantern** (illusion), and **Silent Bell** (containment/counterspells). The Grand Conclave arbitrates dangerous demonstrations; restricted vaults remain restricted even for excellent students.

Playable scenes: students cross rune-tram bridges and rechargeable skywalks; a rival tower offers a public puzzle; a small team repairs a containment grid; a tower apprentice demonstrates a brilliant but impractical invention; the scholars' market offers inks, tools and lawful curiosities. Rival **Dorothy**, **Senku**, **Ilse**, **Evelyne**, **Sylwem** and other students may excel in things different from Nera. A formal dinner with a scholar can be as rewarding as a contest.

**Optional serious seed:** an aether-binding patent includes a ruinous service clause, a military buyer wants exclusive ward research, or a damaged guild ledger carries familiar transport dates. The guild dispute might be completely separate from the trafficking arc. A researcher can be curious about Nera's work without recognizing Ilyra's secret script instantly, reading his mind or learning Vesper's tails.

**Open play:** friendship, a technical triumph, an embarrassing practical error, future mentorship, a beautiful tower festival, private scholarship, a mundane vacation-like day, or refusal to attend. Do not conjure an ancient relic solely to expose Nera's power.

### Y1-06 — Founders' Festival: The People Everyone Admires
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhre2ri10w","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"start":"20/11/738","end":"23/11/738","status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027226,"nanoseconds":998000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684323,"nanoseconds":62000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Major social festival and a credible opening toward Katarina's society.

Preserve the existing guest list, seating arrangements, Group 3's actual eligibility, performances, stalls, dances and chosen companionship. The festival should remain enjoyable for people attending it. The circle's members appear among accomplished hosts and generous patrons, and their pleasant company has time to feel real.

**Opening:** An adult household patron associated with Katarina meets Nera through the actual social roster. Nera's Veyrath name is enough to make an introduction plausible. If the patron has received a credible report of his expensive care for Starlia, it can be interpreted as an aristocrat's unusual taste in a prized dependent. No one acquires that information merely because the narrator knows it.

**Concrete scene:** A member offers the temporary service or transfer of an adult household performer as an ordinary courtesy between important people. The performer has first been introduced through a mundane conversation, skill or personal concern. Other guests continue talking while the holders discuss the arrangement. The same patron can sincerely defend a noble guest from an unwanted advance.

**Connections:** Malfoid's bloodline pride and social fluency can provide an introduction or expose her assumptions; she is not assigned secret membership. Sylwem's protected royal-hostage status stands beside the market treatment of other elves. Fleurdelys can respond to the purchase of people through her own experience of ceremonial obligation, without instantly becoming an Imperial investigator. Lysandra may witness a specific contradiction between royal etiquette and the justice she believes the house should uphold. Cedric values these families' money and influence and can personally smooth a complaint away.

A further dinner invitation is an opportunity, not an acceptance. Katarina may notice Nera's suitability or seek a conversation if actual events warrant it. Her earlier personal invitation stays governed by what was played. The festival does not automatically reveal Starlia's identity, expose Nera's plan or turn into an attack.

### Y1-01 — Group 3's Public Review: The Cost of One Shelter
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhtl7alhf1","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"start":"01/10/738","status":"completed","end":"14/10/738"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027222,"nanoseconds":16000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684326,"nanoseconds":40000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Small-stakes school politics with long-term repercussions.

The Group 3 shelter and ward-charter filing proceeds from its **actual** current drawings, signatures, budget, Elaranth-associated pattern, registry marks and Sylwem's earlier assessor concerns. The review asks who certified the structure, whether a non-Imperial technique can be maintained under Imperial rules, who bears accident liability and who gets formal credit. **Evelyne** may defend financial practicality, **Alaric** test fairness, **Rhea** consider field use, **Liora** address safety, and **Sylwem** guard her intellectual authorship, each according to current relationships rather than imposed agreement.

A successful review can bring a demonstration, a small award, workshop access, field-testing permission or a request for revisions. Failure costs fees, pride or time, not automatic expulsion or a conspiracy. An assessor may reasonably ask about Nera's declared capacity to cut an unfamiliar anchor; that does not grant knowledge of his hidden casting speed. Credits and mistakes affect later sponsorship and the Year Two council.

**Open play:** compromise over ownership, help from student engineers such as Dorothy or Senku **after a proper introduction**, selecting a test site, disputing a technical point, doing nothing, or letting teammates lead. This is where recurring student relationships should begin to matter.

### Y1-04 — Starlia's Aftermath: The Buyer behind the Factor
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhv1ymfklk","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","phases":[],"start":"02/11/738","end":"08/11/738"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027224,"nanoseconds":478000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684323,"nanoseconds":482000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Continuation from the played Vharos aftermath. Use the latest actual state after 4 November; do not replay her discovery or turn a pending action into a completed one.

Starlia remains in the room above the Vharos oil press until her physician clears transport. Dara, Perla and Marta Sedge have real care responsibilities. Her amputations, weakness and dependence persist. Prosthetics require assessment, funding, fabrication, fitting and rehabilitation. She is presently an ordinary, powerless survivor; the act gives her no sudden investigative or combat gift.

The existing clues are the one-gold purchase, red-stone ring, grey coach, Corvan Hess's death and the missing third-pen docket. The played account identifies Hess as a buying factor; do not collapse him into the separate sadistic holder or assume his death settled the chain. The docket's taker is unknown.

**First tangible lead:** A clerk or household agent, reached through an actual inquiry, remembers Hess billing a wealthy capital household for the acquisition and later medical attendance. What the agent considers important is the account holder and whether another owner is claiming compensation. An old transport instruction or actual witness can corroborate this. These two leads can point toward a supplier also preparing a large winter household commission. They reveal particular people; they do not reveal the entire society.

**Character connections:** Lysia can assess medical findings if Nera actually shares them or arranges a consultation. Isolde can distinguish facts she witnessed from inference. Vesper can recognize how an acquisition is discussed without surrendering privacy or her protected status. Liora may favor swift, severe protection of Nera's household; her loyalty does not automatically answer what Starlia wants. Celestine can examine a particular invoice if invited, drawing on her financial competence and creditor wardship.

Keep Starlia's unsigned agreement and requested public reading at their actual state. Her signature, trust, affection and testimony are never supplied by the plan. Any threat to her care must follow a concrete actor and action; her rescue is not repeatedly undone to keep the plot dark.

### Y1-00 — Asterfen Aftermath and the Next Roster Window
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqhxn8n7t03","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"status":"completed","start":"01/09/738","end":"15/09/738"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027222,"nanoseconds":16000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684323,"nanoseconds":482000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Continuity:** Transition from live play; preserve outcomes.

The field-command office closes its route, ward and rescue evaluations. Instructor **Severin Holt** can publish objective results and individual comments, while classmates compare accounts of the same journey. **Lysandra**, **Malfoid**, **Rhea**, **Ilse**, or others involved in the live exercise have reasons to speak again based on what actually happened; do not predetermine affection, resentment or alliances. Student fatigue, practical supplies and medical follow-up count more than a new scripted emergency.

The announcement of another academic trip raises a realistic registration question: **which excursions are class-organized, which are optional, what capacity and qualifications apply, and whether friends may request to travel together**. Let Academy officials or posted rules answer it. Asterfen field teams are not automatically the same as the Vharos excursion party or Group 3's workshop roster.

**Open play:** debriefs, apologies, celebrations, grades, a quiet meal, visiting friends, exploring the town, a medical check, shopping or returning to normal study. If Nera wants the next trip, he can ask and apply; do not enroll him automatically.

### Y1-03 — Riverside Days: Rival Clubs, Markets and First Friendships
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqi0vs1oq0a","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"30/11/738","phases":[],"start":"01/11/738"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027224,"nanoseconds":478000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684327,"nanoseconds":256000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Recurring social anchor, not a compulsory single outing.

Crownspire's Riverside Market, class lounges, formal dining halls, indoor rings, library terraces, training courtyards and student-club rooms give a tangible home base. The Academy houses 240 new students across **Classes A–F**, with widely differing specialties; a strong student in ward geometry or medicine need not be a great duelist. The martial clubs arrange voluntary bouts; artificers seek funds; the News Bureau wants a good story; students from rich families host gatherings that scholarship cadets may not afford. Introduce distinct classmates as **new acquaintances**, never retconned childhood friends: **Artoria** and **Mordred** around knightly discipline, **Riven** and **Okita** in the ring, **Fran and her sentient Teacher** during a sword-related scene, **Dorothy and Senku** at a workshop, **Ningguang** among merchants, and **Jeanne** through volunteer healing.

Possible smaller scenes: a cheap supper versus an extravagant invitation; a playful two-class practical; a dispute over shared gear; a gift bought with earned wages; an upper-year offering advice with strings attached; Vayne finally seeking the sabre bout interrupted earlier. Nothing requires a spectacular villain. **The first year should repeatedly return to life like this after heavy events.**

### Y2-12 — The Eastern Muster: An Actual War Approaches
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqi1wavj8vf","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"status":"pending","start":"08/06/740","end":"15/06/740"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027246,"nanoseconds":436000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684330,"nanoseconds":909000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Military-political transition.

The spring Blackgorge clashes have expanded into a **bounded frontier campaign**: rival units occupy contested watch stations, artillery damages road forts, families are moved out of exposed hamlets, and a convoy has been taken or turned back near the Redsteppe approaches. This is **real organized warfare**, not a school rehearsal. Whether it escalates into a general Imperial–Varkesh war remains open. The **Thornhold ducal staff** allocates sectors; **Harrowmere** purchases feed and controls wagon schedules; **Forgevault** repairs bombards and armor; **Blackwing Keep** receives marching instructions under the hereditary charter.

The command structure matters. Cassian determines broader house-wide strategy; the charter gives Nera lawful operational control of his **existing 5,000 Blackwing soldiers** within its assigned duties. His sworn marshal manages readiness; **Tanya Rauth** controls the existing 930 Ironthorn assault infantry and does not magically become commander of all five banners. **Magerrett** and **Taigar** control their own forces, and **Lucien**, now a graduate, may have orders involving First Thorn. All of these people can disagree professionally without automatically revolting.

**Open play:** consultations, letters, budgets, approved battlefield travel plans, warnings against overextending, a late peace offer. Nera can inspect the actual orders and decide whether to leave Crownspire during the forthcoming recess.

### Y2-07 — Beneath the Market of Oaths
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqi27sr35q7","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"17/03/740","phases":[],"status":"pending","end":"29/03/740"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027244,"nanoseconds":4000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684330,"nanoseconds":909000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Major investigation opportunity.

Evidence—if any was gathered—points toward a network using creditor courts, transport seals and binding contracts to move people into mines, private workshops, noble service and war-construction gangs. Vaelcross's illegal market is not a magical doorway where all missing victims sit together. The true system consists of intermediaries: a false debt, a bought clerk, a route through **Amberreach or Greyford**, guards paid to look away and buyers in separate jurisdictions. Some actors may also use northern **Orsenn** brokers; avoid blaming all Free Cities or every merchant.

A sting, lawful warrant, debtor redemption, printing campaign, whistleblower protection or discreet recovery is possible, each with costs. Survivors can refuse publicity or mistrust rescuers. A freed person still needs a safe legal identity, travel money, food and somewhere to sleep. Cassian's **Black Concordance** may use coercive oath technology, but do not collapse all mundane trafficking into a single secret system without proof. Whatever was closed during Year One remains closed.

### Y2-04 — Winterglass Ball: Debts, Embassies and Unspoken Promises
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqi5ajrti38","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"18/01/740","start":"12/01/740","phases":[],"status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027241,"nanoseconds":591000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684328,"nanoseconds":48000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Political social set piece with romance, culture and pressure.

A major **Vaelcross** reception gathers Imperial court families, provincial officers, former students, Solenne and Elaranth delegates, grain merchants, and some Varkesh envoys still entitled to negotiated passage. Let guests arrive through the capital's seven defensive rings; noble privilege and city poverty coexist outside the lighted ballroom. People come with goals beyond matchmaking: military contracts, embassy protections, Elaranth ward timber, Solenne charitable charters, Celestine's wardship finances and provincial funding. **Katarina**, now a final-year student, may meet Nera if the prior personal thread naturally brings them together; her past condition of speaking in person remains hers.

Romance, dancing, social embarrassment and chosen affection have real space. No partner can veto Nera's dialogue, and Nera cannot decide another character's public relationship status by himself. Negotiators must leave with an actual answer—offer, condition, refusal, written deadline or invitation to formal talks. A dark rumor may circulate, but do not interrupt every dance with armed attackers.

### Y2-08 — Interacademy Concord Games: Opponents Worth Knowing
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqi7aicyvoi","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","start":"06/04/740","end":"13/04/740","phases":[]},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027244,"nanoseconds":4000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684331,"nanoseconds":321000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Academic competition and foreign camaraderie.

Foreign academies and specialist schools send teams for formal martial, healing, ward design, engineering and command contests. The usual diplomatic limits apply. Rival schools need distinct talents; no rule says the only contest worth seeing is Nera's duel. **Irelia and Musashi** are now seniors nearing graduation; **Kujou Sara, Amiya, Artoria, Riven, Skadi, Fran and Okita** may show their growth; **Alaric** can excel by his own choices. Allow a believable underdog victory in a technical discipline, honest sporting respect and enjoyable festival nights.

Military observers notice how young commanders reason about supply and civilian protection, not just how fast they swing swords. An exchange officer from **Varkesh** can be a professional rival and later an advocate for a humane prisoner exchange. A nation does not become a single enemy because its student lost a match.

### Y2-11 — Campaign Examination: Maps Cannot Feed Horses
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqi8e0f83w8","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","phases":[],"end":"07/06/740","start":"20/05/740"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027246,"nanoseconds":436000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684328,"nanoseconds":48000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Academic command climax.

Students plan and execute supervised simulations covering battlefield supply, retreat, convoy defense, relief corridors, casualty evacuation, fortification repair and anti-air defense. The exercise can use scaled versions of **Blackgorge's cliff gates, Harrowmere's grain routes and Varkesh's winter supply problem**, without turning a classroom map into actual state secrets. A banner of elite armor wins little if its replacement straps, grain or dragon fodder never arrive. **Imperial Crown Legions**, **Veyrath heavy infantry**, **Varkesh Redsteppe Spearguard**, **Ashfur Bear Riders** and **Storm-Wyvern Riders** have distinct doctrines, with matching counters and limits.

This tests professionalism and the ability to work with others; it does **not** award Nera the command he already legally owns or give other first-years instant generalships. Nera may earn recognition, attract an internship offer or reveal as much of his talent as he chooses. End-of-year results have consequences for Year Three placements.

### Y1-16 — Crown Trials: A School with an Outside World
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqiar0vgn6f","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","phases":[],"end":"24/06/739","start":"10/06/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027234,"nanoseconds":397000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684330,"nanoseconds":498000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Year-end practical and social climax.

Students face professional tests in tactical planning, team rescue, ward repair, command ethics, combat skill and a seeded warded melee. There are classmates who know things Nera does not and opponents who can score points under defined rules without absurdly matching his hidden full power. A well-played tactical outcome matters; **Nera is neither forced to win nor forced to lose**. Alaric retains his independent path and grievances. Academic rank and promotion through **A–F** follow actual evaluation, not prewritten romance or bloodline.

The year closes with awards, rival farewells, senior careers, possible changes to club offices, and practical feedback. **Lucien and Irene's cohort graduates at the correct summer 739 boundary**; that does not make them disappear from politics or the eastern army. Crownspire publicly announces the coming **Year Two student-council election**, advanced electives, and upcoming Taigar intake. No early graduation for Nera.

### Core Steering Contract
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqibanv4si0","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"30/09/741","start":"01/09/738","phases":[],"status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027219,"nanoseconds":300000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684327,"nanoseconds":652000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
1. **Hard situation, soft outcome.** School terms, announced examinations, scheduled festivals, charter reviews, foreign diplomatic windows, and opportunities to undertake travel or legitimate banner command arrive. Personal attendance, victories, investigations, romances, casualties, guilt, coups, invasions, and particular peace terms are not preselected.
2. **Canon precedence:** (a) new explicit user directions; (b) played narrative; (c) current lorebook state; (d) this anchor plan; (e) possible outcomes. If a once-future anchor is already resolved in play, record that resolution and **do not replay it**.
3. **No character puppetry.** The user chooses Nera's dialogue and voluntary acts. Classmates, teachers, families, foreign envoys and military officers have separate schedules, goals and opinions. They may act while he is absent, but cannot acquire knowledge of his secrets by narrative convenience.
4. **Real consequences, no endless replacement crises.** Proved rescues stay rescued, convictions stay effective absent a real reversal, dead organizations do not respawn, and a prevented atrocity is not replaced by a suspiciously identical atrocity. When an old incident becomes impossible, preserve its narrative function through existing survivors, hearings, reconstruction or legitimate new interests—not an arbitrary catastrophe.
5. **Maintain texture.** Most weeks are school weeks: exams, meals, clubs, ward benches, sparring, academic failures, friendships, markets, letters, courtship, local travel and rest. A major dark event should have earlier clues and a subsequent aftermath; do not put a massacre, duel, kidnapping and world treaty into the same afternoon. Play quiet scenes, holidays, travel and recovery rather than summarizing them away.
6. **Respect Nera's power.** At eighteen his exceptional bodily strength and speed, nearly instantaneous casting, perfected mountain-cleaving **Heaven Splitter**, and established Arc 4 classification are real. Ordinary students or raiders cannot suddenly overpower him in a straight unrestrained fight. Credible difficulties are protecting others in several places, incomplete intelligence, hostage safety, command and supply, political jurisdiction, civilian losses, fatigue of allied forces, revealing secrets and accountability.
7. **Relationships remain individual.** Liora and Vesper are already Nera's adult girlfriends by independent choice; neither is an owned subordinate or automatic exclusive gatekeeper. Isolde's personhood is legally recognized, her soul bond remains magically unremovable, and her feelings are not dictated by obedience. Celestine, Lysandra, Sylwem, Lysia, Katarina and all other adults make their own choices; do not impose romance or choose a hierarchy.
8. **Secrets stay secret until earned.** Only Nera knows Vesper's actual nine tails; magic presents **one tail** to everybody else, including her close companions. Her Lan Hsin identity is a distinct concealed fact. Nera's instant casting, full physical ceiling, Ilyra-linked ancient script knowledge, and information omitted from Cassian's weekly reports are not revealed by a perceptive NPC without credible evidence or Nera's actions.
9. **Use real geography and authority.** Vaelcross and Crownspire are in the central Imperial basin; **Vharos** is south toward Solenne; **Miravel** is the eastern inland nine-tower city; **Gullhaven** opens onto the western Glass Sea and the **Tideglass Isles**. **Amberreach** supplies grain between the capital and the eastern marches. The Veyrath circuit is **Greyford → Harrowmere → Thornhold / Forgevault → Blackgorge → the contested Redsteppe / Urzak Gate toward Varkesh**. A route may use alternatives and branches; do not collapse these into adjacent neighborhoods. Ships and armies consume actual days, escorts and supplies.
10. **Military scale is binding.** Nera's permanently sworn **Blackwing Banner = exactly 5,000 professional fighters**, an existing combined-arms force based at **Blackwing Keep**. Its 30 Thorn Paladins, 24 Dreadlance Cataphracts, **six Skythorn true-dragon knights**, and the 930 Ironthorn assault infantry under **Tanya Rauth** are parts of those 5,000, not extras. Its marshal manages daily orders in Nera's absence. Cassian controls the grand Veyrath war effort and reserves; Lucien, Magerrett, Taigar and Elin have their own separate, already counted banners. Never conflate Veyrath **Thornwall Shield Legions** with Imperial **Crown Legions** or coerced Imperial **Chain Levies**. Dragons, wyverns and bird riders are different.
11. **War has prerequisites.** Frontier hostility is plausible, but an Empire-wide declaration, an invasion of Vaelcross, a fully mobilized continent and specific atrocities require motives, orders, transport, food, reconnaissance and unresolved disputes. Varkesh's factions disagree with each other; Elaranth, Saryuun, Solenne, Orsenn, Kharad and Umbrath have their own interests, not interchangeable villain roles.
12. **Recess stays playable.** Announce every term break in advance. Give realistic options and ask what Nera does; no forced destination, companion roster or multi-month time jump. If fighting prevents leisure travel on a particular road, alternative destinations and ordinary days still exist.

### Y2-01 — Council Election and the Price of a Voice
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqibd1sx1hd","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"06/10/739","status":"pending","phases":[],"end":"20/10/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027239,"nanoseconds":184000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684331,"nanoseconds":321000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Serious campus political contest.

With Cordelia and Cedric now alumni, a new student-council coalition forms around actual sitting students: senior incumbents, scholarship advocates, club treasurers, dormitory representatives and nobles protecting privileges. Alumni may sponsor candidates or lobby, but **cannot pretend still to be eligible student candidates**. Topics are specific: how activity fees are allocated, whether low-income students can join excursions, access to practice rings, equipment ownership, student press freedom, protected-student rights and what happens when a sponsor's name appears on a questioned contract.

Candidates need volunteers, ballots, rules and competing manifestos. Nera can run, advise, campaign, vote, oppose or ignore the election; his Class A ranking grants no council office. **Ningguang, Evelyne, Amiya, Rhea, Artoria** or other students can express differing policies, not predetermined teams. Small electoral decisions alter who can authorize a hearing or print a document later. A campaign can produce a genuine friendship, a petty scandal, an honest compromise or a defeated candidate who remains admirable.

### Y2-06 — Capital Service: Seven Rings, Different Lives
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqiccx4wq1j","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"end":"16/03/740","start":"03/03/740","status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027241,"nanoseconds":591000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684330,"nanoseconds":63000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Immersive civilian arc in Vaelcross.

Second-years apply to **real city placements**: clinics, civic courts, printing houses, river-port warehouses, engineering shops, ward inspection offices, relief kitchens and district legal aid. The capital's ring system matters. A hospital in a crowded downstream ward sees injuries that an upper-ring noble clinic never records; merchants at bridge chain-gates care about toll authority; a legal clerk can find a missing signature with more lasting effect than a duel.

Crownspire students work within placements; they do not command the city's guard. Show several ordinary days: tending patients, losing an argument with a competent shopkeeper, catching a genuine accounting error, having lunch with coworkers, meeting the family of an injured porter, or attending an evening concert. Expose poverty, species discrimination and exploitative labor without assuming every Imperial official is criminal. Characters such as **Lysia, Jeanne, Mirelle, Ningguang, Senku** or **Dorothy** may serve in parallel sites independently.

### Y1-14 — Gullhaven Departure and Life aboard the Crownward Leviathan
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqied2f39kn","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"14/05/739","phases":[],"start":"01/05/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027234,"nanoseconds":397000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684329,"nanoseconds":661000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Length:** 2 weeks including the journey

**Type:** Major maritime adventure and social centerpiece.

Crownspire announces a paid, supervised sea excursion to the **Tideglass Isles**, with proper enrollment limits and alternatives for students who stay home. Students travel west by arranged road or river links to the fortress harbor of **Gullhaven**; the huge **Crownward Leviathan** waits at the Crownward Lock with its rune-ribbed hull, substantial crew, multiple decks, classrooms, dining halls, cabins, infirmary, lifeboats and naval escort craft. It is a true large sea-going vessel, not a small magical skiff. The captain commands seamanship; academic staff govern school activities. Neither authority passes to Nera merely because he is a duke's son.

**Make the outward voyage playable:** sorting cabins without predetermined roommates, students watching gulls over the outer harbor, a chart-room navigation lesson, a deck games afternoon, a concert or formal supper, teasing over seasickness, stars during night watch, professional crew conversations, and quiet space for friends or lovers who choose it. A disagreement about noble cabin privileges can be meaningful without the ship immediately coming under attack. Nera's gifts do not replace weather reading, shipbuilding, sailing skill or coastal jurisdiction.

**At the islands:** explore coral-blue shallows, basalt cliffs, fishing settlements, markets, ancient lighthouse wards and volcanic trails. Offer routes suited to different people: navigation research, island botanical observation, a licensed ruin survey, cooperative harbor repair, sea ecology, a boat race or a festival evening. Local islanders have their own livelihoods, resentments and stories; they are not disposable quest markers.

**Optional danger with causes:** old lighthouse ward drift, contraband cargo, a disputed customs boarding, a sudden squall forecast earlier, or reef predators in a known season. Sailors, escorts, ward batteries, lifeboats and prior good preparation must work. There is **no compulsory shipwreck, abduction or fatal disaster**. Clues might connect to shipping contracts seen at Gullhaven or Orsenn, but not every sea incident is a trafficking operation.

**Open play:** Nera may decline travel, enjoy the cruise, choose shore activities, befriend sailors or classmates, study ancient runes privately, intervene in a credible danger or simply watch the sea. Include the return sailing and journey to Crownspire; do not cut directly from embarkation to the Crown Trials.

### Y2-09 — South Causeway Ambush: A Bell that Stays Silent
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqig8sholps","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","phases":[],"end":"21/04/740","start":"19/04/740"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027244,"nanoseconds":4000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684330,"nanoseconds":63000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Credible lethal attack and accountability.

A mixed school-and-medical convoy passes the **southern Imperial causeway that connects the capital through Vharos toward Solenne**. An armed company with military-grade equipment may attack it over hostages, documents or supplies. If warning systems, escorts or earlier investigations give adequate protection, the attack can be repelled or prevented. **Do not guarantee a slaughter.** If the attack succeeds, name its real consequences: lost escorts, missing attendants, injured learners, interrupted messages, funerals, trauma, demands for inquiries and differing testimony.

This is separate in geography and chain of custody from eastern Blackgorge. A seller of Imperial weapons, a rogue military faction, a privateer-employed gang or an unconnected enemy could be responsible; insignia are not proof. Some classmates may have known a victim without putting an established major character on a preassigned death list. It can sharpen campus political conflict between demands for punishment and demands for evidence.

### Y1-05 — Kestrel Hollow: A Wedding Gift
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqiidnrs91b","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"16/11/738","start":"10/11/738","phases":[]},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027226,"nanoseconds":998000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684327,"nanoseconds":652000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** A planned collective atrocity with preventable consequences.

**Narrator-facing cause, subject to prior play:** An adult member of Katarina's inner circle commissions captives as a commemorative gift for another member's forthcoming marriage. Cooperating households provide funds, armed retainers and buyers. They choose a vulnerable settlement off the Greyford–Harrowmere approaches because its people have little enforceable protection. This commission uses a broker encountered through Starlia's purchase chain. It does not make Katarina responsible for every raid in the Empire.

Seed at least two discoverable warnings before the attack window: a named household's unusually large transport-and-provisions order and a locally credible report that armed contractors are surveying an off-road settlement. Each warning must travel through real people and roads. Nera is not conveniently present at Kestrel Hollow. Messages and any Blackwing defensive instructions take the established relay time; his marshal and other officers retain lawful command responsibilities.

**If the raid proceeds:** Homes and stores burn, resistance is killed, and surviving inhabitants are taken. Report only casualties and missing people supported by what actually happens. Captured adults can later be distributed as gifts or assigned to the circle's private entertainments and rites. A recipient may thank the giver sincerely and commission art to remember the occasion. The atrocities are discussed in the language of hospitality and property.

**If protection succeeds:** Preserve the saved settlement, intercepted wagons or reduced losses. The members dispute the expense and embarrassment; they cannot replace the prevented atrocity with an identical massacre elsewhere. Their later celebration must reflect the people and resources actually available.

The public explanation may be banditry or frontier violence. Uninformed officials can blame Varkesh without proving its involvement. Harrowmere's local authorities may prioritize freight and taxes. Survivors have burial, housing, family and recovery needs that continue after any investigation. News reaching Lysia, Sylwem or another student gives them an opportunity to respond independently, rather than assembling Nera's entire class into a rescue party.

### Y2-00 — New Faces, Older Responsibilities
