<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqinp9aqyd7","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"05/10/739","phases":[],"start":"01/10/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027236,"nanoseconds":791000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684330,"nanoseconds":909000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Cohort transition and grounded homecoming.

**Taigar Veyrath**, **Fiammetta Kaorun** and **Sanhua Orren** belong to the **new IY739 first-year intake**, not Nera's original class. They complete their own placement examinations; do not assign them Class A or a ranking by fiat. Taigar arrives with her existing **3,000-person Ironroot Banner** charter, independent ambitions and pressures from Cassian; she is neither Nera's automatic subordinate nor an enemy. Nera's cohort enters Year Two, taking some supervision and club responsibilities without magically becoming faculty.

Maintain correct upper-year progression from the lorebook: **Lucien, Irene, Cordelia, Cedric, Zima and Gellert**, who were Year Four in autumn 738, have graduated in summer 739. They can now influence the Academy from court, military posts, professional networks and invitations—not by sitting unchanged in their old classroom seats. **Katarina, Aurelia, Irelia and Musashi** are now Year Four; **Magerrett, Changli and Senshi** are now Year Three. Correspondence with graduates creates new power relationships. Play first-week reunions, changed billets, new teaching assistants and independent freshman friendships before plunging into a new crisis.

### Y1-15 — First Blood at Blackgorge
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqirr5r04f2","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"status":"pending","start":"20/05/739","end":"27/05/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027234,"nanoseconds":397000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684329,"nanoseconds":661000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** First clearly military crisis, initially beyond student control.

Along the **Blackgorge Corridor**, Imperial and Varkesh border parties clash near a disputed eastern transit point leading toward the **Redsteppe and Urzak Gate**. A watch fort suffers casualties, merchants turn back, and some families seek protection behind the layered cliff defenses. Each side releases an incompatible map and an account of who fired first. This is **limited organized violence**, not proof that all Varkesh factions favor invasion or that war has reached Vaelcross.

**Harrowmere** begins counting grain and fodder; **Forgevault** checks repair backlogs; **Thornhold** receives officer reports; **Blackwing Keep** may be told to heighten readiness in its existing assigned sectors. **Tanya Rauth** and Blackwing's sworn marshal can send honest progress and resource requests to Nera. Because he already commands this force, nobody explains his charter as if he has just earned it. Students receive letters; Varkesh classmates are judged unfairly by some peers; others defend them.

**Open play:** Nera can send lawful defensive instructions, ask for reports, request a consultation, leave military management with his marshal while at school, or take whatever action is genuinely available. The event begins the road to Year Two; it does not force an early battlefield departure.

### Y2-05 — Funding Review: Who Pays for an Honest Academy?
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqivhs2icsl","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"26/02/740","start":"12/02/740","status":"pending","phases":[]},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027241,"nanoseconds":591000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684331,"nanoseconds":321000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Institutional follow-through.

Crownspire audits donations, excursion bursaries, repair budgets and service contracts. A donor implicated by reliable evidence may threaten to pull support; another may finance reform because she sees genuine opportunity. Students have to price actual reforms: documentation, escrow, protection for complainants and alternative funding for labs. **Evelyne** or **Ningguang** can ask who covers next semester's aether cells; **Jeanne** or **Lysia** can ask who bears the medical cost of a student worked to exhaustion. Both matter.

A successful outcome might be an independent scholarship pool, new inspection rights or publication of contract terms. A failed outcome might be lost funding, an appeal or reputational damage. Do not strip away an achieved reform simply to make a villain appear powerful.

### Y1-17 — Summer Convoy, Family Invitations and the First Free Recess
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqiwjq656r0","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"30/09/739","phases":[],"start":"25/06/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027236,"nanoseconds":791000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684330,"nanoseconds":63000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Multi-path break with an optional tangible civilian crisis.

A grain-and-relief convoy is scheduled along the **Amberreach → Harrowmere** network, with freight branching to **Greyford, Thornhold and the frontier**. Relief administrators seek escorts while military offices prioritize replacement armor, fodder and aether cells. Families from Kestrel Hollow and earlier disappearances search manifests for missing people. Officers, not students, control the actual route unless lawful authority is delegated. The convoy's departure date and state of danger are known; any attack depends on a prepared adversary, not a guaranteed cliffhanger.

The Academy closes for recess. Nera can **return to the Veyrath estate, visit Blackwing Keep, remain at Crownspire, vacation at Tideglass, revisit Miravel or Vharos, travel with willing friends, undertake a lawful internship, or choose a different plausible destination**. Other people have their own permissions and commitments. A homecoming may reveal Blackwing officers, its dragon aerie and Tanya's reports; it is a chance to renew personal contact with the force before later war, **not** a requirement to fight immediately. A holiday may remain entirely peaceful.

**Carry to Year Two:** actual study results and friendships; roster membership; available evidence and victims; exposed or intact broker cells; border orders and casualties; what Nera instructed Blackwing; personal promises; and any holiday travel he freely chose. Announce autumn enrollment in advance. Do not jump ahead without permission.

### Y2-13 — Blackwing Summons: The Commander Who Has Never Needed a Classroom Promotion
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqixabo51ca","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"23/06/740","status":"pending","start":"16/06/740","phases":[]},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027248,"nanoseconds":850000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684329,"nanoseconds":661000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Explicit personal battlefield opportunity.

An operational message from **Blackwing Keep** requests Nera's decision on a concrete mission: **secure a contested relief-and-supply route linking Harrowmere's western depots through Thornhold to the Blackgorge approaches**, while other formations hold the main cliff gates. The report identifies the units available, the road distance, how long provisions will last, an intelligence gap, and who currently has tactical command. A hostile force, independent of Nera, is preparing to contest a roadhead, bridge or forward magazine. Someone must act. This is **not** a ceremonial visit to his soldiers and not a forced enlistment: they are already sworn to him.

The Academy has an established recess and credible leave procedures; a twenty-year-old Year Two student who is also a hereditary banner commander may seek lawful travel and command without pretending the school has conscripted its entire cohort. Classmates who would like to accompany him must have actual grounds—medical certification, reporting assignment, family duty, approved observer status, independent enlistment or travel permission. **No automatic harem party**, no unapproved freshman battalion, no classmate made a dragon rider without training.

**Open play:** Nera may accept, refuse, limit his personal travel, delegate his authority, request alternative objectives, negotiate with the duke, summon reports or change his plan. If he does go, the next anchor is a **played, genuine Blackwing war operation**, not a cutscene skipping to victory.

### Y2-10 — After the Bells: Emergency Council and Relief
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqiy2kom5zc","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"22/04/740","phases":[],"end":"12/05/740","status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027246,"nanoseconds":436000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684328,"nanoseconds":48000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Consequences, not another disaster.

The Academy debates safer travel protocols, the limits of the Disciplinary Committee, escorts, survivor care and emergency funds. A refugee reception or public vigil gives named people room to grieve or challenge official narratives. Diplomatic envoys argue over whether private contractors should be barred from transport charters. Nera may lead a small reform, oppose rumor, keep training, visit survivors or do none of these things.

**Personal arc seed:** a Blackwing officer asks what civil protections Nera expects of his **own** soldiers and contracted suppliers. This is a genuine command question before he ever rides onto a battlefield. It must not be answered for him by the narrator, nor should Tanya suddenly be incompetent simply to make Nera look wiser.

### Y2-02 — Scholarships Written in Chains
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqiyq0dqa4f","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"start":"21/10/739","status":"pending","end":"07/11/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027239,"nanoseconds":184000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684327,"nanoseconds":652000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Academic policy meets Imperial law.

A new donor-backed scholarship promises tuition relief but contains a **transferable service placement** clause. Some clauses are cruel but recognized under Imperial law; others are forged or falsely concealed. The affected student need not be an acquaintance of Nera. A lawful scholarship board, creditor, or independent family asks the Academy to rule on disclosure and contractual fairness. Separating enforceable terms from outright fraud keeps the legal conflict more interesting than simply punching a broker.

**Celestine** may recognize structures reminiscent of the Arvayne creditor arrangements; **Isolde's legal certification** is relevant precedent but does **not** allow the Academy to remove her magical soul bond. Vesper's still-imperfect registry classification may reappear in a personal encounter **only if it remains unresolved in live play**. Students can create emergency funds, demand plain-language contracts, pursue court action, withdraw sponsorship or accept a compromise. A council vote affects campus rules; it does not abolish slavery across the Empire overnight.

### Y2-03 — The Cinder Archive: One Stamp, Different Owners
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqizb7ac6e1","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"10/11/739","phases":[],"status":"pending","end":"21/11/739"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027239,"nanoseconds":184000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684330,"nanoseconds":498000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Scholarly expedition with regional ties.

An approved team visits a damaged historical ward repository associated with **Ashbridge in the Imperial Cindervale Marches**, where volcanic crossings, regional governors, old sealworks and contested labor records make the site coherent. The archive is not secretly in Varkesh or across an impossible continent-wide day trip; escort permissions and journey time apply. Researchers reconstruct burnt ward measurements and shipment ledgers, compare replacement parts with **Forgevault** manufacture marks and examine whether some emergency procurement warrants were issued under real military authority.

The exercise begins as legitimate research. If investigators choose to compare records with Year One evidence, a supplier's seal may connect legal arms shipments to illegal worker transfers, but it is not proof that every clerk in the ducal household obeyed Cassian's personal criminal order. **Mirelle, Yinlin, Dehya**, regional sealwrights or an honest archivist may have specialized expertise, with their own reasons to preserve local history. **Umbrath's Glassfire Fault and Gravefire Gate** may be discussed as neighboring strategic geography without rewriting the sovereign **Umbrath Covenant** as merely an Imperial province.

**Open play:** translation, ward demonstrations, a personal research contact, lost notes, compelling testimony, a narrow discovery, or nothing sinister at all. Evidence already found earlier becomes corroboration rather than the same revelation staged twice.

### Y2-BREAK / Y2-WAR01 — The First Blackwing Campaign
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqizq2ohpqr","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"24/06/740","end":"30/09/740","phases":[],"status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027248,"nanoseconds":850000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684330,"nanoseconds":498000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Major playable war arc; the first planned on-page Blackwing campaign during his Academy years; his pre-Academy Eastern service remains established history.

This is the Year Two–Three break battlefield opportunity. Nera's participation remains voluntary and user-controlled; if he does not attend, the military operation proceeds independently through credible reports rather than a narrator-controlled version of Nera.

**Carry to Year Three:** exact troops and equipment lost or retained, command relationships, supply rights, civilians helped or harmed, prisoners and their legal status, what others publicly know of Nera's power, which enemy officers are still active, dates of any ceasefire, and Nera's own explicit orders. These facts determine the final year's international and family politics.

#### Phase: Blackwing Keep, the muster
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"first-blackwing-campaign-muster","start":"24/06/740","end":"27/06/740","status":"pending"} -->
Present the keep on its real gray ridge outside the Thornhold relief road: armored parade grounds, regimental smiths, bow and mage drills, stables, siege galleries, infirmaries and the aerie holding Nera's **six assigned true-dragon knights**. The banner comprises **2,350 Thornwall infantry; 930 Ironthorn assault infantry; 620 Blacksteel armored horse; 220 March Beast Riders; 300 Steelwatch scouts/archers/mage-hunters; 310 warders, mages, sappers and fortress troops; 210 armed artillery/supply defenders; 30 Thorn Paladins; 24 Dreadlance Cataphracts; and six Skythorn dragon knights**. Exactly 5,000. Show commanders and working soldiers as adults with competence, morale, problems and independent attachments. Command is a relationship, not a glowing title.

#### Phase: The road to the front
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"first-blackwing-campaign-road-to-front","start":"28/06/740","end":"03/07/740","status":"pending"} -->
A heavy force cannot teleport from school or fortress to the pass. The column must ration feed, inspect armor, bridge artillery wagons, screen the flanks, repair roads, coordinate military intelligence and reserve ward cells. Scouts might report **Varkesh Redsteppe infantry, Frostmane lancers, Ashfur Bear Riders, Rimebolt handgonners, storm-wyverns or mountain bombards**, but a report is not perfect truth. Friendly forces include trained Imperial or Veyrath soldiers where authorized, **not** automatically every unit of Cassian's 75,000–90,000 warhost.

#### Phase: The first organized engagement
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"first-blackwing-campaign-first-engagement","start":"04/07/740","end":"08/07/740","status":"pending"} -->
Near a **Blackgorge approach road, contested bridge or defended forward depot**, Blackwing elements come under real opposing military pressure. If Nera chose to attend, **let him personally meet his officers, make orders, move through the battlefield, fight or hold back as he decides**. A strong mission has several simultaneous objectives: hold a route for relief wagons, recover scouts, prevent a flanking force from reaching civilians, protect dragon aerie logistics, and decide whether to press a retreating enemy. Combat should honestly reflect his monstrous personal force if he unleashes it. The opposing army may retreat from Nera and attack another position; the difficulty is not making a normal enemy sword stronger than Heaven Splitter.

#### Phase: The cost and the choices
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"first-blackwing-campaign-cost-and-choices","start":"09/07/740","end":"14/07/740","status":"pending"} -->
A battle can end with an orderly limited victory, costly exchange, defensive success, unfavorable withdrawal, negotiated truce or an enemy force slipping away. Casualties are not scripted by name, and Nera's soldiers do not automatically die just to create sadness. But repairs, funeral rites, prisoners, displaced villagers, damaged bridges, missing scouts, frightened militia and disrupted schedules remain real. A surrendering opponent is still a person and may possess useful testimony. A destroyed bridge cannot be restored merely by a celebratory speech.

#### Phase: The holiday remains Nera's
<!-- nera-phase: {"id":"first-blackwing-campaign-remaining-recess","start":"15/07/740","end":"30/09/740","status":"pending"} -->
After whatever involvement he chose, he may return to Crownspire, visit family, continue work at the keep, take a permitted leave with willing companions, seek medical rest, or pursue another project. If he chose not to go, show the **independent military operation through credible reports** and preserve the choice; do not substitute a narrator-controlled version of Nera on the battlefield. The year moves forward only after the player approves the chosen remaining recess.

### Y3-01 — Provincial Practicum: Four Windows on One Country
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqldw8git4y","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"08/10/740","end":"28/10/740","phases":[],"status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027251,"nanoseconds":255000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684331,"nanoseconds":718000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Playable field placement, assigned by application and capacity.

Students choose or receive offers for practicums that cover different aspects of the Empire. Potential venues include **Forgevault's repair and labor offices** (weapons, aether cells, injured workers); **Harrowmere's wagon gates** (food, feed and requisition); **Amberreach's Citadel of Measures** (canals, crop storage, civilian supply); **Greyford** (transport and debt courts); **Thornhold or Blackwing Keep** (command and rehabilitation where chartered permission exists); **Vaelcross clinics and courts**; or **Miravel** (technical assessment). Some classmates may take lawful foreign placements with paperwork; nobody can teleport to Saryuun and back after lunch.

The exercise asks for usable reports, not miraculous reform: where a shipment disappeared, why a bridge cannot support cataphracts, whether a ward failed because of weather or sabotage, which families lost harvest money, and how a hospital triages real injuries. Different placements discover genuinely different parts of the truth. Students develop professional competence away from Nera, and officials may reject a student's overconfident conclusions. **Nera decides which opportunity he pursues**, subject to actual approval.

### Y3-05 — The Greenveil Treaty: Elaranth's Price
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqlf3dpqp14","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"22/12/740","start":"12/12/740","phases":[],"status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027253,"nanoseconds":630000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684331,"nanoseconds":718000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Diplomatic window on the western frontier.

The Empire and **Elaranth** face renewal of ward-timber export rights, sacred-grove restrictions and cross-border freight arrangements. Western travel requires the known forest approaches; envoys from **Aelthalas** and the **Ravensong Canopy** bring interests distinct from those of merchants at **Moonwake** harbor. Forest wardwood cannot be mass-harvested without damaging protected places; Imperial fortress builders cannot simply switch it off without engineering alternatives. The negotiations ask who pays for defense, trade inspection and pollution, and what status applies to **Sylwem Aelthir**, the already enrolled Elaranth royal hostage.

Sylwem is not guaranteed liberation, romance, deportation or further captivity. Her actual actions, documents, family and commitments matter. A trade concession may be possible without changing her status; a humanitarian compromise may require a separate instrument. **Nera's connection to her ward pattern** is an opportunity for expert input, not omnipotent diplomatic influence. If western diplomacy succeeds, preserve its benefits during later eastern fighting rather than manufacturing an elven invasion to replace a missing villain.

### Y3-10 — Blackgorge: The Gate of Three Throats
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmjnu7r2yb","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"10/04/741","start":"24/03/741","phases":[]},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027258,"nanoseconds":377000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684331,"nanoseconds":718000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Final battlefield crisis **or** peaceful enforcement mission.

**Active-war version:** The eastern line focuses on the **actual Blackgorge fortress-city**, with its **Lower, Middle and Upper Throats**, glacial ravine, layered gates, high bridge, counter-bombard wards, signal towers and protected dragon platforms. An enemy army tries to isolate a forward roadhead, cut supply lifts or force the bridge; Imperial generals need time to bring ordinary **Crown Legions**, siege engines and artillery into connected positions. The Veyrath warhost deploys **Thornwall Shield Legions, Ironthorn Breakers, Blacksteel Lancers, March Beast Riders**, specialized **Thorn Paladins** and **Dreadlance Cataphracts** under the authority of their actual commanders. **Varkesh** counters with cold-weather infantry, bear cavalry, primitive handgonnes and storm-wyverns backed by limited ammunition and fodder. Terrain, food and artillery arcs govern what is possible.

**Nera's role is contingent on his choice and status.** His Blackwing Banner can guard a specified approach, screen evacuation, hold a reserve, exploit a breach or remain elsewhere according to legal orders and prior losses; he does not automatically command every Imperial formation. If he enters battle, his full Heaven Splitter is genuinely overwhelming where unleashed, but a mountain-cleaving attack also raises questions about terrain collapse, nearby allies, civilian structures and what the world learns of his abilities. Do not turn a Varkesh general into a physical equal without extraordinary independently established grounds. The opposing commander's strategy may instead seek time, hostages, mobility, retreat or diplomatic leverage.

**Peace version:** If the Three Bridges agreement or earlier successful action prevented a siege, the same Blackgorge location hosts inspections, reopening of lifts, dismantling of dangerous wards, prisoner releases, military withdrawal and a credible attempt by **specific remaining spoilers** to derail implementation. Do not resurrect a defeated army. Even a peaceful victory requires physical access, maps and trained technicians.

**Open play:** negotiated surrender, limited fighting, evacuation, breakthrough, imprisonment for actual offenses, earned compromise or survival of a bitter stalemate. Keep captured people and ruined property on the subsequent records.

### Y3-12 — The Ledger of the Missing and the Names Still Alive
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmjuh797rj","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"15/05/741","end":"28/05/741","phases":[],"status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027260,"nanoseconds":766000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684332,"nanoseconds":118000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Emotional and investigative resolution.

A cross-jurisdiction process collects the **real remaining** records of missing workers, false service transfers, former Chain Levies, captive fighters, ruined villages, the lost carriage and the South Causeway case. Some records may be in **Greyford transport books, Harrowmere depots, Vharos courts, Vaelcross creditor offices, a Solenne sanctuary** or a captured war-worksite; do not compress them all into a conveniently discovered master ledger. Investigators match names, dates, sealed orders and survivor testimony. Evidence of one broker's guilt should not convict every Varkesh trader or every Veyrath officer.

An identified survivor needs the chance to choose where to go next. Lost wages, custody of children, certification of personhood, burials and damaged homes are actual tasks. If the main trafficking network has already been broken, this becomes the **work of finishing freedom**, not rebuilding the network for a final villain reveal. If parts survive, an accountable court can identify known perpetrators and issue warrants. Neither a spectacular duel nor an emperor's speech reunites all families overnight.

### Y3-07 — The Black Concordance Evidence Crisis
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmk2bda7om","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"24/02/741","status":"pending","phases":[],"start":"10/02/741"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027255,"nanoseconds":999000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684332,"nanoseconds":118000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Central institutional antagonist, evidence and legal authority.

The **Black Concordance** is Cassian's concealed project using military ward relays and **coerced service oaths** under an emergency-control framework. It is not a free-floating spell that can seize all citizens whenever the plot wants. It needs defined **hardware, registered people, oath access, matching relay nodes, legal or forged emergency authority, operators and supply**. Earlier investigations may have reduced its capacity through confiscations or destroyed components. Its remaining network can be examined through credible physical places: **Forgevault** fabrication, **Thornhold** registry or command offices, remote pass relays and actual contracted transport.

The revelations should depend on what was proved in play. A procurement seal might trace to a middle officer rather than Cassian's direct command; a testimony might implicate a powerful official but be vulnerable to counterevidence. The former **Black Lantern** cannot be declared universally controlled by Cassian without proof. Some soldiers may believe relays would protect frontier hospitals; others see the cost to bound people. The danger becomes politically urgent as officials invoke war to bypass consent.

**Open play:** secure witness protection, inspect infrastructure, seek lawful injunctions, disrupt an installed relay, expose a buyer, oppose emergency laws or bargain for narrower safeguards. If Nera or another actor already disabled a piece, cross it permanently off the technical plan. An intact legal objection does not by itself destroy an armed system; destroying a node does not replace the need for justice.

### Y3-00 — The Returning Commander and the People Who Stayed
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmmkh73l7p","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"07/10/740","phases":[],"start":"01/10/740","status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027251,"nanoseconds":255000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684332,"nanoseconds":920000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Personal consequences and changes to the student world.

Year Three begins after the **actual** Year Two battlefield and recess decisions, not after a predetermined heroic victory. Students returning from family homes, clinics, laboratories, border assignments and vacations know different portions of the war. Rumors about Nera's abilities may spread **only** to the extent supported by real witnesses. A letter from his banner's marshal may be routine; a commemorative ceremony may honor those actually lost. Some students are impressed by practical leadership, others distrust hereditary armies, and friends care more that he has returned safely than what a court newspaper claims.

**Katarina, Aurelia, Irelia and Musashi** graduated at summer 740; they continue in diplomacy, medicine, sword travel or other roles consistent with choices made in play. **Magerrett, Changli and Senshi** are now Year Four, preparing for graduation in 741; **Taigar, Fiammetta and Sanhua** are Year Two. Crownspire changes with each cohort. If Nera is still carrying promises to **Liora, Vesper, Isolde, Celestine, Alaric, Lysandra, Sylwem, Lysia, Malfoid** or others, give them natural moments to address those promises rather than assuming all personal threads are resolved by a war chapter.

### Y3-11 — Emergency Powers: The Concordance Decision
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmof8v1xgg","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"11/04/741","phases":[],"end":"24/04/741","status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027258,"nanoseconds":377000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684332,"nanoseconds":118000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Highest political tension of the three-year arc.

As war or reconstruction strains Imperial institutions, competing officials argue whether the **Black Concordance** and related ward infrastructure may be activated, seized, dismantled, independently inspected or replaced. This is not automatically a coup. If Cassian is still powerful, he has leverage; if he has lost power, only actual surviving assets and associates remain. The Imperial court, ducal household, military command, temple advocates, civil plaintiffs and independent inspectors may have conflicting legal bases. Evidence from **Nine Hollows**, fraudulent wages, the creditor courts, Vharos or the Cinder Archive may be relevant, but historic guilt is not automatically identical to current control of the Concordance.

The climax should offer concrete competing proposals: limited emergency use with enforceable consent, judicial seizure of unlawful nodes, protection for registered subjects, restitution funds, surrender of responsible officers, or total dismantling with a plan for maintaining legitimate defenses. **Isolde's existing soul bond is not a node to switch off**, and the plan cannot use her as a magical control key by convenience. Nera's answer is wholly user-controlled. Powerful people can reject him and still be credible. Some relationships with Cassian or siblings may improve, fracture or simply remain unresolved after a legal outcome.

### Y3-04 — Soleris Convocation: A Person beneath an Oath
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmp5egzkaw","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"status":"pending","end":"05/12/740","start":"25/11/740"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027253,"nanoseconds":630000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684332,"nanoseconds":509000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Solenne religious and legal diplomacy.

A major convocation meets in **Soleris, the City of the Living Flame**, reached south from **Vaelcross through Vharos toward the Whitewatch–Sablewater crossing**. Church synods, the Solenne crown, hospitals, pilgrimage guardians and legal advocates dispute a narrow but consequential issue: whether governments may treat a magically bound person's compelled work as voluntary assent for taxation, debt, military service or testimony. The gathering can also address sanctuary for war refugees and the debts of healing houses.

**Isolde already possesses certified personhood and wages**; she does not need a second story arc pretending nobody ever recognized her as human. Her soul bond cannot be safely unmade or transferred by mortal hands, and no vote may casually cancel that world fact. The political issue is how a person **around the bond** controls residence, money, testimony and free choices. **Lysia's pyre in IY736 is completed backstory**, not a future episode; surviving clerical opponents or supporters may respond to her public authority. **Jeanne** and other sincere faithful may disagree with their superiors without becoming heretics on cue.

**Open play:** debates, healing obligations, contested rites, testimony, faith, hypocrisy, reconciliation, doctrinal limits or refusal to attend. A meaningful outcome should be a ruling, protection, concession, protest or published disagreement—not the narrator declaring Isolde romantically liberated by decree.

### Y3-03 — Provincial Petitions and the Black Orchard Witnesses
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmtc2j1m8r","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","phases":[],"start":"10/11/740","end":"22/11/740"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027253,"nanoseconds":630000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684332,"nanoseconds":509000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Courtroom tension and moral ambiguity.

A coordinated petition circuit hears claims from war-touched settlements, wagon guilds, soldiers' families, contract workers and displaced residents. Among the cases is the aftermath of **Black Orchard**, a small agricultural hamlet on a contested supply spur whose legal district must be identified from the actual campaign map. Witnesses describe a raid, civilian harm and missing workers. If their suffering was already averted by an effective intervention, adapt the event into a damage claim or inquiry into the attempt; do not stage a replacement village massacre. Some witnesses disagree in good faith over uniforms obscured by smoke, or confuse commissioned soldiers with armed contractors.

The court must examine chain of custody, duty to protect, actual command orders, compensation and whether witnesses can safely return home. One person's priority is burial rights, another wants a missing relative found, and another only wants surviving farmland restored. An **independent medic** may contradict the military report; don't declare an existing major character murdered for dramatic stakes. Evidence from Kestrel Hollow, the South Causeway and past investigations can support a pattern only if the signatures and routes really match.

**Open play:** testimony, investigative travel, appeal, settlement, protection, compensation, diplomatic protest or a failed case. A judge's written decision creates consequences even for officials who ignore it.

### Y3-09 — The Three Bridges Conference: Peace within Reach
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmu4eiu21z","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"start":"12/03/741","end":"20/03/741","status":"pending"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027258,"nanoseconds":377000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684332,"nanoseconds":920000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Major decisive diplomacy.

The **Three Bridges Conference** meets in a secured **Vaelcross river-bridge district** or another previously approved conference site, so its name fits the capital's three-river geography. The Aurelian court, Varkesh officials from **Kharzun**, Veyrath officers, civilian grain negotiators, and authorized neutral envoys debate the **Blackgorge–Urzak transit line**, prisoners, destroyed watch posts, inspections, the grain trade and remedies for civilians. **Orsenn** may offer convoy insurance; **Kharad** may discuss alternative mountain routes; **Solenne** may propose humanitarian monitoring. Those roles require invitations and independent national choices.

Bring credible treaty drafts, not abstract speeches: demilitarized bridge widths, a schedule for opening a pass, named prisoner-exchange supervisors, payment for damaged grain, restrictions on raids, independent inspection and real deadlines. Military commanders cannot promise resources they do not own. The conference can genuinely produce an enforceable ceasefire, a shaky narrow settlement, a temporary prisoner exchange or failure. Even a strong settlement cannot erase the lives already lost in the Year Two frontier campaign.

**War pathway:** If diplomacy fails or spoilers retain substantial forces, limited fighting can broaden. If it succeeds, do not invent an entirely new invasion to ensure a siege. The final military anchor below then becomes verification, withdrawal and spoiler containment, still important to the people who fought.

### Y3-06 — The Three-River Winter: Grain, Tax and the Capital
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmvdyo9kvx","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"24/01/741","phases":[],"start":"10/01/741"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027255,"nanoseconds":999000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684332,"nanoseconds":920000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Civil politics, not guaranteed rebellion.

The three rivers of **Vaelcross** make it both the richest political center and the terminus of many strained supply routes. **Amberreach's** measured grain is promised to the capital, to refugees and to men holding the eastern passes. Price rises or uneven distribution cause arguments in city assemblies. Some dock unions strike lawfully; some merchants hoard; some neighborhood councils organize competent kitchens; court factions call for stricter emergency powers. Recovered evidence about coercive contracts can complicate legislation even if the original criminal ring was dismantled.

Student leaders and recent graduates have realistic minor roles: writing petitions, staffing clinics, negotiating supply credit, serving as messengers or choosing another cause. The political tension is strongest when every faction has something tangible to lose. **A citywide uprising, plague or famine is not required**. A peaceful ration agreement, a limited riot, a failed tax proposal or a controversial relief appropriation are all usable outcomes.

### Y3-13 — Terms of Peace, Graves and New Defenses
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmxkd5b344","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"29/05/741","phases":[],"status":"pending","end":"08/06/741"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027260,"nanoseconds":766000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684333,"nanoseconds":326000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** World-story settlement.

A formal armistice or limited-war settlement addresses the **Blackgorge corridor, the Redsteppe approaches, Urzak Gate transit, prisoner exchange, refugee return, patrol limits, reconstruction grain, bond-labor abuses and credible war crimes**. If the fighting stopped earlier, this is implementation, not a new declaration of war. If it never expanded beyond small frontier engagements, the treaty stays appropriately limited. Varkesh merchants, Imperial court factions, neutral shippers and local civilians may disagree over the terms without being framed as collectively treacherous.

Count damaged roads and ward pylons, supply money and repairs. **Amberreach** can send grain; **Harrowmere** can organize protected wagons; **Forgevault** can produce fittings; **Thornhold** can provide engineers; none does everything for free. Hospitals in **Vaelcross, Soleris and the eastern marches** care for different people. The dead from Kestrel Hollow, attempted or actual student killings, Black Orchard and the frontier are not erased. Survivors can demand trials while diplomats bargain over amnesties, and the resulting peace can be valuable despite imperfection.

This is also an opportunity for personal consequences: students may rethink their careers; a foreign friend may have to return home; a sibling may reconsider command; family letters may at last contain words not expressible in public. **Do not close a relationship merely because the main conspiracy closes.**

### Y3-08 — The Veyrath Assembly: Five Banners, One Ducal Seat
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqmxqqxedub","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"08/03/741","start":"25/02/741","phases":[]},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027255,"nanoseconds":999000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684332,"nanoseconds":509000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** House politics and personal reckoning.

A formal assembly in **Thornhold's Upper Thorn** addresses command charter conflicts, war financing, inheritance, regency and the next generation's obligations. A death or abdication is **not** required to trigger this meeting; Cassian may still rule. In his continued presence the issue is succession designation and what banner rights may be exercised or constrained during emergency operations. If he has already fallen, adapt to a lawful inheritance or regency settlement without resurrecting him.

The existing five hereditary banners are material facts: **Lucien's First Thorn 6,000; Magerrett's Silverward 4,200; Nera's Blackwing 5,000; Taigar's Ironroot 3,000; and Elin's Greenwatch 1,800**—**20,000 counted within the existing wider warhost**, not a new 20,000-person reserve. **Elin is younger and her regent countersigns dangerous deployments**. Each banner already has elite troops. Cassian's central host and strategic authority remain separate. The heirs may disagree on troop requisitions, how refugees are treated, whether command orders may demand unlawful bonded labor, and whether family obligations override personal conscience.

**Magerrett's protection, Lucien's preparation, Taigar's independent ambitions, Elin's future, Cassian's ruthless methods and Nera's declared desire to build something else** are all meaningful. This is where the childhood family arc becomes political without reducing every sibling to an enemy. A settlement can clarify legal rights, succession, command safeguards or continued dispute. Nera's eligibility is not the same as automatically becoming duke.

### Y3-15 — The Quiet Return: A Free Summer and an Unwritten Fourth Year
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqn82abuqff","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"status":"pending","start":"29/06/741","end":"30/09/741"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027263,"nanoseconds":133000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684333,"nanoseconds":728000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Epilogue to the main arc; playable recess.

The principal dark-fantasy conflict has reached whichever durable settlement the characters actually earned: open or constrained border, surviving or dismantled Concordance, named legal responsibilities, surviving relationships and an honest accounting of the disappeared. **Nera chooses his summer.** He might visit **Blackwing Keep** without a crisis, spend time with his household, sail to Tideglass, return to Miravel, arrange separate meetings with friends, revisit an eastern village he affected, rest near Crownspire or pursue a new ambition. The people he cares about choose their own movements, salaries, assignments and companionship.

Close this document with a **state checkpoint**, not a cliffhanger demanding a new war. Record the peace terms or ongoing ceasefire, Blackwing's true remaining strength, offices and banners still held, verified Concordance components, legal reforms, released prisoners, missing names, medical aftermath, each significant relationship's actual state, and the next known Academy calendar date. These become **inputs to a later Year Four plan**. Do not narrate the whole recess or advance automatically to autumn 741.

### Year One Direction — The School Opens onto the Empire
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqnfqg1kv84","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"30/09/739","phases":[],"start":"01/09/738"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027222,"nanoseconds":16000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684333,"nanoseconds":728000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Primary feel:** 55–65% Academy friendships, practical learning, rivalries, social trips and relationships; 35–45% investigation, politics, horror and family responsibilities. Scheduled classes, ordinary pleasure and recovery continue between consequential scenes. Keep a few school days or weeks between connected developments.

**First major personal arc:** Starlia's rescue opens Nera's path toward Katarina's private society. The Empire's slavery, corrupt courts and aristocratic entitlement are accepted parts of ordinary life. The Emperor and Prince Cedric knowingly protect and benefit from that structure. Katarina's inner circle goes further through deliberate psychological destruction, abusive medicine, private sacrifice and coordinated atrocities. The same people can be admired patrons and affectionate peers.

**Source priority:** The character lorebooks rank Nera, Isolde, Vesper, Liora, Cedric, Sylwem, Lysia, Lysandra, Malfoid and Fleurdelys as Core; Starlia's later card also makes her Core. Katarina, Celestine, Alaric and Magerrett are Major. Connect this act through those characters' actual roles and motives, with selective scenes and independent choices. Importance controls narrative attention and does not confer knowledge, office, power or automatic involvement.

The situations bring the darkness close to familiar people. Public exposure may harm a patron, protect a captive or fracture a family while the wider order continues to function. Particular successes remain real.

### Year Three Direction — The War Outside and the Law at Home
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqnjrcdcxai","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"end":"30/09/741","status":"pending","phases":[],"start":"01/10/740"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027248,"nanoseconds":850000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684333,"nanoseconds":728000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Primary feel:** The major plot reaches resolution during Year Three, not Year Four. Military consequences move into households, courts, embassies and the family command structure. Academy life still exists: classes, practices, personal celebrations, friendship, chosen intimacy and ordinary study provide contrast. Nera is **still an enrolled third-year**, even if he has spent a recess with a real army. Academic institutions can assess and discipline him under their own charter; he does not gain sovereign immunity or automatic political authority through battlefield fame.

### Y3-02 — Harvest of Captives: A War Eats Its Own Roads
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqnxw49fzjm","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","end":"28/10/740","phases":[],"start":"15/10/740"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027251,"nanoseconds":255000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684333,"nanoseconds":326000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Human and logistical consequences.

In the canal country between **Amberreach and Harrowmere**, requisition offices compete for winter grain, while private contractors promise debt relief to families whose fields have already been seized. Some agreements are voluntary under severe pressure; others conceal transfers into **Imperial Chain Levy** construction camps, guarded worksites or illegal captive labor. Meanwhile, a legitimate Blackwing quartermaster may refuse to accept an unverified supply invoice because it risks feeding an enemy agent. These choices have costs on both sides.

Different workstations reveal different facts: hospital amputations, fraudulent grain weights, unusually cheap armor components, missing workers from Greyford and unregistered convoy passengers. A recovered child and a liberated laborer need homes, not just a public victory speech. Do not assume Nera's own Blackwing soldiers are Chain Levies: the two institutions are separate, with different authorities. If Year Two reforms already blocked an abusive contract route, represent improved circumstances rather than silently restoring the same crime.

### Y3-14 — Crownspire Grand Command Melee and Third-Year Review
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqnz7p1ouf4","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"status":"pending","phases":[],"end":"28/06/741","start":"15/06/741"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027260,"nanoseconds":766000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684333,"nanoseconds":326000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Type:** Academy climax, not graduation.

After a year shaped by real events, Crownspire hosts a major **third-year interdisciplinary challenge** with legal, warded combat and civic-command tasks. Call it the **Grand Command Melee**, not the guaranteed final graduation exam: Nera and his classmates are still completing Year Three of a four-year program. Teams must solve objectives under formal scoring, competing commands, contested relief routes and changing information. Some students excel at dueling, others at preservation, engineering, healing or negotiation. A team led by a clever low-ranked classmate can outperform a stronger team on goals that do not require maximum damage. Honor what the players actually learned in the last three years.

The event permits callbacks to **Alaric's moral standards, Sylwem's protected authorship, Rhea's leadership, Liora's discipline, Vesper's deliberate discretion, Isolde's independent work and the class's older friendly rivalries**. Do not force a named person to be Nera's opponent or romantic partner. Prizes, sanctions, mentor recommendations and practical Year Four options follow performance.

At the end, faculty and classmates recognize **the completion of the three-year main story**, but **no early certificate, final class rank or obligatory Grand Melee victory is declared**. The future fourth academic year remains open.

### Year Four Reserved — No Prescribed Plot
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqo1byva8bs","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"phases":[],"status":"pending","start":"29/06/741","end":"30/09/741"},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027263,"nanoseconds":133000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684334,"nanoseconds":129000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**No prescribed Year Four plot, war, graduation tournament, final antagonist, political outcome, romance decision, career, inheritance settlement or forced travel.** Crownspire's established fourth year still exists; its final graduation remains part of the school's ordinary four-year chronology, but **this file intentionally does not plan or stage it**. A separate Year Four design can later use the full Year Three state checkpoint without undoing the earned results. Do not import the previous outline's old Year Four crisis or endgame automatically.

### Year Two Direction — From Student Politics to Real Orders
<!-- nera-entry: {"id":"lore_mvjpvwqoboyf548v","draft":false,"createdFrom":"import","schedule":{"start":"01/10/739","status":"pending","end":"30/09/740","phases":[]},"createdAt":{"seconds":1792027236,"nanoseconds":791000000},"updatedAt":{"seconds":1792684334,"nanoseconds":129000000}} -->

<!-- nera-section: {"origin":"import","kind":"background","cutoff":null} -->
**Primary feel:** Academy remains the everyday home, but money, law, foreign allegiance and the eastern border now affect people the students personally know. Compared with Year One, events are tighter and more cumulative. Nera has already demonstrated talent; the challenge is deciding what kind of responsibility, public reputation and command practice he wants. A real **Blackwing campaign in the late-year recess** is the central new set piece. Political outcomes and his participation remain open.
